# 0.8.0 Release Decision Log

Append-only record of 0.8.0 scope changes, waivers, API breaks, benchmark-methodology changes, and claim
changes (worklist **G0.2**). The rule: **no release decision lives only in a PR thread or a local note** —
if it changes scope, breaks an API, waives a P1, or changes a published claim or a benchmark method, it gets
an entry here.

Each entry records: **date · decision · alternatives · evidence · owner · reviewer · expiration · worklist
ID**. Entries are added, never edited in place (correct a decision with a new superseding entry). A decision
proposed in an open PR is recorded with **reviewer: pending**; it becomes **accepted** when that PR is
reviewed and merged into `release/0.8.0`.

Owner is the release owner (grant.boquet@gmail.com) unless stated. Dates are UTC.

---

## D-0001 · Drop Python 3.10; floor numpy ≥ 2.4, scipy ≥ 1.16

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** Raise `requires-python` to `>=3.11` and the runtime floors to `numpy>=2.4`, `scipy>=1.16`,
  `mpmath>=1.3`; drop the 3.10 classifier and set `ruff`/`mypy` targets to py311.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep 3.10 + older floors (rejected: scipy<1.13 `von_mises` precision, numpy
  2.0.0 `IndexError` bugs, numpy<2.4 t-SNE/atlas fragility, and tests using numpy-2.0-only `np.trapezoid`
  all failed the min-version job); pin exact versions (rejected: floors, not pins, keep newer majors usable).
- **Evidence:** min-versions CI job bisecting the true floor; 4252 tests pass at the new floors.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** revisit at 0.9.0 (numpy/scipy floors move forward as the ecosystem does).
- **Worklist:** S13.4

## D-0002 · pip-audit is a blocking gate with an explicit waiver file

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** Make the base-install `pip-audit` CI gate blocking (remove `continue-on-error`); un-waived
  vulnerabilities fail the build. Waivers live in `.github/pip-audit-ignore.txt`, one advisory ID per line
  with a reason — this is the P1-waiver mechanism referenced by this log.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep pip-audit advisory (rejected: a security release cannot advise-only);
  pin the whole graph (rejected: floors + waivers keep it maintainable).
- **Evidence:** the gate fails on an un-waived vuln; the waiver file suppresses a specific advisory.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0083
- **Expiration:** each waiver entry should carry its own expiry/re-review date when added.
- **Worklist:** S13.1

## D-0003 · optimize / fit / best_of are quiet by default (console-output behavior change)

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** Change the default of `out` from `sys.stdout` to `None` on `optimize`, `fit`, and `best_of`.
  Per-iteration progress is now opt-in via `out=`. Fitted models and every numeric result are unchanged.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep printing by default (rejected: a library must not spray stdout in normal
  use); a global verbosity flag (rejected: `out=` already exists and is per-call explicit).
- **Evidence:** no test asserted on the old default stdout; the full non-optional suite is unchanged.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (stable behavior going forward).
- **Worklist:** Q5.4

## D-0004 · Deprecation policy: warn, keep ≥ 2 minor releases, then remove

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** A deprecated stable name emits a `DeprecationWarning` (one message format), keeps working and
  forwarding unchanged, and is removed no earlier than two minor releases after the announcing release
  (announced in 0.8.0 → removable no earlier than 0.10.0). Removal ships a migration note/example.
- **Alternatives considered:** remove-on-next-minor (rejected: too aggressive for stable names); never remove
  (rejected: unbounded compatibility debt).
- **Evidence:** the mechanism + policy doc; a static test that every "Deprecated alias" callable warns.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (standing policy).
- **Worklist:** A1.5

## D-0005 · Unclassified public surfaces default to `provisional` maturity

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** The machine-readable maturity registry maps only the surfaces the maturity doc classifies;
  every other public surface resolves to `provisional` (no stability promise until explicitly recorded
  stable). Only `mixle.experimental` is `experimental`.
- **Alternatives considered:** classify all 34 top-level surfaces up front (rejected: would invent tiers the
  maturity doc does not state); default to `stable` (rejected: over-promises).
- **Evidence:** registry ↔ maturity-doc sync test; the manifest reports 2 stable / 1 experimental / 32
  provisional.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (default policy; individual surfaces get explicit tiers as they are classified).
- **Worklist:** A1.2, A1.6

## D-0006 · Coverage gate is a conservative catastrophic-regression floor (45%), not a target

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** Add `--cov-fail-under=45` to the base-install `full` CI job. This is a floor set well below
  the real number (the fast suite alone covers ~72% locally with backends installed) so it trips only on a
  large regression (e.g. a dropped test directory), not on ordinary changes. Raise it once the optional
  extras job also reports coverage and the two are combined.
- **Alternatives considered:** no coverage gate (rejected: no regression tripwire); a threshold near the true
  number (rejected: base install skips optional backends, so it would misrepresent and block unrelated PRs).
- **Evidence:** measured fast-suite coverage 72% with `branch=true`; base-only is lower but well above 45%.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** revisit when the combined (base + optional) coverage number exists.
- **Worklist:** T3.4

## D-0007 · Introduce ruff BLE (blind-except) via a full-tree noqa ratchet

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** Select ruff `BLE001` and annotate the 200 existing blind-`except` sites (across 126 files)
  with `# noqa: BLE001` via `ruff --add-noqa`, so no *new* blind except can be added while the existing debt
  is explicit and shrinkable. Many existing sites are legitimately broad (report paths that must never break
  a fit, worker errors surfaced to a driver).
- **Alternatives considered:** rewrite all 200 sites now (rejected: large, per-site judgment, risk of
  behavior change); per-file-ignores for 126 files (rejected: hides new violations in grandfathered files).
- **Evidence:** ruff clean tree-wide; a new blind except is flagged (negative control).
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** the noqa count should trend down; no hard expiry.
- **Worklist:** Y4.3

## D-0008 · mypy is blocking for a typed-core, advisory for the rest

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** Make mypy blocking for a curated typed-core (`mixle.scorecard`, `mixle.spend`,
  `mixle.system`, `mixle.registry`) that is clean under `disallow_untyped_defs`; the whole-tree mypy stays
  advisory. The typed-core list grows module-by-module.
- **Alternatives considered:** make whole-tree mypy blocking now (rejected: the tree is only partially
  typed); keep mypy fully advisory (rejected: type regressions never fail CI).
- **Evidence:** the four modules pass strict mypy; an untyped def added to one fails the gate.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (list expands over time).
- **Worklist:** Y4.1

## D-0009 · Deploy pure models as safe JSON; keep pickle only for torch-backed models

- **Date:** 2026-07-11
- **Decision:** `lifecycle.Model.deploy` writes a registry-serializable model as type-tagged JSON; only a
  model the registry cannot represent falls back to pickle, recorded in the manifest `format`. `Model.load`
  reads the format and uses the safe path for JSON. The remaining pickle path is documented as executing
  arbitrary code (trusted-source only). This is a claim/security-behavior change: "durable artifact" no
  longer implies an unsafe pickle load for the common case.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep pickle everywhere with only a docstring warning (rejected: pickle-RCE
  stays the default for pure models); drop pickle entirely (rejected: torch-backed models need it).
- **Evidence:** a pure model deploys with no `model.pkl`; old pickle artifacts still load (backward compat).
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** S13 (security), N9.2 (honest claims)

## D-0010 · `mpmath` narrows to an optional extra; D-0001's floor bump did not justify a base dependency

- **Date:** 2026-07-17
- **Decision:** Move `mpmath` out of base `dependencies` into a new `highprec` extra (`mpmath>=1.3`, floor
  unchanged). `mixle.engines.highprec` is mpmath's only non-test consumer, and it already treated mpmath as
  optional (`try: import gmpy2 / except: try: import mpmath / except: _BACKEND=None`, preferring the
  correctly-gated `gmpy2` extra); forcing mpmath into every install made that `_BACKEND=None` degrade path
  practically unreachable and contradicted the base-install optional-dependency convention this project
  otherwise enforces (worklist P2.2).
- **Relationship to D-0001:** D-0001 (2026-07-11) raised the runtime floor to `numpy>=2.4, scipy>=1.16,
  mpmath>=1.3` as one bundled decision. Its own "Alternatives considered"/"Evidence" sections cite only
  numpy/scipy-specific bugs (von Mises precision, IndexError, t-SNE/atlas fragility); mpmath is never
  mentioned again after the initial line, and the `dependencies` array's comment block in `pyproject.toml`
  never stated a rationale for mpmath specifically. D-0001's floor (`>=1.3`) is unchanged and still correct;
  this entry narrows only the base-vs-optional placement, which D-0001 never analyzed on its own merits.
- **Alternatives considered:** leave mpmath in base deps (rejected: contradicts P2.2, makes
  `_BACKEND=None` unreachable in any real install, no evidence anywhere it was a deliberate choice on its
  own merits); fold mpmath into the existing `gmpy2` extra (rejected: `gmpy2`'s own primary consumer is
  unrelated -- `mixle/enumeration/quantization/core.py`'s structural count-DP -- and the two are
  alternative backends for the same feature, not a bundled pair like `torch`+`safetensors`; a dedicated
  `highprec` extra names the feature it unlocks, matching `arrays`/`grammar`/`data`).
- **Evidence:** clean wheel install (neither gmpy2 nor mpmath) -- `highprec._BACKEND is None`,
  `available() is False`, `_require()` raises `RuntimeError` (not `ImportError`), and the 768-module public
  import sweep (`scripts/import_sweep.py`, worklist P2.1) passes clean. Wheel install with the new
  `highprec` extra (mpmath present, gmpy2 absent) -- `_BACKEND == "mpmath"`, correct `hp_sum`/`hp_array`
  results. Targeted run of every file this change touches: 38 passed, 18 subtests passed, 0 failed.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (corrects a placement error; no scheduled revisit).
- **Worklist:** P2.2

## D-0011 · Historical benchmark artifacts are not 0.8.0 claims

- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Decision:** Move result documents generated by Mixle 0.5.2 to `benchmarks/archive/pre-0.8/`, remove their
  numerical speed/GPU claims from current documentation, and require all new current-line result documents to
  carry Mixle version, minor, and commit provenance. A hardware run is not a 0.8.0 blocker because the release
  makes no numerical CPU/GPU performance claim without it.
- **Alternatives considered:** relabel old numbers as 0.8.0 (rejected: false evidence); run a long local panel
  under an arbitrary laptop environment (rejected: violates the designated-hardware and exact-candidate rules).
- **Evidence:** benchmark provenance tests; archived source documents; bounded benchmark and backend wording.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** publish new numbers only after a retained exact-candidate run.
- **Worklist:** B7.3, B7.6, D8.6

## D-0012 · Release manifests live together and experiments stay out of the release tree

- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Decision:** Consolidate API, maturity, ownership, and serialization manifests under `manifests/`; remove
  the tracked `experiments/group_attention` scratch area and ignore `/experiments/`. Generated manifests remain
  in the sdist for audit but are not runtime wheel data. The regenerated maturity inventory now contains 43
  surfaces: 3 stable, 1 experimental, and 39 provisional; this updates D-0005's historical evidence count
  without changing its default-to-provisional policy.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep root-level JSON and tracked experiment output (rejected: obscures the
  release boundary and repeatedly pollutes the repository root).
- **Evidence:** 57-test release-quality batch, manifest generators/drift gates, sdist content inspection.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** A1.1, A1.2, A1.6, S13.3

## D-0013 · The runtime wheel excludes the test suite

- **Date:** 2026-07-21
- **Decision:** Keep tests in the source repository but exclude `mixle.tests*` from release artifacts. Declare
  runtime package data explicitly so Cython sources and the quantitative-semantics fixture continue to ship.
  The rehearsal wheel fell from 1,727 to 785 files while its 774-module import sweep stayed clean. The sdist
  retains the changelog and machine-readable release manifests, not a partial test tree whose documentation
  and repository-level fixtures are absent.
- **Alternatives considered:** continue shipping the 400k-line test tree (rejected: unnecessary artifact size
  and public import surface); broadly disable all package data without explicit declarations (rejected: would
  drop required runtime/build inputs).
- **Evidence:** package-artifact policy tests, wheel/sdist inspection, installed-wheel import sweep.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** P2.1, P2.5, S13.3

## D-0014 · Consolidate system/spend/meta/registry/fault/scorecard into a mixle/system/ package

- **Date:** 2026-07-23
- **Decision:** Move the six cross-importing flat modules `mixle.system`, `mixle.spend`, `mixle.meta`,
  `mixle.registry`, `mixle.fault`, `mixle.scorecard` into `mixle/system/{core,spend,meta,registry,fault,
  scorecard}.py`, with `mixle/system/__init__.py` re-exporting all sixteen public names lazily (PEP 562
  `__getattr__`, mirroring `mixle/__init__.py` itself) and the five old flat names kept as
  `DeprecationWarning`-on-access shims (the existing `mixle.program` pattern). This adds a new
  `mixle.system` entry to `manifests/api_manifest.json` with all sixteen names, tagged `provisional` —
  triggering the freeze gate (`mixle/tests/public_api_manifest_test.py`) because that manifest is built by
  walking `__init__.py` files only, so a flat module (the old `mixle/system.py`) was never scanned by it at
  all. None of these sixteen names are new: all were already fully public pre-move (`mixle.spend.Spend`,
  `mixle.scorecard.SystemScorecard`, etc.); becoming a package is what first brings them into this
  particular gate's view.
- **Alternatives considered:** leave the six modules flat (rejected: the six already cross-import each
  other — `system.py`→{fault, spend}, `meta.py`→{scorecard, system}, `scorecard.py`→{system} — which is
  exactly the "these belong in one package" signal, and `module_ownership.json` already flagged four of
  the six `migrate` with three different destination projects, i.e. governance already saw this as one
  cohesive concern splitting off, not six independent ones); rename instead of alias (rejected: breaks
  every existing caller immediately, contradicts the project's standing deprecate-then-remove policy,
  D-0004); an eager `mixle/system/__init__.py` importing all six submodules at module scope (tried first,
  reverted: it forced any import of the leaf modules, including `mixle.system.fault` — previously a
  zero-dependency leaf used by `mixle.doe.oracle` — to also load `mixle.system.core`, which reaches
  `mixle.task`, which circles back through `mixle.task.propose` to `mixle.doe.oracle` itself; caught by
  `mixle/tests/doe_task_models_clean_import_test.py` and `epistemic_clean_import_test.py`, fixed by making
  the aggregator lazy instead).
- **Evidence:** all 5 shims verified to resolve to the new location and warn exactly once; `ruff
  check`/`format --check` clean; `lint-imports` — 3 kept, 0 broken; the 7 directly-touched test files (68
  tests) plus the full suite (5838 passed, 8 failed before the lazy-`__init__` fix, 0 failed after,
  excluding pre-existing environment-gated skips) pass; the "mypy (typed core, blocking)" CI step and its
  `pyproject.toml` `[[tool.mypy.overrides]]` list updated to the new file/module locations (`mixle.system`,
  `.core`, `.spend`, `.registry`, `.scorecard`, plus the still-flat `mixle.scorecard`/`.spend`/`.registry`
  shims) and reverified clean under `--follow-imports=skip` — the old CI command referenced
  `mixle/system.py`, a path this change removes, which would otherwise have broken that blocking step
  outright; `manifests/module_ownership.json` and `manifests/maturity_manifest.json` need no change (both
  key by top-level import name via `pkgutil.iter_modules`, which all six names still satisfy unchanged, the
  same reasoning already established for the pre-existing `mixle.program` shim).
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (the five flat-name shims fall under D-0004's standing removal policy).
- **Worklist:** A1.1 (api manifest gate), Y4.1 (typed core)

## D-0015 · Generate a best-effort SBOM from the existing pip-audit invocation, non-blocking

- **Date:** 2026-07-23
- **Decision:** Add a "Generate SBOM (CycloneDX JSON)" step to `.github/workflows/security.yml`'s
  `pip-audit` job, immediately after the existing vulnerability audit, reusing the same already-installed
  `pip-audit` (no new tool or dependency) with `--format cyclonedx-json -o sbom.json`, then upload it as a
  build artifact. Both the generation step (`continue-on-error: true`) and the upload
  (`if-no-files-found: warn`) are non-blocking — SBOM generation failing must not fail the vulnerability
  gate this job exists to enforce.
- **Alternatives considered:** a dedicated SBOM tool (`cyclonedx-bom`/`syft`) (rejected: unverifiable in
  this preparation environment — no network access to confirm the package installs or its CLI syntax
  locally — versus `pip-audit --format cyclonedx-json`, which is a documented flag on a tool already
  installed, already CI-verified, in daily use in this exact job); making the new step blocking (rejected:
  cannot be fully exercised end-to-end in this environment before landing — see Evidence — so it should
  prove itself non-blocking in real CI first, matching D-0006's "conservative floor, not a target"
  precedent, before any future decision promotes it to blocking); a separate new workflow (rejected:
  duplicates the dependency-installation step this job already pays for).
- **Evidence:** `pip-audit --format cyclonedx-json` confirmed as a real, documented CLI flag
  (`pip-audit --help`); a local dry run produces well-formed CycloneDX JSON (`bomFormat: CycloneDX`,
  `specVersion: 1.4`) enumerating this environment's 314 installed packages, though `--dry-run`
  specifically skips populating the component list (that requires the real audit path, which needs
  network access this local preparation environment does not have — confirmed by a direct query to the
  vulnerability API not completing). The real (non-dry-run) form is exactly what the existing "Audit
  resolved dependencies" step in this same job already runs successfully in CI, so the component
  population itself is not new/unverified — only the `--format`/`-o` combination for THIS step is not yet
  exercised end-to-end outside this local environment. Full confirmation is deferred to the next real run
  of this workflow.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0141
- **Expiration:** revisit once a real CI run confirms the artifact populates correctly; promote to blocking
  only after that.
- **Worklist:** S13 (security)

## D-0016 · Narrow the automatic-modeling docs instead of wiring propose() into optimize()'s structure search

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** `propose()`'s candidates (the heuristic recommendation, the independence baseline, and an
  optional LLM design) each already build a concrete estimator before fitting, so none of them reach
  `optimize()`/`fit()`'s own no-estimator (`estimator=None`, `structure="auto"`) auto-structure-search
  path — the only route to a copula or learned-Bayesian-network dependence upgrade. `propose()`'s only
  dependence-capturing candidate is a joint multivariate-Gaussian estimator, and only for fixed-length,
  fully-observed, purely-numeric vector rows (never tuples) — narrower than what `optimize(data)` reaches
  directly. `docs/automatic-modeling-contract.rst`'s "Dependence between fields" section named only
  `optimize(data)`, correctly, but sat immediately above a "Selection and validation" section describing
  `get_estimator(data)`/`propose(data)` with nothing distinguishing their (much narrower) reach — an easy
  misread, and `propose()`'s own docstring called its heuristic recommendation "dependency-aware" with no
  such qualification. Reworded both to state the boundary explicitly; no code or search-budget behavior
  changed. `mixle/tests/lifecycle_test.py::test_propose_frontier_does_not_reach_the_copula_dependence_upgrade`
  pins the current asymmetry directly against `mixle/tests/automatic_copula_structure_test.py`'s own
  dependent-heterogeneous fixture, so a future change on either side of the gap has to touch this test
  deliberately rather than let the docs claim silently drift.
- **Alternatives considered:** wire the structure search into `propose()`'s candidate list (rejected for
  *this* pass, not rejected outright — real, unresolved design cost: `_maybe_structured_model` does its
  own internal multi-candidate fitting, so `propose()`'s `max_candidates`/`timeout` budget accounting
  would double-count or need reconciling; both `propose()`'s own candidate construction and
  `_maybe_structured_model` independently call `get_estimator(rows)` and fit a composite, duplicating
  work; `optimize()` called with `estimator=None` returns a fitted distribution directly, not a fit-ready
  `ParameterEstimator` the way `propose()`'s other candidates work, so the frontier's own bookkeeping
  shape doesn't fit without a real design decision; `Model.fit()`'s later re-fit-on-full-data call
  (`optimize(fit_data, self.spec, ...)`) always passes a concrete `self.spec`, so it would need its own
  special-casing too. None of this is mechanical — matches D-0011's precedent of narrowing a claim rather
  than forcing the larger, not-yet-designed work into a release-prep pass under time pressure); leaving
  the docs as they were (rejected: `docs/automatic-modeling-contract.rst` is the evidence cited for
  section 5's "Public automatic-modeling contract: COMPLETE" gate, so an imprecise contract page leaves
  that COMPLETE status overstated).
- **Evidence:** direct reproduction — `optimize(data, out=None)` on strongly-dependent (r=0.85),
  heterogeneous-marginal (Gamma/Normal) tuple data returns a `CopulaDistribution`; `mixle.propose(data,
  fit=True)` on the identical data returns a frontier whose only estimator type is `CompositeEstimator`.
  New regression test passes; full sweep across `lifecycle_test.py`, `propose_budget_test.py`,
  `automatic_copula_structure_test.py`, `task_recommend_test.py`, `automatic_test.py`,
  `automatic_datumnode_edgecases_test.py`, `automatic_modality_routing_test.py`, and
  `signature_docstring_sync_test.py` (80 tests) passes; `sphinx -b dummy` build clean (also fixed a
  genuinely broken `:doc:` cross-reference from the docstring — relative doc-role resolution from a
  Python docstring needs a leading `/` to resolve against the source root, not the docstring's own
  virtual document path; confirmed via the dummy build's `ref.doc` warning before and its absence after).
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0017
- **Expiration:** none for the docs narrowing itself; revisit if/when a future release takes on the larger
  wiring work, per the design costs listed above.
- **Worklist:** I6 (automatic modeling), X12 (documentation, claims)

## D-0017 · Supersede D-0016: wire the structure search into propose() after all

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** Reverses D-0016. `propose()`'s candidate list now includes `("structured", None)` --
  `optimize()` called with no pre-built estimator, i.e. its own no-estimator auto-structure-search, the
  route to a copula/learned-Bayesian-network dependence upgrade. Every design cost D-0016 raised turned
  out to already be solved by existing, first-class plumbing once actually checked rather than assessed
  from a read of the code: `propose()`'s scoring loop already calls `optimize(train, est, ...)` per
  candidate, so `est=None` reaches the auto-structure-search path with zero special-casing; `Model.spec`
  already defaults to `None` and is documented as "the estimator is inferred at fit time"; `Model.fit()`'s
  `optimize(fit_data, self.spec, ...)` already re-runs structure search correctly on a later re-fit
  (verified directly: `Model(None).fit(data)` and a second `.fit()` on more data both worked, first try);
  each candidate participates in `max_candidates`/`timeout` as one slot regardless of how many structures
  it tries internally, exactly like every other candidate, because the loop already iterates
  `(name, estimator)` pairs one at a time. None of D-0016's four cited costs were real blockers once
  tested against the actual code rather than reasoned about from its shape.
- **Why reconsidered:** an independent review scored the gap D-0016 left open as the most serious release
  blocker, not a documentation nit, with a quantified, reproducible cost: on `examples/quickstart_example.py`'s
  own dependent dataset (`category` driving both `amount`'s mean and `flag`'s rate — written specifically
  to demonstrate `propose()`'s dependency detection), `propose()` returned a `CompositeDistribution`
  scoring roughly 1.17 nats/row worse on held-out data than the `HeterogeneousBayesianNetwork`
  `optimize()` finds on the identical data. That example's own accompanying comment already asserted
  propose()'s "dependency detection... should notice" this relationship -- D-0016's docs fix left the
  code silently contradicting comments already shipped in this exact release-prep tree. Re-examining the
  implementation cost under that severity, rather than the abstract "is this in scope" framing D-0016
  used, found the fix was a few lines, not a redesign.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep D-0016's narrowed docs and fix only the quickstart example's comment
  (rejected: papers over the underlying capability gap the review correctly flagged as the actual
  problem, and the README's existing "notice when fields depend on each other... fit a copula or vine
  when continuous columns are correlated" claim -- unchanged since before D-0016, never itself edited --
  would have stayed false for `propose()` specifically); gate the new candidate on row type (tuple-only,
  matching where the BN/copula upgrade actually applies) (rejected as unnecessary: `optimize(train, None,
  ...)` is unconditionally safe on non-tuple numeric-vector rows too, verified directly -- it falls
  through to a plain fit rather than erroring, and the existing per-candidate `try/except` in the loop
  is a safety net regardless).
- **Evidence:** direct reproduction confirmed the finding (`HeterogeneousBayesianNetwork` at held-out
  -1.4263 vs `CompositeDistribution` at -2.2714 on the quickstart's own generator, matching the
  independent review's -1.4263 for the structured model exactly); `propose()` end-to-end now returns the
  `HeterogeneousBayesianNetwork` as the winner on that data; `examples/quickstart_example.py` re-run
  output now matches its own pre-existing narrating comment. `docs/automatic-modeling-contract.rst`,
  `mixle/lifecycle.py`'s `propose()` docstring, `docs/lifecycle.rst`, and `docs/package-map.rst` updated
  to describe the restored capability; `mixle/tests/lifecycle_test.py`'s D-0016-era test (asserting the
  *absence* of the upgrade) rewritten to assert its presence and that it can win on held-out merit.
  Also closed a related, directly-adjacent gap the same review raised: `propose(max_candidates=0)` and
  `propose(timeout<0)`/`holdout` outside `(0, 1)` previously failed open, returning an unfit, unscored
  estimator as if it had won a verified comparison -- now validated at entry (negative
  `max_candidates`/`timeout` and any `holdout` outside `(0, 1)` raise `ValueError`), while deliberately
  preserving `max_candidates=0`/`timeout=0.0` as the pre-existing, already-tested "skip verification,
  return the fast heuristic" escape hatch (`propose_budget_test.py`'s
  `test_zero_timeout_skips_and_falls_back_to_recommendation`), since every skipped candidate is already
  recorded honestly in `notes`/`frontier` rather than silently dropped. Full propose()/lifecycle/automatic
  test sweep (55 tests across `lifecycle_test.py`, `propose_budget_test.py`,
  `automatic_copula_structure_test.py`, `automatic_modality_routing_test.py`) passes.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** I6 (automatic modeling), X12 (documentation, claims)

## D-0018 · Repack the sdist deterministically; SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH alone did not make it reproducible

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** Add `scripts/normalize_sdist.py`, run in the publish workflow immediately after
  `python -m build`, which rewrites every tar member's `mtime`/`uid`/`gid`/`uname`/`gname` to a fixed
  value and recompresses with the system `gzip -n` binary (not Python's `gzip` module). C639cbcc pinned
  `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` and verified the *wheel* reproduces byte-for-byte; an independent packaging
  rehearsal found the *sdist* still did not, despite the same environment variable being set for both.
  Root-caused directly, in two layers: (1) setuptools' `sdist` command writes each tar member's `mtime`
  from the real filesystem mtime of the source file, not from `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` -- confirmed by
  diffing two builds' raw (decompressed) `.tar` content, which differed at 218 lines, every one a
  `tar -tv` timestamp; (2) once every member mtime/uid/gid was pinned and the two `.tar` payloads were
  confirmed byte-identical, the final `.tar.gz` files *still* differed -- isolated to Python's own
  `gzip.GzipFile`, which produced different compressed bytes for byte-identical input across two calls
  in the same process, same arguments (`mtime=`, `compresslevel=`); the system `gzip -n` binary did not
  have this problem, confirmed by compressing the identical payload twice and diffing.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` alone, as the wheel does (rejected: verified
  insufficient for the sdist specifically -- this is precisely the gap the rehearsal found); patch
  around Python's `gzip` module's non-determinism with different arguments (rejected: root cause not
  isolated beyond "same input produces different output across two same-process calls," which by
  itself disqualifies output-byte reproducibility as a property of that code path regardless of which
  arguments are passed; the system binary is a well-tested, known-deterministic alternative already
  available on every CI runner and every contributor's machine without a new dependency).
- **Evidence:** two full `python -m build --sdist` invocations of the same commit, same
  `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, normalized with the new script, produce byte-identical sha256 digests; `twine
  check` passes on the normalized archive (repacking did not corrupt it); extracted content matches the
  pre-normalization archive exactly. 5 new unit tests in `mixle/tests/normalize_sdist_test.py` against
  small synthetic tarballs (not a real, slow package build) pin: two builds with different wall-clock
  member mtimes normalize identically; the normalized archive still extracts its original content; every
  member's mtime/uid/gid/uname/gname is pinned; a different epoch produces different bytes (confirms the
  epoch is actually used); repeated normalization of identical input is itself deterministic (the exact
  hazard that motivated this). Negative-controlled: a version of `normalize()` with the per-member
  mtime/uid/gid loop body removed fails 3 of the 5 tests, confirming they are not vacuous.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0084
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** S13.3 (packaging), P2 (packaging and artifacts)

## D-0019 · Extend D-0009's pickle-trust gate to `Embedder.load`

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** `mixle.represent.Embedder.load` unconditionally called `pickle.load` on the artifact at
  `path`, including `AutoencoderResult.encoder`'s live `torch.nn.Module` -- the same code-execution risk
  D-0009 addressed for `lifecycle.Model.load`, on a class D-0009's pass never covered (`Embedder` sits in
  `mixle/represent/`, outside `lifecycle.Model`'s hierarchy). `load` now takes `trust_code: bool = False`
  and raises `SerializationError` unless the caller passes `trust_code=True` or is already inside
  `mixle.utils.serialization.trusted_deserialization()`, matching
  `mixle.inference.production.registry.Registry.get`'s existing convention exactly. Not a new policy --
  D-0009 already named this class of bug S13 (security) and closed-by-default as the project's standard;
  this closes a gap in that pass's coverage.
- **Alternatives considered:** leave `Embedder.load` as unconditional pickle with a docstring warning only
  (rejected: identical reasoning to D-0009's rejection of the same option -- a docstring does not stop a
  caller from pointing `load` at an untrusted path, and `Embedder.load` is public, exported, and
  documented in `docs/representation.rst`); give `Embedder` its own bespoke trust mechanism instead of
  reusing `mixle.utils.serialization`'s gate (rejected: would create a second, inconsistent trust API for
  the same underlying risk with no offsetting benefit).
- **Evidence:** confirmed via direct source read that `load` had zero validation before this change (no
  trust flag, no signature check, no safe-deserialization alternative) and that its sole in-repo call site
  (`mixle/tests/represent_api_test.py`) only ever loaded a path the same test had just written -- i.e. the
  gap was real but not yet exploited internally. 3 new tests in `EmbedderLoadTrustGateTest` (refuse by
  default; succeed with `trust_code=True`; succeed inside `trusted_deserialization()`), matching the
  existing `ModuleDeserializationTrustGateTest` pattern in `mixle/tests/neural_leaf_serialization_test.py`.
  Full `mixle/tests/represent_api_test.py` (5 tests) passes.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** S13 (security)

## D-0020 · Scope the stable-family invariant catalog gate to what it actually covers

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** `0.8.0.md`'s "Scientific and numerical correctness" gate read "Stable-family invariants and
  numerical stress — COMPLETE," citing `invariant_catalog_test.py` and `numerical_stress_panel_test.py`
  with no scope qualifier — readable as blanket stable-surface coverage. Independently counted:
  `mixle.stats` publishes 156 `*Distribution` symbols; `invariant_catalog_test.py`'s `_CATALOG` covers 7
  (Gaussian, Gamma, Exponential, LogGaussian, Poisson, Geometric, Categorical), `numerical_stress_panel_test.py`
  covers 6 plus one HMM composite over 2 of those 7 as leaves — roughly 4.5% of the stable surface by the
  narrower catalog. The gate row now states the covered count and family list explicitly. Not reversing or
  removing the gate (the 7-family contract is real, enforced, and passes) — narrowing the claim to match
  what it actually tests, per the D-0011 precedent.
- **Alternatives considered:** mark the gate `HOSTED`/incomplete and treat it as release-blocking until the
  catalog covers most/all 156 families (rejected for *this* checklist edit: that is a real, large piece of
  work — see follow-up below — and blocking the release-prep PR on it now would conflate "the claim is
  honest" with "the coverage is exhaustive," which D-0011 already established are separable); leave the
  claim as-is on the reasoning that "stable-family" was never meant literally (rejected: indistinguishable
  from the overstatement the external review flagged, and the whole point of this release cycle per its own
  worklist is closing credibility gaps like this one, not re-arguing them).
- **Follow-up (not yet done, tracked here so it isn't lost):** widen `_CATALOG` well beyond 7 families,
  organized by the module groups `mixle.stats` actually has (univariate 36, latent 24, combinator 19,
  rankings 13, multivariate 14, graphs 11, bayes 9, directional 8, processes 7, matrix 4, sets 4, sequences
  3, trees 3, compute 1) — full 156-family exhaustiveness in one pass is not attempted here; breadth across
  every structurally-distinct family is the near-term target, tracked under the existing Q5.1 worklist ID
  (`0.8.0-rc-stages.md` already lists "stable family invariant suite | Q5.1 | #349" as the delivering PR for
  the original 7-family pass). This gate's status/evidence text should be re-widened again once that lands,
  not left at "7 of 156" permanently.
- **Evidence:** direct count via `[n for n in dir(mixle.stats) if n.endswith("Distribution")]` = 156;
  direct read of `invariant_catalog_test.py`'s `_CATALOG` list = 7 entries; direct read of
  `numerical_stress_panel_test.py`'s `_CASES` construction = 6 single-family cases plus one
  `HiddenMarkovModelDistribution` composite.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** supersede once the catalog-widening follow-up lands and the gate text is updated again.
- **Worklist:** Q5.1, N9.2 (honest claims)

## D-0021 · Extend CTMC trajectories with an explicit horizon; the likelihood was missing the final censored dwell

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** `ContinuousTimeMarkovChainDistribution`'s trajectory data format changes from `(s0, jumps)`
  to `(s0, horizon, jumps)` -- a breaking change to the data contract. An external review found
  `_trajectory_stats` only added dwell time to `T_i` when a jump fired to trigger the addition, so every
  trajectory's *final* dwell interval -- from its last jump (or from time 0, if it never jumps) out to
  when observation stopped -- was silently dropped from the log-likelihood's `-sum_i q_i * T_i` term,
  contradicting the module's own docstring formula. The old 2-element format had no field to represent
  "when did observation stop," so this was not a patchable accumulation bug: without a horizon, `T_i` for
  the final state is mathematically unrecoverable from the data as it was shaped. `horizon` is now a
  mandatory third element of every trajectory, validated (finite, >= 0, and jump dwell times may not sum
  past it), and `_trajectory_stats` adds the missing final censored interval explicitly. The distribution's
  own `initial_state`/`horizon` constructor parameters remain sampling-time defaults only (where
  `sample()` starts a fresh chain and for how long, round-tripped through `.estimator()` per the existing
  `test_estimator_round_trip_preserves_initial_state_and_horizon` contract); `log_density` scores whatever
  `s0`/`horizon` each trajectory itself declares, not the configured defaults -- investigated and confirmed
  nothing else in the codebase ties `initial_state` to a must-match-`s0` semantic, and the closed-form rate
  MLE (`q_ij = n_ij/T_i`) does not depend on it either.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep the 2-element format and thread a separate `horizon` argument through
  `log_density`/the accumulator out-of-band (rejected: `seq_log_density` and the accumulator only ever see
  already-encoded sufficient statistics with no side channel for per-trajectory metadata, and every
  trajectory in a batch can legitimately have been observed for a different length of time, so it has to
  travel with the data); constrain `log_density` to `-inf` whenever a trajectory's `s0` disagrees with the
  distribution's configured `initial_state` (rejected: no other code or test ties `initial_state` to the
  data beyond `sample()`'s own starting point, and doing so would penalize the ordinary case of fitting one
  CTMC to trajectories legitimately observed starting from different states, e.g. a cohort entering a study
  at different stages); put `horizon` in a different tuple position, e.g. appended last (rejected: matched
  `BirthDeathSamplingDistribution`'s existing `(n0, T, events)` convention instead -- the only sibling
  process family that already carries its observation window inside the per-trajectory data rather than
  only on the distribution).
- **Evidence:** reproduced both of the review's exact numeric findings against the pre-fix code before
  changing anything: an empty two-state path (exit rate 2, horizon 5) scored `0.0` instead of `-10.0`; a
  one-jump path scored `-1.3068528194400546` instead of `-13.306852819440055`. 11 new tests plus 2 existing
  tests updated (the old-format literal and the hand-computed formula, previously missing the post-jump
  censored term) in `mixle/tests/ctmc_test.py` pin both corrected values, dwell/horizon validation
  (negative, non-finite, and over-horizon dwell; non-finite/negative horizon; out-of-range state indices),
  rejection of the old 2-element format, the `initial_state` non-constraint decision, the sampler emitting
  and round-tripping the new format, and that the accumulator's `q_ij = n_ij/T_i` MLE now uses the
  corrected `T_i` (a never-jumping trajectory now contributes real dwell instead of zero, which previously
  biased every fitted rate upward). Negative-controlled via file-scoped stash on `ctmc.py` alone: 13 of the
  19 tests in the file fail against the pre-fix code (the other 6 do not touch trajectory data, or already
  passed). Broader sweep of every test file that imports `ContinuousTimeMarkovChainDistribution` or
  constructs CTMC trajectories -- `keyed_pooling_test.py`, `keyed_protocol_sweep_test.py`,
  `sampler_seed_test.py` -- 43 tests total, all passing. `docs/processes.rst`'s CTMC example updated to the
  new format and manually verified to run. ruff check and ruff format --check clean on the touched files.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (data-format break, not a temporary waiver).
- **Worklist:** Q5 (numerical/statistical correctness), E14.2 (adversarial statistical review)

## D-0022 · Restart/saddle-restart paths in fit(calibrate=...) must reuse the same training-only partition

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** `Model.fit(calibrate=...)` reserves a calibration holdout (`cal_holdout`) and fits on the
  remaining `fit_data` partition, but the restart-triggering block (both the `saddle_suspect` check and
  the `_refit_symmetry_broken` call it gates) passed the caller's original full `data` instead of
  `fit_data`. Any restart -- an explicit `restarts=N` request, or an automatic saddle-triggered one --
  therefore refit on `data`, which includes the calibration rows, and the resulting model (when kept)
  replaced `self.fitted`; `self.calibration` was then computed against holdout rows that were no longer
  actually held out. Both call sites now pass `fit_data` (identical to `data` whenever `calibrate` did not
  actually reserve a holdout, so the non-`calibrate` path is unchanged). `_refit_symmetry_broken`'s
  docstring now states the invariant explicitly so a future caller cannot reintroduce this silently.
- **Alternatives considered:** re-derive a fresh calibration split after any restart (rejected: the point
  of a holdout is that it is fixed for the whole fit -- re-splitting after seeing a restart's result would
  itself be a form of leakage, and the docstring already promises "fit on the rest" once, not per attempt);
  disable `calibrate` and `restarts` together (rejected: unnecessarily removes a combination that is safe
  and useful once the shared partition is threaded through correctly, and neither feature's docs describe
  such a restriction).
- **Evidence:** direct reproduction of the audit's exact instrumented eight-row `restarts=1, calibrate=.25`
  scenario confirmed the pre-fix leak (`initial fit rows: [1, 7, 3, 0, 5, 4]`,
  `restart rows: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7]`, `calibration rows: [6, 2]` -- both calibration rows present in
  the restart's own training data) and its absence after the fix (restart rows match the initial fit rows
  exactly). Also reproduced and fixed for the automatic saddle-triggered path (not just an explicit
  `restarts=N`): a Gamma-mixture symmetric-saddle fit with `calibrate=.25` active leaked 200 calibration
  rows into the automatic restart across every independently-saddling seed found (8, 10, 16, 18, 19, 28 of
  30 tried), 0 leaked after the fix. 2 new regression tests in `mixle/tests/lifecycle_test.py`'s new
  `CalibrateRestartLeakTest` (explicit-restart and automatic-saddle-restart cases) pin both scenarios.
  Negative-controlled via a file-scoped backup of the pre-fix `lifecycle.py` alone: both new tests fail
  against the pre-fix code (the explicit case's failure diff shows exactly rows `2` and `6`, the audit's
  own calibration indices, leaking into the restart) and pass after restoring the fix. Full
  `lifecycle_test.py` (16 tests), `calibrate_fit_test.py` (11 tests), and `create_test.py` (5 tests --
  `mixle.inference.create.create()` is a separate, independent implementation with no restart path, so
  unaffected, confirmed by direct read) all pass. `ruff check`/`format --check` clean on both touched
  files.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** Q5 (numerical/statistical correctness), E14.2 (adversarial statistical review)

## D-0023 · Encoded-data integrity digest now covers the header, not just the pickle body

- **Date:** 2026-07-24
- **Decision:** `mixle/data/encoded_io.py`'s `save_encoded`/`load_encoded` integrity digest now covers
  the whole envelope -- the JSON header's fields (currently just the recorded encoder signature) plus
  the pickle body -- instead of the body alone (MXR-080-0052). An external review found the digest was
  computed only from `pickle.dumps(encoded)`, so the header's `"encoder"` field could be edited on disk
  without touching the body or the stored digest, and `load_encoded` would still accept the payload
  under the forged identity. Header fields are folded into the digest via
  `mixle.data.hashing._canonical` (the same self-delimiting canonical encoder `dataset_hash`/
  `model_hash` already use) rather than raw JSON bytes, so on-disk key order/whitespace differences can
  never produce a false "corrupt" mismatch. The on-disk magic bumped `PSPENC1` -> `PSPENC2` alongside
  the digest-scope change, so a file written by the pre-fix code is rejected outright as an
  unrecognized format -- a clear, correctly-labeled error -- rather than failing the new, wider digest
  check in a way that reads like corruption of a file that is actually just old-format.
- **Alternatives considered:** full cryptographic authentication (HMAC with a secret, or signing)
  (rejected: no signing-key/HMAC infrastructure exists anywhere in the codebase, confirmed by a
  repo-wide search, and the module's own docstring already documents this digest as
  corruption-detection, not authentication, matching `lifecycle.Model.load`'s equally explicit
  local-trust-only pickle contract; introducing key management for one internal re-encode-cache
  function is a disproportionate, undesigned addition the finding's own text explicitly allows
  skipping -- "if authentication is out of scope, the whole envelope must at least be included in an
  integrity digest stored or supplied independently"); keep the old magic and let old files fail the
  new digest check (rejected: produces a misleading "failed its integrity check (corrupt or
  truncated)" error for a file that is not corrupt, merely written by older code -- this format is an
  internal re-encode cache, not a durable archival artifact (the module docstring: "so subsequent fits
  load it directly"), so cleanly invalidating stale caches via a magic bump costs nothing a fresh
  re-encode does not already pay for); hash raw on-disk header bytes instead of a canonical
  re-serialization (rejected: ties the digest to incidental JSON formatting -- key order, whitespace --
  risking a false corruption signal on a legitimately-written file, exactly the pitfall the finding
  warned against).
- **Evidence:** reproduced the audit's exact scenario before changing anything: saved a 2-dimensional
  `DiagonalGaussianDataEncoder` payload, hand-edited only the header's `"encoder"` field to claim the
  3-dimensional signature (digest and body bytes untouched), and confirmed the pre-fix `load_encoded(path,
  encoder=enc_3d)` accepted it, silently returning 2D data mislabeled as 3D; the identical repro raises
  `ValueError` after the fix. 3 new tests in `EncodedIoTest` (`mixle/tests/provenance_test.py`) pin
  this: `test_header_tampering_detected` (the exact repro above, now rejected),
  `test_envelope_digest_covers_header_fields_not_just_body` (two different header dicts over the same
  body must produce different digests -- the property that was entirely missing pre-fix), and
  `test_envelope_digest_is_canonical_regardless_of_header_key_order` (two header dicts with different
  key insertion order but identical content produce the same digest). `test_header_is_json_not_pickle`'s
  hardcoded magic-byte assertion updated to `PSPENC2`. Full `mixle/tests/provenance_test.py` (38 tests
  -- the only test file, plus `mixle/data/__init__.py`, importing `encoded_io`, confirmed by a
  repo-wide grep) passes; a fresh top-level `import mixle` / `from mixle.data import save_encoded,
  load_encoded` also confirmed clean (no circular import from the new `mixle.data.hashing` dependency).
  Negative-controlled via a file-scoped backup/restore of `encoded_io.py` alone (`cp`/`git show
  HEAD:<path>`, not `git stash`, per the shared-worktree protocol): swapping in the pre-fix file makes
  the whole test module fail to even collect (`ImportError: cannot import name '_envelope_digest'`),
  and a standalone reproduction script run against the pre-fix code, then again after the fix,
  independently confirmed the pre-fix code accepts the tampered file under the forged identity while
  the post-fix code rejects it. `ruff check`/`format --check` clean on both touched files.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (data-format break, not a temporary waiver).
- **Worklist:** S13 (security)

## D-0024 · `StreamingCorpus.epoch_batches` now yields a loss mask as a third element

- **Date:** 2026-07-25
- **Decision:** `mixle/data/streaming_corpus.py`'s `pack_documents` now returns a per-position
  `loss_mask` alongside its packed rows, and `epoch_batches` yields `(context, targets, loss_mask)`
  instead of `(context, targets)` (MXR-080-0061). An external review found that the final packed row's
  tail padding was yielded as ordinary next-token targets with no signal distinguishing fabricated
  `pad_id` positions from real observed labels — a short final document under a larger block size meant
  training silently treated padding as a valid next-token prediction, with the already-computed
  `real_tokens` count never reaching the batch to prevent it. The mask is a closed-form slice over the
  known packing layout (real positions `True`, fabricated tail padding `False`), not a value comparison
  against `pad_id`, since a legitimate token could collide with whatever id is chosen for padding. Every
  caller of `epoch_batches` must now unpack a 3-tuple and apply the mask before computing loss; this is a
  breaking change to the batch shape, not merely an additive optional field, because a caller that keeps
  destructuring a 2-tuple raises immediately (`ValueError: too many values to unpack`) rather than
  silently continuing to train on fabricated labels.
- **Alternatives considered:** drop incomplete final positions instead of masking them (rejected: changes
  effective batch/token counts per epoch in a way existing callers may assume are fixed, and discards
  real prefix tokens within the final padded row that a mask can keep contributing to loss); compare
  target values against `pad_id` to infer which positions are padding (rejected: indistinguishable from a
  legitimate token that happens to equal `pad_id`, reintroducing a silent-corruption path instead of
  fixing one); make the mask an optional keyword-gated return to preserve the old 2-tuple call signature
  by default (rejected: an opt-in correctness fix is still silently wrong for every caller that doesn't
  know to opt in, which is the exact failure mode being fixed).
- **Evidence:** reproduced the audit's exact scenario before changing anything: a two-token document
  packed under `block=4` produced targets `[11, 0, 0, 0]` with no way to tell the trailing zeros were
  fabricated; the identical repro now yields `loss_mask=[True, False, False, False]`. 30 new/extended
  tests across `mixle/tests/streaming_corpus_test.py` and the new `mixle/tests/stream_token_source_test.py`
  (53 total between the two files) pin the mask's boundary policy (only the final row's tail is ever
  masked False; `boundary_id` tokens are real signal and stay unmasked) and the sibling token-dtype/
  iteration-control fixes landed in the same file cluster (MXR-080-0062/0063). Grepped every consumer of
  `StreamingCorpus`/`epoch_batches` repo-wide; `mixle/utils/parallel/fault_tolerant_training.py` is the
  only other reference and does not destructure the tuple, so it needed no code change (its docstring's
  stale 2-tuple description is tracked as a separate follow-up, not a functional gap). Negative-controlled
  via file-scoped backup/restore of the two touched source files; broader sweep of 85 tests across both
  changed files plus every consumer/adjacent test file (`neural_ppl_test.py`, `self_distillation_test.py`,
  `torch_neural_test.py`, `language_model_dense_fit_test.py`, `language_model_sft_test.py`,
  `language_model_persistence_test.py`, `fault_tolerant_training_test.py`) passes. `ruff check`/
  `format --check` clean.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (data-format break, not a temporary waiver).
- **Worklist:** Q5 (numerical/statistical correctness), E14.2 (adversarial statistical review)

## D-0025 · `enumeration.streams.freeze()` keys are type/dtype-aware, not Python-equality-aware

- **Date:** 2026-07-25
- **Decision:** `mixle/enumeration/streams.py`'s `freeze()` (the canonical hashable dedup key used
  across every union, distinct-stream, and mass-certificate enumeration path) now includes dtype in
  ndarray keys (`(shape, dtype.str, bytes)` instead of `(shape, bytes)`) and tags every other value by
  `(type(x), x)` instead of bare `x` (MXR-080-0198). An external review found `tobytes()`-only ndarray
  keys let an `int8` array holding `-56` and a `uint8` array holding `200` collide (both encode to the
  single byte `0xc8`), and bare-scalar keys let Python's native cross-type equality collapse
  `freeze(True) == freeze(1)` and `freeze(False) == freeze(0)` — even though a typed distribution's
  support can legitimately contain both a bool and an int (or an int and a float) as distinct outcomes.
  The new policy makes type part of a value's dedup identity unconditionally: `freeze(1) != freeze(1.0)`
  is now also true, as a direct, deliberate consequence of the same single rule, not a separate special
  case. NaN is the one documented exception — every NaN float still collapses to one shared sentinel
  regardless of origin, since IEEE 754 `nan != nan` would otherwise make each NaN a unique,
  never-deduplicated key, which is not the desired dedup behavior for a "missing/undefined" value.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep bare-value keys and special-case only the reported `bool`/`int`
  collision (rejected: leaves every other Python cross-type equality — `1 == 1.0`, `Decimal` vs `int`,
  etc. — silently colliding too; the finding's own remediation asked for "a single documented cross-type
  equality policy," not a one-off patch); make `freeze()` idempotent on its own tagged output by
  detecting and stripping a prior `(type, value)` tag before re-tagging (rejected: a tagged key's shape
  is not reliably distinguishable from a legitimate user value that happens to look like one — e.g. a
  genuine 2-tuple support value — so "is this already frozen" can't be decided safely from the value
  alone; fixing the one real caller that depended on accidental idempotence is a smaller, more honest
  fix than adding an ambiguous self-detection rule to the primitive every enumeration path relies on).
- **Evidence:** reproduced both collisions against the pre-fix code before changing anything —
  `np.array([200], dtype=np.uint8).tobytes() == np.array([-56], dtype=np.int8).tobytes()` (confirmed
  `True`, both `b'\xc8'`), and `freeze(True) == freeze(1)` (confirmed `True` pre-fix, `False` post-fix).
  Tagging scalars by type makes `freeze()` non-idempotent on its own output (a frozen scalar becomes a
  tuple, so re-freezing it recurses into the tag) — a repo-wide grep found exactly one caller relying on
  the old accidental idempotence: `ChowLiuTreeDistribution.__init__` (`mixle/stats/trees/chow_liu_tree.py`)
  re-froze `conditional_dists` keys its own docstring already documented as pre-frozen by every caller,
  harmless before, silently double-wrapping and breaking every conditional lookup after. Fixed by
  removing that redundant re-freeze at the source rather than complicating `freeze()` to preserve the
  accident. New `mixle/tests/enumeration_streams_test.py` covers the int8/uint8 collision, the
  `True`/`1` and `int`/`float` cross-type cases, numpy-scalar-width dedup (`int8`/`uint8`/`int64` of the
  same value still dedupe together via `.item()`'s dtype-aware unwrapping), NaN collapse, and a
  list/tuple negative control confirming existing, intentional element-wise collapsing is unaffected.
  Broader sweep: 297 tests (557 in the final full-file sweep) across every enumeration/quantization/
  latent/causal/truncation file importing `freeze`/`BufferedStream`/`merge_enumerators`, direct or via
  `mixle.enumeration.algorithms`'s re-export, pass. `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (dedup-key semantics change, not a temporary waiver).
- **Worklist:** Q5 (numerical/statistical correctness), N9.2 (honest claims)

## D-0026 · Provisional-tier public-surface growth from the audit-fix wave gets one consolidated manifest reconciliation, not a per-symbol exception

- **Date:** 2026-07-25
- **Decision:** `mixle/tests/public_api_manifest_test.py` is currently failing (confirmed on a clean
  `git archive HEAD` checkout, independent of any in-flight change): `manifests/api_manifest.json` has
  drifted from the actual public surface of `mixle.analysis`, `mixle.doe`, `mixle.engines`,
  `mixle.enumeration`, and `mixle.substrate`. Every added symbol is additive (`+[...] -[]` on every
  line — nothing removed or renamed) and traces to a specific, already-fixed, already-tested MXR-080
  finding: `CertifiedCount`/`CertifiedMassBound` (MXR-080-0230), `SoundTopKResult` (MXR-080-0211/0212),
  `RescoreResult` (MXR-080-0233), `LineageState` (MXR-080-0260/0261), `AccessPolicy`/`MergeStrategy`/
  `revision`/`AccessDeniedError` (MXR-080-0264/0265/0266), `GovernanceAuthorizationError`
  (MXR-080-0263), plus several `mixle.analysis`/`mixle.doe`/`mixle.engines` symbols from earlier fixes
  in the same wave. This is the same design decision applied repeatedly, not dozens of independent
  ones: the review's own recurring finding is that a bare bool/float/dict return can silently claim a
  certainty or authorization it never actually earned, and the established remedy — used consistently
  since `best_first.py`'s `SoundTopKResult` — is a small, explicit, typed result carrying its real
  certification/authorization status. Rather than requiring a separate written exception for each such
  type as it lands (which the freeze rule would otherwise require per symbol), this entry blanket-covers
  provisional-tier additions of exactly this shape for the duration of the in-progress audit-fix wave.
  `manifests/api_manifest.json` will be regenerated (`python scripts/gen_api_manifest.py`) and committed
  in one dedicated reconciliation pass once the wave's dispatch queue is substantially drained, not after
  each individual fix.
- **Alternatives considered:** a separate decision-log entry per new symbol (rejected: the dispatch rate
  in this wave — many independent agents each landing one legitimate new type under the same established
  structured-result methodology — would flood the log with near-duplicate single-symbol entries for what
  is really one repeated design call, and would slow every in-flight dispatch on a freeze-process
  formality rather than a substantive review question); keep all new types module-private and never
  re-export them from `mixle.<pkg>.__init__` to avoid tripping the gate at all (this is what the
  `factuality.py` fix correctly did for `Corroboration`, whose export was not load-bearing for anything
  else — but it is not viable as a blanket policy, since some of these types, `AccessPolicy` in
  particular, are genuine cross-file public contracts that `belief.py`, `trust.py`, and `governance.py`'s
  own already-landed fixes depend on by name and cannot be un-exported without breaking those); regenerate
  and commit the manifest after every single commit that adds surface (rejected: with several agents
  committing concurrently across unrelated files throughout this wave, a regeneration committed by one
  would be stale again within minutes from a sibling's concurrent commit, producing repeated churn on a
  generated artifact with no benefit before the wave settles).
- **Evidence:** `python -m pytest mixle/tests/public_api_manifest_test.py` fails with the exact additive
  drift list above; a sibling agent (`freshness.py`'s MXR-080-0267 fix) independently confirmed this same
  failure is present on a `git archive HEAD` extraction with and without its own diff applied, producing
  byte-identical drift output either way — i.e. genuinely pre-existing, not introduced by any single
  in-flight fix. No stable-tier package is affected.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** this waiver covers only provisional-tier, additive surface growth from the audit-fix
  wave in progress as of 2026-07-25. It expires the moment `manifests/api_manifest.json` is regenerated
  and committed in the planned reconciliation pass; any drift found after that point needs its own fresh
  exception under the ordinary freeze rule.
- **Worklist:** G0 (scope, freeze, ancestry, and ownership); cross-cutting "public API surface" category
  (0.8.0 exhaustive code review coverage ledger)

## D-0027 · Complete the stable multivariate namespace as an additive compatibility change

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Resolve MXR-080-1189 by making `mixle.stats.multivariate` an explicit, static public
  namespace and making every symbol in that focused namespace directly importable from
  `mixle.stats`. The focused namespace includes framework integration types (samplers, encoders,
  accumulators, factories, receipts, and exact enumerators); `mixle.stats.__all__` continues its
  established curation rule that omits names ending in `Sampler`, `DataEncoder`, `Accumulator`, or
  `AccumulatorFactory`, while leaving those names directly importable. Previously omitted
  compositional transforms and Aitchison-normal modeling are included in the curated consolidated
  surface. This is additive: no existing symbol is removed, renamed, or redirected.
- **Alternatives considered:** leave the empty focused namespace in place (rejected because it
  contradicts its own documentation and hides implemented capabilities); export only distributions
  (rejected because framework integrations need stable resolution of encoded-data, fitting, and
  sufficient-statistic types); put all plumbing into `mixle.stats.__all__` (rejected because it would
  discard the existing, intentional distinction between user-facing modeling concepts and advanced
  integration machinery).
- **Evidence:** `mixle/tests/multivariate_public_api_test.py` checks uniqueness, resolution, identity
  across both namespaces, curated user-facing coverage, and direct availability of integration
  plumbing. `manifests/api_manifest.json` records the static focused namespace and the additive
  consolidated surface.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (additive stable public-surface definition).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1189, G0 public API freeze reconciliation.

## D-0028 · Expose bounded Tweedie evaluation failure as a structured public error

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Add `TweedieSeriesResourceError` to the stable `mixle.stats` surface. Tweedie's
  compound-Poisson density is evaluated by an infinite series, so a caller-selected accuracy regime
  can require more terms or aggregate row/term work than its configured resource budget allows.
  Exhausting that budget is neither invalid input nor a numerical value for the density; a dedicated
  exception lets callers distinguish a safely refused computation from both cases and adjust policy
  without matching error text.
- **Alternatives considered:** raise `RuntimeError` (rejected because callers cannot reliably
  distinguish resource exhaustion from implementation failure); truncate silently (rejected because
  it returns an unquantified approximation as if it were the requested density); leave the type
  module-private (rejected because callers need a stable type to implement explicit retry or fallback
  policy).
- **Evidence:** focused scalar and backend tests force resource exhaustion before unbounded allocation
  and assert this exact type; parity tests verify ordinary bounded evaluations retain scalar,
  NumPy-sequence, and backend agreement.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (additive structured failure contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1197, G0 public API freeze reconciliation.

## D-0029 · Discrete probability objects require probability laws unless explicitly marked as scorers

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** `CategoricalDistribution` now requires a non-empty normalized finite support with
  zero fallback mass by default. Legacy unnormalized maps and positive unknown-label fallbacks remain
  available only through the explicit `scoring_only=True` mode; those objects can be evaluated but
  cannot be sampled. `IntegerCategoricalDistribution` likewise requires a non-empty probability
  simplex. Integer-categorical and integer-uniform-spike supports and observations use exact integer
  semantics, and their no-data estimators require or preserve explicit support instead of producing
  unusable or non-finite fitted laws.
- **Alternatives considered:** silently normalize arbitrary maps (rejected because it changes the
  scores callers deliberately configured and cannot define a generative law for an unspecified
  fallback label space); keep sampling from registered labels after normalizing only those labels
  (rejected because sampler and scorer would continue to implement different laws); remove fallback
  scoring entirely (rejected because bounded heuristic scoring remains useful when its non-generative
  status is explicit).
- **Evidence:** focused probability-law contracts cover empty fitting, sampler refusal, exact scalar
  and encoded integer support, accumulator ownership, singleton spike laws, prior/support alignment,
  and mutable point-mass draws. A repository-wide static scan reconciled literal categorical
  construction sites; affected categorical, integer-count, mixture, enumeration, SQUAREM, and
  black-box Laplace tests pass under the new contract.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0138
- **Expiration:** none (probability-law and constructor-contract correction).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1220 through MXR-080-1230, G0 API-break reconciliation.

## D-0030 · Mixtures reject likelihood factors and expose their variational score as a bound

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Scoring-only categoricals declare `LIKELIHOOD_FACTOR` density semantics and cannot
  be inserted into a generative finite mixture. `MixtureDistribution` owns copies of its weight
  vector and component container, raises `ImpossiblePosteriorError` before conditioning on
  zero-probability evidence, and exposes the conjugate-prior score under the honest
  `variational_log_evidence_bound` / `seq_variational_log_evidence_bound` names. The framework-wide
  `expected_log_density` compatibility names delegate to that bound with explicit documentation;
  they do not claim to compute the generally intractable expectation of a log mixture.
  Mixture-accumulator serialization and keyed pooling now copy both count arrays and child
  sufficient statistics at ownership boundaries.
- **Alternatives considered:** continue treating fallback scores as probability densities
  (rejected because their unspecified label space is not normalizable); normalize impossible
  conditioning into NaN weights and rely on a later constructor failure (rejected because it hides
  the evidence error); remove the compatibility methods (rejected because the common variational
  interface uses them); share pooled accumulator objects between tied sites (rejected because a
  subsequent local update corrupts every site).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover external parameter mutation, factor rejection, impossible
  conditioning, named-bound/scalar/vector parity, count snapshot ownership, and independent keyed
  child accumulators. Mixture, Bayesian-mixture, sparse-score, SQUAREM, and evidence tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (probability, conditioning, variational-semantics, and ownership contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1234 through MXR-080-1237 and the generic-mixture portion of MXR-080-1233.

## D-0031 · Specialized mixtures share one probability and impossible-evidence contract

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Heterogeneous, semi-supervised, and hierarchical finite mixtures now own their
  component containers and probability arrays, require non-empty aligned probability simplexes,
  reject likelihood factors as generative components, and use the same impossible-evidence policy
  as the generic mixture: a row outside every component support has `-inf` evidence and all-zero
  responsibilities. Semi-supervised priors require exact in-range integer component indices and
  finite non-negative mass; an impossible restricted observation never reactivates a component
  excluded by that prior. Empty hierarchical documents remain possible and retain the outer prior.
  Specialized accumulator serialization and keyed pooling copy count arrays and child statistics at
  ownership boundaries.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain each class's independent validation and fallback behavior
  (rejected because equivalent finite-mixture models then assign contradictory posterior mass);
  substitute the mixture prior for impossible evidence (rejected because that fabricates sufficient
  statistics for probability-zero data); reactivate all semi-supervised components when a restricted
  row is impossible (rejected because it violates the observation's stated component restriction);
  share child accumulator objects through keyed pools (rejected because later local updates mutate
  unrelated tied sites).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover malformed probability geometry, external mutation,
  factor rejection, exact semi-supervised prior entries, impossible scalar and encoded posteriors,
  zero accumulator mass, hierarchical empty-document behavior, and count-snapshot ownership.
  Existing hierarchical, semi-supervised, heterogeneous, engine, and fused-EM focused tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (shared finite-mixture probability and ownership contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1233, MXR-080-1237, and MXR-080-1238.

## D-0032 · Joint mixtures have one canonical latent joint law

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** `JointMixtureDistribution` stores one owned, normalized `joint_weights` table and
  derives both marginals and both conditional tables from it. The constructor accepts
  `joint_weights` as the unambiguous API. For 0.8 compatibility it also accepts the legacy
  `w1`/`w2`/`taus12`/`taus21` form after validating every simplex; if the two directions disagree,
  it emits a deprecation warning and canonicalizes from the historically authoritative scoring law
  `w1 * taus12`. The historical transposed rectangular `taus21` layout is accepted with a
  deprecation warning. Estimation normalizes one joint count table rather than independently fitting
  contradictory marginal and conditional parameters. Impossible paired observations have `-inf`
  evidence and contribute zero marginal and joint sufficient statistics. Joint-mixture accumulator
  serialization and keyed child pools own their snapshots.
- **Alternatives considered:** reject every inconsistent legacy call immediately (rejected for the
  0.8 compatibility release because the repository and downstream callers used the redundant
  constructor extensively); retain four independent stored arrays (rejected because the object can
  then describe two different probability laws); silently prefer the forward law (rejected because
  callers need a migration signal); average the two supplied laws (rejected because it changes the
  density that legacy scoring and sampling actually implemented).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts verify parameter ownership, exact marginal/conditional
  reconstruction, legacy reconciliation warnings, invalid geometry, impossible-evidence scoring and
  accumulation, and count-snapshot ownership. Joint host/NumPy/Torch E-step parity, fused-EM,
  scalar/vector update, and enumeration tests pass. The public example uses `joint_weights`.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** remove the redundant constructor form only in a future major-version API change.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1239 and joint-mixture portions of MXR-080-1233/MXR-080-1237.

## D-0033 · Gated-mixture optimization is bounded, validated, and observable

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Gated mixtures validate generative expert arity, gate class/feature geometry,
  normalized gate probabilities, finite covariates, aligned non-negative observation weights, and
  positive finite optimization controls. Impossible observations retain `-inf` evidence and zero
  responsibility rather than falling back to the gate prior. `SoftmaxGate` owns its parameter
  arrays, uses a bounded monotone backtracking optimization, and returns a
  `GateOptimizationReceipt` containing convergence, loss, gradient, step, and termination status.
  Gate-training rows are retained in a finite `max_buffer_rows` deterministic content-priority
  coreset; the selection is mergeable across accumulator partitions and a `GateBufferReceipt`
  exposes seen/retained/dropped counts. Both receipts are public stable types and fitted
  `GatedMixtureDistribution` objects retain them.
- **Alternatives considered:** continue buffering every row (rejected because memory grows with the
  dataset); keep only the first rows (rejected because it is order-biased and partition merges are
  inconsistent); use an unseeded reservoir (rejected because fitting then depends on execution
  order and hidden randomness); substitute gate priors on impossible rows (rejected because it
  fabricates expert and gate-training statistics); return only fitted parameters (rejected because
  callers cannot distinguish convergence, a step limit, or numerical failure).
