- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover owned/finite gate parameters, invalid controls, covariate
  width and finiteness, malformed custom-gate output, empty batches, impossible evidence, bounded
  coreset retention and merge status, snapshot ownership, and optimizer receipts. Existing expert
  recovery, likelihood advantage, scalar/vector scoring, and conditional sampling tests pass; the
  public API manifest is synchronized and passes its drift checks.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (resource, probability, and optimization-observability contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1240 and gated-mixture portion of MXR-080-1233.

## D-0034 · Hidden Markov chains are proper stochastic laws with neutral empty batches

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Hidden Markov initial weights, transition rows, and optional per-state topic weights
  are owned normalized probability simplexes with exact state/topic geometry. Outside an explicit
  stopping-time model, a zero transition row is accepted only for a state unreachable from the
  initial law; stopping-time terminal rows may omit an unused outgoing law. Every accepted zero row
  is recorded explicitly and canonicalized to an absorbing self-loop so the stored object remains a probability law. The
  estimator uses the same self-loop rule when a state has no outgoing transition counts. Empty
  sequence batches encode to valid zero-sized layouts; host, backend, and engine scoring return an
  empty vector, and every accumulator path treats the batch as a no-op.
- **Alternatives considered:** permit arbitrary non-negative weights as unnormalized scores
  (rejected because scoring and sampling would no longer describe a probability distribution);
  retain all-zero transition rows (rejected because the stored chain is defective and later
  constructor validation becomes path-dependent); fill unobserved rows uniformly (rejected because
  that invents transitions to unrelated states); special-case only the encoder (rejected because
  scorers and accumulators still index empty time bands).
- **Evidence:** focused constructor contracts cover normalization, arity, matrix geometry,
  reachability, canonicalization, and ownership. Empty-batch contracts cover both HMM encodings,
  direct and backend scoring, and host and engine accumulation. HMM steady-state and engine parity
  tests pass with the same contracts.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (probability-law and batch-composability contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1241 and MXR-080-1242.

## D-0035 · Impossible HMM evidence fails transactionally across model families

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every HMM posterior or E-step that requires a conditional latent law now rejects
  zero-probability evidence with the existing public `ImpossibleEvidenceError`. Batch updates
  validate the complete evidence vector before mutating initial, transition, duration, state, length,
  or child-emission statistics, so a later impossible row cannot leave a partial update. This policy
  covers ordinary, tree, scheduled, semi-supervised, structured, chunked structured, input-output,
  and explicit-duration HMM paths, including engine-routed accumulation. Density evaluation remains
  total and reports impossible observations as `-inf`. Semi-supervised HMM encodings also expose and
  validate their row count so the standard fitting pipeline can enforce batch geometry.
- **Alternatives considered:** silently skip impossible rows (rejected because requested observation
  weight disappears); substitute uniform, prior, or all-zero responsibilities (rejected because no
  posterior law exists and child estimators receive fabricated statistics); mutate valid prefix rows
  and fail on the first impossible suffix row (rejected because retrying or partitioning would
  double-count a partial batch); make density scoring raise (rejected because `-inf` is the correct
  total log-density result and is needed for comparison and conditioning receipts).
- **Evidence:** cross-family contracts combine a valid prefix with an impossible suffix and verify
  the typed batch-row failure plus unchanged sufficient statistics. Ordinary and tree tests cover
  both compiled and NumPy encodings; HMM engine parity, scheduled, semi-supervised, structured
  forward/decoding, terminal, and explicit-duration focused tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (latent-conditioning and transactional-update contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1243.

## D-0036 · Stopping-time HMMs validate termination and fail bounded sampling explicitly

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Terminal hidden-state IDs are owned exact integers in range, terminal emission sets
  are owned non-empty collections, and terminal-state and terminal-value modes are mutually
  exclusive. For terminal-state models, every state reachable from positive initial mass must have
  a positive-probability graph path to a terminal state; terminal rows are canonicalized as
  self-loops when no outgoing law was supplied. All terminal-state and terminal-value sampling loops
  have an exact positive step bound and raise public `HiddenMarkovNonterminationError` if the stop
  event is not reached. They never return a truncated observation outside the scoring support. The
  same state validation and bounded-failure rule applies to lookback, semi-supervised, segmental,
  structured, and input-output HMM samplers. Structured non-absorbing final-state IDs use the same
  exact in-range validation and cannot be combined ambiguously with terminal states.
- **Alternatives considered:** coerce floats, booleans, or negative indices with `int` (rejected
  because distinct invalid inputs silently select real states); require only one reachable terminal
  state (rejected because another reachable closed non-terminal class leaves a defective stopping
  law); retain a large cap and return the unfinished path (rejected because the sampler would emit a
  value with model density zero); leave terminal-value sampling unbounded (rejected because arbitrary
  emission distributions cannot always prove a positive terminal-event probability statically).
- **Evidence:** constructor contracts cover fractional, boolean, empty, and out-of-range state sets,
  reachable traps, owned terminal-value sets, and conflicting stopping modes. Deterministic one-step
  cap tests verify typed failures and receipt fields. Ordinary, quantized, lookback,
  semi-supervised, segmental, structured, input-output, terminal enumeration, and public API
  manifest focused tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (proper stopping-law and bounded-sampling contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1244.

## D-0037 · Lookback HMMs use one exact schema from construction through estimation

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A lookback lag is an exact non-negative integer at the distribution, encoder,
  accumulator, factory, and estimator boundaries. The model owns its component collections,
  initial probability simplex, and exact square transition matrix; it requires one topic per state
  and, for a positive lag, one initial-segment component per state. Reachable non-terminal states
  cannot have defective zero transition rows; accepted unreachable or terminal zero rows are
  recorded and canonicalized to absorbing self-loops. Training components have the same state
  arity, controls are finite and non-negative, terminal IDs and conjugate-prior dimensions match the
  state space, and complete sufficient-statistic geometry is validated before child estimation or
  accumulator mutation. A lag-zero sampler consumes the stable seed stream without attempting to
  construct unused neutral initial-segment samplers.
- **Alternatives considered:** coerce lag values or reshape a flat transition buffer (rejected
  because malformed model schemas become different valid models); normalize arbitrary weight and
  transition inputs (rejected because constructors would silently change the caller's law); retain
  reachable zero rows (rejected because scoring and sampling would describe a defective chain);
  allow positive-lag null initial components (rejected because the initial segment then has no
  generative law); trust serialized sufficient statistics (rejected because mismatched workers can
  corrupt a model or fail after partially estimating children).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover lag exactness at every layer, state/component arity,
  probability normalization and ownership, matrix shape and zero-row reachability, positive-lag
  initial components, training controls, prior geometry, and mismatched sufficient statistics.
  Existing lag-zero density, sampling, estimation, terminal-state, engine, and conjugate-prior tests
  pass under the same contract.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (probability-law, schema, and distributed-statistic compatibility contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1245.

## D-0038 · Tree HMMs lower one canonical rooted-tree schema and decode a joint MAP state tree

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every tree-HMM observation is validated before numerical lowering. Node IDs are exact
  integers forming the dense set `0..n-1`; node 0 is the single root with parent -1; every other
  parent exists and has a smaller ID than its child. Input entry order is canonicalized by node ID,
  and both NumPy and Numba layouts derive emissions, edges, levels, leaves, and child counts from
  that same order. Encoded layouts report their tree-row count to the standard fitting pipeline.
  The NumPy Viterbi decoder performs an exact max-product postorder pass and parent-conditioned
  backtracking rather than combining local argmax indices. Parameter inputs are owned, and the
  per-level marginal cache keys the byte-exact current initial and transition arrays so in-place
  parameter mutation cannot return stale marginals.
- **Alternatives considered:** trust caller node positions (rejected because duplicate, missing, or
  cyclic topology becomes unsafe array indexing); repair arbitrary node IDs silently (rejected
  because output state positions and external provenance become ambiguous); sort only by parent
  (rejected because it can separate node emissions from their IDs); decode each node's marginal
  argmax (rejected because local optima need not form the joint MAP assignment); retain identity-only
  cache keys (rejected because NumPy array mutation preserves identity).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover shuffled entry order, duplicate/gapped/fractional IDs,
  missing and multiple roots, missing/forward/self parents, malformed entries, empty batches, and
  row counts on both layouts. Exact Viterbi results match a brute-force joint enumeration and the
  identity-transition regression. Source-array ownership and in-place cache invalidation are
  exercised directly. Existing tree length, impossible-evidence, encoder-equality, fused-EM, and
  NumPy/Numba parity tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (topology, exact-decoding, and parameter-cache correctness contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1246, MXR-080-1247, and MXR-080-1248.

## D-0039 · Scheduled HMMs preserve exact schedules, probability laws, and heterogeneous emissions

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Built-in phase counts and length boundaries are exact integers with their documented
  positive or non-negative bounds. Every schedule has an exact positive phase count, and every
  phase result used by scoring, sampling, or accumulation is an exact in-range integer. Initial
  laws and every transition row are owned finite non-negative probability simplexes with exact
  phase/state geometry. Scheduled estimators, factories, and accumulators carry a complete
  phase-by-state grid of emission estimators and factories; the scalar constructor remains a
  homogeneous broadcast convenience, while a distribution-derived estimator preserves each
  cell's actual family. Estimator controls and sufficient-statistic geometry are validated, and
  zero-count phases produce uniform initial laws and self-loop transition rows. Impossible batch
  evidence is rejected before any parent or child statistic is mutated.
- **Alternatives considered:** coerce phase counts, boundaries, states, or sampled lengths with
  `int` (rejected because fractional schemas become different valid models); normalize arbitrary
  input probability tables (rejected because construction would silently change the requested
  model); clone the first emission estimator across the grid (rejected because heterogeneous
  families and their statistics are erased); retain zero transition rows after empty-phase fitting
  (rejected because the fitted object would not be a probability law); detect impossible evidence
  only during per-row accumulation (rejected because valid prefix rows would already be committed).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover exact schedule inputs and outputs, probability shape,
  finiteness, normalization, ownership, emission-grid arity, invalid estimator controls,
  heterogeneous Gaussian/integer-categorical fitting, and zero-count laws. The existing brute-force
  forward, homogeneous parity, sampler, EM, serialization, prototype-estimator, and transactional
  impossible-evidence tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (schedule, stochastic-law, and heterogeneous-estimation contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1249, MXR-080-1250, and MXR-080-1251.

## D-0040 · Semi-supervised HMM state evidence is a scale-invariant likelihood potential

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A semi-supervised HMM has an explicit fixed uniform initial state law. Optional
  per-position state evidence is an owned finite non-negative likelihood-potential row, not a
  replacement initial distribution or a normalized categorical observation. Each row must contain
  positive evidence and is normalized to unit maximum. Consequently, row scaling cannot alter the
  score or posterior ratios, and an all-ones row is exactly “no constraint.” Ordinary and
  stopping-time scoring and posterior paths use the same `1 / n_states` initial law.
- **Alternatives considered:** omit the initial normalization (rejected because the distribution
  assigns total mass equal to the state count); normalize evidence rows to sum one (rejected because
  an all-ones no-constraint row would add a state-count-dependent evidence constant at every
  position); accept arbitrary unnormalized weights (rejected because score changes under
  semantically irrelevant row scaling); accept all-zero rows as impossible data (rejected because
  the side-information schema itself claims there is no possible latent state and should fail at
  validation).
- **Evidence:** exact contracts verify normalized mass for one and multiple positions, equality of
  absent and all-ones evidence, invariance to independent row scaling, broadcast ownership and
  normalization, typed rejection of negative/non-finite/all-zero rows, and parity of terminal
  scoring. Existing brute-force forward, one-hot clamp, shared-row, vector scoring, supervised and
  unsupervised estimation, sampling, terminal EM, and transactional impossible-evidence tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (probability-law and side-information semantics contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1252 and MXR-080-1253.

## D-0041 · Segmental HMMs reject invalid laws and preserve zero-count semantics

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Segmental HMM emissions form a non-empty owned state collection. Initial weights are
  an owned exact probability simplex, and transitions are an exact square finite non-negative
  matrix whose nonzero rows already sum to one. Reachable non-terminal zero rows are rejected;
  accepted unreachable or terminal zero rows are recorded and canonicalized as self-loops.
  Estimator pseudo-counts are finite and non-negative, terminal IDs and key arity are exact, and
  complete count and child-statistic geometry is validated before child estimation. A zero-count
  transition row estimates to a self-loop rather than a fabricated uniform row. Sampled lengths
  are exact non-negative integers. Host and engine E-steps reject an impossible batch before
  mutating any statistics, and the encoder exposes and validates its row count.
- **Alternatives considered:** normalize arbitrary constructor arrays (rejected because it silently
  changes the requested stochastic law); replace nonpositive rows uniformly (rejected because it
  invents transitions to unrelated states); retain zero rows in a fitted model (rejected because
  the result is defective); fabricate uniform gamma/xi for impossible evidence (rejected because no
  conditional law exists); trust distributed sufficient-statistic shape (rejected because child
  estimation could partially execute before a mismatch fails).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover empty emissions, initial and transition shape, normalization,
  finiteness, ownership, zero-row reachability and canonicalization, estimator controls and terminal
  IDs, sufficient-statistic geometry, zero-count estimation, transactional impossible evidence, and
  encoded rows. Existing brute-force density, scalar/vector parity, heterogeneous and sequence
  emission EM, engine scoring, terminal sampling/EM, and fused-EM tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (stochastic-law, latent-conditioning, and training-schema contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1254 plus the segmental impossible-evidence and encoded-row gaps exposed
  while verifying the HMM family.

## D-0042 · Structured HMM operators preserve stochastic laws and exact structural zeros

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every dense, low-rank, sparse, block-diagonal, and Kronecker transition is a finite,
  non-negative row-stochastic operator with exact state geometry. Sparse edge IDs are exact,
  in-range, unique integer pairs and every state has positive outgoing mass. Composite operators
  validate each child's materialized matrix and forward/backward algebra at construction. Initial
  state laws are owned exact probability simplexes. Zero-count transition rows retain their
  previous lawful row instead of becoming invalid or inventing new support. Scoring, Viterbi,
  final-state enumeration, and input-output decoding take exact probability logs, so zero initial
  or transition mass remains `-inf`. A request for a posterior or path under zero-probability
  evidence raises `ImpossibleEvidenceError` instead of constructing a synthetic conditional law;
  batch E-steps use the same transactional contract.
- **Alternatives considered:** normalize arbitrary constructor matrices (rejected because it
  silently changes the model); clip negative values (rejected because it hides an invalid law);
  permit duplicate sparse edges for implicit aggregation (rejected because serialized support and
  sufficient-statistic identity become ambiguous); add epsilon before logarithms (rejected because
  it revives forbidden paths); return all-zero or uniform posteriors for impossible evidence
  (rejected because no conditional distribution exists).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover matrix shape, finiteness, normalization and ownership;
  low-rank geometry; sparse edge and value schemas; composite-child algebra; accumulator arity;
  zero-count preservation; initial-law rejection; exact final-state enumeration; and typed
  impossible-evidence behavior for structured and input-output HMMs. Existing structured-HMM,
  read-out, enumeration, terminal-state, chunked-fit, and input-output tests pass under the same
  contract.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (stochastic-law, structural-support, and latent-conditioning contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1255 and MXR-080-1256.

## D-0043 · Structured-family fitting returns a validated model trajectory and receipt

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Structured, input-output, chunked, and explicit-duration fit helpers require exact
  positive iteration budgets, finite non-negative tolerances, non-empty sequence data, and an
  optional finite non-negative weight vector with positive total mass. Boolean and fractional
  integer controls are invalid. IOHMM observation and control batches have equal outer and inner
  lengths, and every control symbol is an exact in-range integer. Chunk and worker controls are
  exact, and overlap is smaller than the chunk. Each helper returns `HMMFitResult`, which remains
  backward-compatible with `(model, log_likelihood_trace)` unpacking while exposing immutable
  `HMMFitDiagnostics`. The trace contains the initial model and every accepted fitted model,
  including the returned final model. Exact EM rolls back an invalid or materially decreasing
  update; chunked approximate EM records non-monotonicity rather than making a false exact-EM
  claim. Transition sufficient statistics are copied on value export, restoration, tied-store
  adoption, and tied replacement.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain permissive `int` coercion (rejected because fractional
  budgets and input symbols become different requests); skip empty or malformed rows silently
  (rejected because the effective training set cannot be reconstructed); return only the
  pre-update scalar trace (rejected because it does not describe the returned model); raise after
  leaving a failed M-step installed (rejected because failure must be transactional); claim
  monotonicity for overlapping chunk approximations (rejected because approximate E-steps do not
  provide that exact guarantee); expose live nested statistic objects (rejected because distributed
  serialization and parameter tying would permit remote mutation of local counts).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover fit-result unpacking, final-score equality, weighted and
  zero-weight behavior, all common controls, empty data, chunk geometry, IO batch and symbol
  schemas, exact and approximate receipts, and nested transition-statistic ownership across
  value/from-value/key-store routes. Existing structured, input-output, explicit-duration,
  chunk-parallel, enumeration, terminal-state, and read-out tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (training-schema, convergence-evidence, and statistic-ownership contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1257.

## D-0044 · Explicit-duration HMMs use one normalized right-censored fixed-horizon law

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** An explicit-duration HMM sequence has an exogenous positive integer observation
  horizon. The generative process draws complete latent durations, while the observation stops at
  the horizon and therefore may right-censor the last duration. Scoring sums duration-survival mass
  for that last segment; sampling emits the same fixed-horizon prefix. Forward/backward, EM,
  duration statistics, state posteriors, Viterbi segmentation, enumeration, and the expanded
  structured-HMM representation all use this law. A censored E-step distributes the final
  segment's posterior across every actual duration at least as long as the observed portion. The
  remaining-duration HMM expansion has no final-state constraint because an arbitrary horizon may
  end in any remaining-duration substate. Constructor probability arrays are owned exact laws;
  segment transitions have an exact zero diagonal and at least two states.
- **Alternatives considered:** truncate only in the sampler (rejected because scoring and sampling
  then describe different distributions); require the last segment to end exactly at the horizon
  without normalization (rejected because fixed-length observation mass is less than one);
  condition exact-completion paths on the requested length (rejected because the missing
  length-normalizer would also require a different conditional-likelihood M-step); add an
  arbitrary termination probability (rejected because it changes the documented fixed-horizon
  API and adds an unidentified model parameter).
- **Evidence:** exhaustive finite-support checks sum the observation probability to one at horizons
  one through four. Focused contracts compare scorer probabilities with sampler frequencies,
  verify censored duration statistics, exercise a horizon wholly inside a deterministic duration,
  reject inexact sample lengths and silently repaired constructor laws, and prove likelihood parity
  with the remaining-duration structured-HMM expansion. The existing brute-force segmentation,
  EM duration recovery, posterior, Viterbi, enumeration, generic optimizer, and fit-receipt tests
  pass under the same semantics.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (fixed-horizon probability-law and censoring contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1258.

## D-0045 · Probabilistic circuits are validated topological probability DAGs

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A probabilistic circuit is a non-empty, rooted, fully reachable acyclic DAG whose
  flattened child IDs reference earlier nodes. Node kinds and tuple arities are explicit; product
  and sum child sets are non-empty and unique; leaf IDs are a dense range referenced exactly once.
  Leaf scopes contain unique exact in-range variable IDs. Product scopes are decomposable, sum
  scopes are smooth, and the root covers every declared variable. Sum builders accept an owned
  probability simplex; flattened nodes accept its exact log-simplex representation, including
  `-inf` for structural zero weights. Encoder equality compares leaf order, scope, distribution
  family, structural categorical support, and leaf-encoder compatibility. EM weights and
  pseudo-counts are finite and non-negative. Impossible positive-weight root evidence is rejected
  before any circuit or leaf statistic mutates; zero-weight evidence is ignored. Every effective
  sum count is validated, and a zero-count M-step retains the previous sum law.
- **Alternatives considered:** mark DFS nodes only after recursion (rejected because a cycle reaches
  Python recursion failure); treat unknown node kinds as sums (rejected because malformed schemas
  become executable); trust the tuple fast path (rejected because serialization is a trust
  boundary); normalize arbitrary or negative sum weights (rejected because construction silently
  changes the law or creates NaN); compare encoders by scope alone (rejected because payloads can
  cross incompatible leaf semantics); convert impossible flow to zero responsibilities (rejected
  because the observation silently disappears from training).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover builder and tuple cycles/topology, empty and unknown nodes,
  exact scopes, disconnected graphs, leaf IDs, child ranges and duplication, log-simplex weights,
  input ownership, encoder semantic equality and row counts, transactional impossible evidence,
  zero-weight rows, weight and pseudo-count domains, malformed effective counts, and zero-count
  retention. Existing float/LNS scoring, zero-code propagation, decomposability, smoothness,
  shared-DAG, sampling, and EM recovery tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (circuit-schema, encoder-compatibility, and EM-evidence contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1259, MXR-080-1260, and MXR-080-1261.

## D-0046 · PPCA parameters are immutable snapshots with exact event geometry

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A probabilistic-PCA model owns finite non-empty mean and loading arrays, a nonzero
  latent width, and a finite positive noise variance. Public `w`, `mu`, and `inv_covar` accessors
  return snapshots, while `sigma2` is read-only, so callers cannot desynchronize displayed
  parameters from the cached Woodbury inverse and log determinant. Scalar density accepts exactly
  one finite `(d,)` event. Vectorized, backend, encoder, and accumulator routes accept exactly a
  finite `(n,d)` matrix. Transform accepts either form explicitly and returns `(q,)` or `(q,n)`.
  Encoders carry the event dimension in their compatibility identity. Accumulator dimensions,
  weights, serialized moments, and estimator dimensions use the same exact schema.
- **Alternatives considered:** copy only constructor inputs (rejected because mutating the public
  model array would still stale the cache); recompute the covariance inverse on every score
  (rejected because it discards PPCA's intended cached Woodbury advantage); reshape or broadcast
  arbitrary inputs (rejected because one scalar could become a two-dimensional event); validate
  only the ordinary NumPy route (rejected because backend and accumulator paths could still train
  or score a different event schema).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts mutate constructor sources and every returned public array,
  reject parameter reassignment, compare the score before and after attempted mutation, exercise
  invalid and non-finite parameters, and cover valid and invalid geometry on density, transform,
  vectorized, backend, encoder, and accumulator routes. Existing multivariate-normal parity,
  Woodbury inverse/log-determinant, Torch parity, string reconstruction, sampling, and closed-form
  covariance recovery tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (parameter/cache coherence and event-schema contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1262.

## D-0047 · Hierarchical-normal EM uses exact weighted size-bucket statistics

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Each group contributes its finite non-empty vector statistics multiplied by its
  finite non-negative frequency/importance weight. Zero-weight groups contribute nothing. Rather
  than buffering every group, the accumulator stores four weighted moments for each distinct exact
  positive group size: group mass, mean sum, squared-mean sum, and within-group SSE. This
  representation is algebraically sufficient for the exact empirical-Bayes E- and M-steps and is
  bounded by a configurable maximum number of distinct sizes; exceeding the budget fails before
  mutation. Model `mu` is finite and `tau`/`sigma` are finite positive. Groups, encoded statistics,
  posterior means, shrinkage queries, estimator controls, distributed statistics, and sampler sizes
  use exact schemas. Empty training statistics return an explicit neutral `(0,1,1)` unidentified
  model. Every fitted model carries an immutable receipt with identifiability, convergence,
  termination reason, accepted marginal-likelihood trajectory, parameter delta, size-bucket count,
  and effective group and observation weights.
- **Alternatives considered:** append each group and multiply only during estimation (rejected
  because memory remains unbounded and distributed payloads scale with row count); ignore weights
  (rejected because exclusions and replication semantics fail); aggregate all group sizes together
  (rejected because posterior shrinkage depends nonlinearly on size); silently discard excess size
  buckets (rejected because the fitted dataset changes); take means or divide by zero for no data
  (rejected because it creates NaN parameters without provenance).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts prove zero-weight exclusion, equality with explicit weighted
  replication, compression of 1,000 groups into two buckets, transactional budget enforcement,
  strict weight/model/group/posterior/encoder/control/statistic domains, correct empty encoding,
  explicit no-data behavior, and a finite monotone objective receipt. Existing population parameter
  recovery, partial-pooling RMSE, shrinkage, and group-posterior tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (weighted-statistic, bounded-memory, and empirical-Bayes receipt contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1263 and MXR-080-1264.

## D-0048 · Finite IBP probabilities and feature identifiers use exact schemas

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Finite-IBP feature dimensions are positive exact integers, and sparse feature
  identifiers are non-boolean exact integers in range before any numeric cast. Plug-in feature
  probabilities are always represented in a numerically effective open unit interval. A requested
  probability floor smaller than machine epsilon is promoted to machine epsilon, including a zero
  floor, because no smaller symmetric floor is representable below and above one in binary64.
  User-supplied endpoint probabilities are therefore smoothed into a valid Beta posterior rather
  than constructing a zero Beta shape. Explicit Beta parameters retain their positive finite
  variational shapes, while their derived plug-in means receive the same open-interval protection.
- **Alternatives considered:** cast sparse IDs to integers (rejected because fractional IDs silently
  select a different feature); retain a nominal `1e-128` symmetric floor (rejected because
  `1 - 1e-128` rounds to one); represent deterministic plug-in Bernoulli features beside an
  improper Beta variational factor (rejected because expected-log and global variational terms
  would need a separate measure and entropy contract).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover zero and one feature probabilities on every binary row,
  scalar and encoded plug-in and expected-log scoring, invalid probability floors, fractional,
  non-finite, boolean, and string sparse IDs, rejected inexact dimensions, and accepted NumPy exact
  integer identifiers. Existing posterior-update, pseudocount, scalar/vector parity, accumulator,
  sampling, reconstruction, enumeration, backend, Fisher, and sampler-accuracy contracts pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (finite-IBP parameter and observation schema).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1265 and MXR-080-1266.

## D-0049 · Integer PLSI observations are canonical normalized count vectors

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** An integer-PLSI observation is one exact in-range document ID and a canonical sparse
  bag of unique, sorted, exact in-range word IDs with positive exact-integer counts. Duplicate word
  entries are combined and zero entries removed before scoring or training. Its probability is the
  document prior times the length probability times the normalized multinomial count-vector law,
  including `log(n!) - sum_w log(c_w!)`. Scalar, vectorized, backend, fused-likelihood, sampler, and
  finite-support enumerator routes use this same law. Enumeration without a proper length law fails
  closed because joining one conditional multinomial law for every length has infinite total mass.
  Empty bags are valid when the length model permits zero and have an explicit zero component-score
  vector. Constructor matrices are owned finite nonempty simplexes with matching geometry.
  Dimensions, encoded payloads, weights, pseudo-counts, and sufficient statistics have exact
  validated schemas. Positive-weight impossible evidence fails before mutation; zero-weight
  evidence is ignored.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep the token-product objective (rejected because it is neither the
  sampler's grouped-output law nor a normalized count-vector distribution); treat duplicate sparse
  entries as distinct (rejected because scalar NumPy advanced indexing loses repeated updates);
  enumerate every conditional length without a length law (rejected because total mass is
  infinite); silently normalize malformed parameter arrays (rejected because construction would
  change the stated model).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts prove the complete length-two bag support has probabilities
  `(0.25, 0.5, 0.25)` and total mass one, scalar/vector/backend/enumerator score parity, duplicate
  canonicalization and scalar/vector EM equality, strict matrix, ID, count, dimension, weight, and
  statistic schemas, defined empty-document component geometry, and transactional impossible
  evidence. Existing enumeration, engine-resident E-step, fused-EM, and parity tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (integer-PLSI probability-law and observation-schema contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1267, MXR-080-1268, and MXR-080-1269.

## D-0050 · Hidden-association outputs use one canonical grouped-count law

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Generic and integer hidden-association observations are pairs of canonical sparse
  count vectors. Counts are finite non-negative exact integers; duplicate entries are combined and
  zeros removed. Integer IDs are exact and within their configured supports. A nonempty emitted bag
  requires positive conditioning mass. The emitted probability is the normalized multinomial
  count-vector law, including `log(n!) - sum_y log(c_y!)`, multiplied by the given-bag and emitted-
  length laws. Scalar, encoded, backend, fused-likelihood, sampler, and integer finite-support
  enumerator routes use that contract. Generic enumeration fails closed until conditional
  distributions expose a finite support manifest; the former token-product enumerator did not
  enumerate the normalized law. Integer state-emission and conditioning matrices are owned finite
  row simplexes with matching state geometry, and `alpha` is finite in the closed unit interval.
  Dimensions, encoders, weights, estimator statistics, and controls use exact schemas. Impossible
  positive-weight evidence fails before mutation; zero-weight evidence is ignored.
- **Alternatives considered:** preserve expanded ordered-sequence scores for grouped sampler output
  (rejected because multiple ordered sequences collapse to one returned observation); convert
  impossible denominators to zero responsibilities (rejected because training silently discards
  evidence); define a partial background law for an empty conditioning bag (rejected because its
  emitted probabilities sum to `alpha`, not one, unless the model is separately redefined);
  continue generic best-first multiset enumeration (rejected because the multinomial coefficient
  breaks its monotone token-product frontier and arbitrary child supports have no finite manifest).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts show both families' two-trial binary supports have probabilities
  `(0.25, 0.5, 0.25)` and total mass one, with scalar/encoded/backend and Numba/non-Numba parity.
  They cover duplicate canonicalization, fractional, negative, non-finite, and out-of-range data,
  empty conditioning, matrix/simplex/alpha validation, exact integer enumeration parity, generic
  fail-closed enumeration, and transactional impossible evidence. Existing gate-posterior,
  engine-resident, fused-likelihood parity, and empty-input regression tests pass under the same
  schema.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (hidden-association grouped-output and evidence contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1270 and MXR-080-1271.

## D-0051 · Dirac-length mixtures include both exact closed boundaries

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A Dirac-length model uses the closed mixture interval `p in [0,1]`: `p=0` is the
  exact point mass at `v`, while `p=1` is exactly the child length law. The Dirac location and every
  observation are exact non-boolean integers before any array conversion; encoded observations use
  signed 64-bit storage without truncation. Scalar, vectorized, backend, stacked, sampler, posterior,
  enumerator, accumulator, factory, and estimator routes preserve those boundaries. An observation
  outside both active component supports has log probability `-inf` and an all-zero posterior.
  Positive-weight impossible evidence fails before mutation; zero-weight impossible evidence is
  ignored. Random scalar and vectorized initialization both apply the complete finite non-negative
  observation weight. `fixed_p` accepts and preserves both zero and one. Probability priors,
  pseudo-counts, weights, component statistics, sample sizes, and encoded row counts are validated.
  Shared sparse-count contracts reject numeric strings as identifiers and boolean weight vectors.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep the open lower boundary (rejected because a pure point mass is a
  valid identifiable model); replace an impossible posterior with the component prior (rejected
  because it fabricates training mass for zero-probability evidence); retain int32 lowering
  (rejected because valid large integer support values can wrap); treat `fixed_p=0` as absent
  (rejected because it changes an explicit fixed model); apply random responsibilities without the
  observation weight (rejected because scalar and vectorized initialization then fit different
  weighted datasets).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover exact `p=0` and `p=1` densities, sampling, posterior,
  vectorized scoring, and enumeration; scalar/vector initialization equality at weight ten; fixed
  zero estimation; lossy location and observation coercions; invalid probabilities, weights, and
  statistics; and transactional scalar/vector impossible evidence. Existing overlap, finite and
  infinite enumeration, scalar/vector posterior, engine E-step, fused-likelihood, and no-Dirac-row
  tests pass. The PLSI, IBP, and association contract suites pass after the shared coercion
  tightening.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (Dirac-length boundary, support, and evidence contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1272 and MXR-080-1273.

## D-0052 · LDA variational inference is finite, validated, and fail-closed

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Plain LDA requires at least one topic, a positive finite alpha entry for every topic,
  positive finite convergence thresholds, and exact positive iteration budgets. Its grouped corpus
  schema preserves document-row counts; validates exact ordered document IDs, positive finite token
  counts, warm-start geometry, and finite non-negative document weights; and rejects lossy or boolean
  coercions. A token rejected by every topic gives its document an ELBO of `-inf` and zero topic
  responsibilities. Positive-weight impossible evidence fails before any statistic is mutated, while
  zero-weight evidence adds no training mass. Document coordinate ascent and alpha optimization have
  finite budgets and immutable termination records; exhausting a budget or decreasing the Dirichlet
  objective raises with that record instead of returning an unmarked final iterate. Estimated models
  retain their alpha-optimization diagnostics.
- **Alternatives considered:** floor undefined responsibilities and topic probabilities to a tiny
  positive number (rejected because that fabricates evidence outside every topic's support); silently
  accept a capped variational iterate (rejected because callers cannot distinguish convergence from
  budget exhaustion); keep the unbounded alpha fixed point (rejected because malformed controls or
  pathological statistics can make fitting nonterminating); treat flattened token count as encoded
  corpus row count (rejected because it violates document-level weighting and distributed row
  conservation).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover model geometry, grouped-count schemas, encoded IDs/counts/warm
  starts, row conservation, impossible and zero-weight evidence, transactional host updates, invalid
  weights, bounded gamma failure, and monotone bounded alpha receipts. Existing LDA length fitting,
  posterior, empty-document, backend scoring/E-step, and fused-EM parity tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (LDA evidence and inner-optimization contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1274 and MXR-080-1275.

## D-0053 · Labeled LDA defines one canonical joint-law contract

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A labeled-LDA observation has a nonempty canonical label set: label IDs are exact
  non-boolean integers, in range for the alpha matrix, sorted, and deduplicated before encoding.
  Every alpha entry is positive and finite, and the matrix has exactly one column per topic.
  Conditional models omit `set_dist` and `len_dist`; when either structural law is supplied, its log
  probability is part of host, backend, and fused ELBO scoring. Both structural laws remain fixed
  through topic/alpha estimation, so estimation cannot silently discard them. Sampling requires both,
  validates their outputs, and no longer resamples empty label sets into a different conditional law.
  Topic or structural evidence with zero joint probability scores `-inf`; a positive-weight E-step
  rejects it before mutation. Independent and coupled alpha optimizers have validated finite budgets,
  monotone objective traces, explicit failure records, and never publish a failed iterate. Sparse
  underidentified updates retain the previous accepted alpha with a non-convergence receipt.
- **Alternatives considered:** preserve duplicate labels as repeated alpha-row weight (rejected because
  the public object is a set and repetition changed the model); keep scoring conditional while
  sampling a joint model (rejected because scoring and generation then describe different laws);
  re-estimate structural laws without adding their statistics to the public accumulator schema
  (rejected because hidden mutable estimator state would break distributed folding); silently return
  the last coupled-alpha iterate (rejected because budget exhaustion is not convergence); repeatedly
  sample until a nonempty label set appears (rejected because it renormalizes `set_dist` without
  declaring that conditioning).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover alpha geometry and controls; empty, fractional, boolean,
  string, negative, and out-of-range labels; duplicate canonicalization; document-row conservation;
  exact label-set and length score contributions; host/backend parity; fixed-law preservation through
  estimation; topic and structural impossible evidence; transactional updates; and bounded coupled
  optimization failure. Existing independent/coupled alpha, engine E-step/scoring, legacy
  scalar/vector, keyed-statistic, and fused-EM tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (labeled-LDA observation and joint-law contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1276 and MXR-080-1277.

## D-0054 · Attention leaves preserve probability support, weights, and fit state

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Responsibility, chained, variational-embedding, and variational-multihop attention
  distributions own finite arrays, require compatible geometry, normalized emission rows, normalized
  position priors where applicable, and positive finite gate variances. Their encoders accept only
  exact non-boolean in-range identifiers and fixed positive context geometry. Structural zero
  probabilities remain zero and score `-inf`; positive-weight impossible evidence fails before
  accumulator mutation, while zero-weight evidence adds no mass. Every variational objective and
  embedding-gradient contribution is multiplied by its observation weight before reduction. The
  ordinary variational sampler uses the posterior-mean plug-in law used by scoring; a separate
  posterior-predictive sampler draws an independent embedding table for every returned event.
  Variational Adam means, log variances, moments, iteration, seed, algorithm, and schema version form
  an explicit immutable optimizer-state artifact carried by the model and serialized through
  sufficient statistics. Estimators are stateless functions of those statistics, and merging rejects
  statistics from different optimizer generations.
- **Alternatives considered:** clip zero emissions or priors to tiny positive values (rejected because
  it changes model support and fabricates gradients); weight only closed-form emission counts
  (rejected because it optimizes a different variational objective); share one posterior embedding
  draw across a requested batch (rejected because the returned events are not iid); keep Adam and RNG
  counters on estimator instances (rejected because retries, distributed folds, and call ordering
  then change the fit without serialized provenance).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover malformed simplexes and variance geometry; fractional,
  negative, and out-of-range identifiers; structural position and target zeros; transactional
  impossible evidence; zero-versus-unit weighted gradient equivalence; plug-in sampler invariance to
  posterior variance; batched-versus-sequential posterior-predictive equality; optimizer-state
  serialization, restoration, and estimator call-order independence. Existing attention mechanics,
  forward-backward enumeration, variational likelihood-improvement, and variational two-hop learning
  tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (attention probability, evidence, sampling, and fit-state contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1278 through MXR-080-1283.

## D-0055 · Heterogeneous PCFGs certify almost-sure finite termination

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A heterogeneous PCFG exposed as a probability distribution must terminate in a
  finite derivation almost surely from its start symbol. Construction builds the reachable
  multitype branching process, computes its mean-progeny spectral radius and strongly connected
  classes, rejects supercritical processes and critical singular classes with a deterministic
  infinite spine, and retains an immutable termination certificate on accepted models. Reachable
  nonterminals without positive productions are invalid. Induced grammars require initial terminal
  mass in `[0.5, 1]` and project learned per-parent branching mass to at most `0.5`, preserving
  zero-pruned rules while ensuring at most one expected child. If pruning removes every production
  or all terminating support needed by a supercritical parent, fitting fails instead of silently
  restoring prior rules. Rule counts, weights, `nobs`, pseudo-counts, pruning controls, recursion
  limits, keyed state, and statistic geometry are validated before use. Statistic values own their
  arrays; tied emission accumulators are cloned on adoption and replacement. Positive-weight
  impossible sequences fail before any PCFG or child statistic mutation. The flattened encoder
  reports document rows rather than terminal rows.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on sampler depth limits (rejected because a runtime cap does not
  normalize a subprobability law); use only a spectral-radius threshold (rejected because a
  critical deterministic spine has radius one but never terminates); condition sampling on eventual
  termination (rejected because scoring and estimation do not define that renormalized law);
  silently restore a parent's prior after pruning removes every rule (rejected because the requested
  pruning result then depends on an undocumented fallback); share live child accumulator lists
  across tied sites (rejected because later local updates corrupt other sites).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts reject pure recursive, supercritical, deterministic-spine, invalid
  control, malformed count, and empty-pruning cases; accept and certify acyclic and nonsingular
  critical grammars; verify the induced properness projection; prove returned statistics and tied
  emission state do not alias; and enforce transactional impossible evidence and invalid-weight
  failures. Existing scalar/vector scoring, sampling, EM, heterogeneous emission, recursive bounded
  enumeration, induced learning, engine parity, and high-level grammar-helper tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (PCFG probability-law, fit, pruning, and statistic contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1284 and MXR-080-1285.

## D-0056 · Quantized HMMs preserve exact encodings and publish fit receipts

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Quantized-HMM exponents are exact, non-boolean int64 values; `-1` is the only
  structural-zero sentinel, and finite exponents obey the declared cap. Models own their exponent
  arrays. Emission levels are nonempty, hashable, finite when numeric, unique under equality, and
  retain declared order followed by a deterministic order for newly observed values. Estimator
  controls, sufficient-statistic geometry, non-negative finite counts, per-state emission mass, and
  global hidden-state responsibility mass are validated before fitting. Bounded theta optimization
  fails closed on an unsuccessful or non-finite result. Every successful fit carries an immutable,
  versioned receipt identifying all restart iteration counts and termination reasons, the selected
  restart and its convergence state, optimizer evaluations, objective, theta, and symmetry splits.
  The non-monotone collapsed-state escape heuristic is explicit opt-in rather than default behavior.
- **Alternatives considered:** cast exponents to integers (rejected because it silently changes the
  probability law); deduplicate equality-colliding levels through a categorical map (rejected
  because columns then lose their declared meaning); clip or normalize malformed counts (rejected
  because invalid distributed statistics would become a plausible model); publish the best
  coordinate candidate without its termination state (rejected because callers cannot distinguish
  convergence from a cycle or exhausted budget); enable symmetry splitting by default (rejected
  because its deliberate non-monotone perturbations can destabilize an otherwise valid EM fit).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts cover fractional, boolean, out-of-range, and invalid-sentinel
  exponents; level duplication, equality collision, non-finite values, and ownership; invalid
  controls and count geometry; local and global responsibility conservation; deterministic level
  order; fixed and optimized convergence receipts; and injected scalar-optimizer failure. Existing
  quantized-HMM parameterization, dense-law parity, sampling, enumeration, estimation, stationary
  initialization, structural-zero, keyed-statistic, and explicit collapsed-state escape tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (quantized-HMM encoding, statistics, and fit-provenance contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1286 and MXR-080-1287.

## D-0057 · Determinized machines score and sample one proper stochastic law

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every public deterministic-machine state owns disjoint accept and transition maps
  whose positive finite log weights sum to one. Materially unnormalized rows, invalid destinations,
  malformed edges, and symbol-role collisions are rejected; rounding-scale residuals alone are
  normalized away so scoring and sampling use the same row law. Every state reachable from the
  initial state must retain a path to positive accept mass, certifying almost-sure finite
  termination. General-HMM determinization rationalizes each transition, initial, and emission row
  and then normalizes that row exactly before belief expansion. Quantized determinization likewise
  validates exact positive row mass. State and denominator limits and sampler sizes are exact
  non-boolean integers with their documented positive or non-negative domains.
- **Alternatives considered:** let the sampler normalize arbitrary machine rows (rejected because it
  samples a different law from the one scored); reject every floating row not bit-exactly normalized
  (rejected because logarithm/exponential roundoff is unavoidable); rationalize each float
  independently without row normalization (rejected because the resulting exact weights need not
  form a probability row); rely on a runtime sampling limit for nonterminating classes (rejected
  because it does not turn a subprobability distribution into a proper law).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts reject weight-two and deficient-mass rows, malformed graph
  geometry, invalid destinations, symbol collisions, non-finite weights, reachable nonterminating
  classes, invalid controls, and invalid sampler sizes; verify graph ownership, roundoff-only
  normalization, scored/sample-law agreement, and exact normalization after deliberately coarse
  rationalization. Existing quantized and general HMM determinization, duplicate-free enumeration,
  structural seek, finite-expansion failure, and terminated-sampling tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (determinized-machine probability and termination contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1288.

## D-0058 · Sparse-mixture reduction distinguishes limits from model identity

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Sparse scoring and Gaussian reduction require a positive exact non-boolean component
  limit. Weight geometry must match component geometry and contain only the finite non-negative
  values appropriate to the operation. Exact duplicate collapse groups components only when both
  their fully qualified family and canonical safe serialized state match; a component without a
  canonical serialization is conservatively left separate. Gaussian reduction validates the
  original family before discarding zero-weight components, removes those zero-mass components
  exactly, and only moment-matches positive-mass pairs.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep Python slice semantics for zero or negative limits (rejected
  because they produce vacuous or surprising certification brackets); use `str(component)` as an
  identity key (rejected because display text is neither injective nor a parameter contract); hash
  serialized state without retaining family identity (rejected because a collision or shared state
  schema could merge different laws); attempt to introspect un-serializable objects (rejected because
  a false-positive merge changes the law); let the Gaussian merge encounter zero total pair weight
  (rejected because zero-weight components can be removed exactly first).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts reject zero, negative, fractional, boolean, and non-finite limits
  for both sparse scoring and Gaussian reduction; distinguish two parameter-distinct Gaussian laws
  forced to share display text; merge independently constructed canonical duplicates exactly;
  remove multiple zero-weight Gaussians without division by zero; and reject mismatched weight
  geometry. Existing certified-bracket, bound-tightening, exact fallback, density-supremum, exact
  duplicate collapse, heterogeneous-family rejection, and global-moment-preservation tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (sparse-mixture certification and reduction contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1289.

## D-0059 · The latent namespace is an explicit lazy compatibility boundary

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** `mixle.stats.latent` owns an ordered, immutable manifest for its stable 0.8.0
  distributions, estimators, samplers, enumerators, encoders, accumulators/factories, schedules,
  transition operators, diagnostics, receipts, errors, and high-level helpers. Every entry records
  its source module and resolves lazily; duplicate public names fail at import rather than silently
  shadowing one another. `mixle.stats` resolves every focused latent symbol to the identical object.
  Its curated star-import surface includes user-facing families, operators, schedules, diagnostics,
  and helpers while the established suffix policy keeps sampler, encoder, and accumulator plumbing
  directly importable but out of `__all__`. The repository API artifact records both package
  surfaces.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain an empty focused initializer and document internal module paths
  (rejected because there is no auditable compatibility boundary); eagerly import every latent
  implementation (rejected because heavy optional numerical kernels would tax unrelated imports);
  export every top-level non-underscore function automatically (rejected because low-level kernels
  and accidental names would become API); expose integration plumbing only from implementation
  modules (rejected because framework authors need stable imports); allow duplicate generic helper
  names to overwrite one another (rejected because package lookup would depend on declaration order).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts verify manifest order, uniqueness, status/source metadata, lazy
  resolution, identity through the consolidated namespace, required new family/operator/receipt
  coverage, and the curated plumbing split. The repository-wide public API manifest regenerates
  deterministically and all of its drift, resolution, maturity, and artifact-integrity tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (latent public compatibility boundary).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1290.

## D-0060 · Latent estimators conserve effective sample mass

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every latent accumulator accepts only finite, non-negative observation weights and
  every mass-aware estimator validates its declared effective sample before fitting. Posterior,
  occupancy, segment, component, topic, leaf, length, and keyed conditional statistics may neither
  create mass nor silently substitute an unrelated row count. Child estimators receive the mass
  actually assigned to that child rather than `None`, the outer row count, or an implementation
  buffer size. Impossible evidence fails transactionally; a family may leave mass unassigned only
  where its explicit contract records that policy. Versioned statistics carry the added mass
  receipts while preserving the established tuple iteration of gated mixtures, association
  models, probabilistic circuits, and heterogeneous PCFGs.
- **Alternatives considered:** continue treating `nobs` as optional metadata (rejected because
  pseudo-counts, priors, and nested estimators then use incompatible sample sizes); infer child mass
  from sufficient-statistic shapes (rejected because shape is not statistical mass); pass the
  parent mass to every child (rejected because it multiplies evidence); and silently normalize or
  clip malformed distributed statistics (rejected because corruption would become a plausible
  fitted model).
- **Evidence:** cross-family contracts reject Boolean, negative, non-finite, length-mismatched, and
  mass-creating weights; verify explicit unassigned-mass receipts; and prove posterior mass reaches
  semi-supervised HMM emissions, tree-HMM topics and length laws, gated experts, and probabilistic
  circuit leaves. Focused mixture, HMM, tree, attention, PPCA, association, PLSI, LDA, labeled-LDA,
  grammar, circuit, and structured-HMM contract suites pass under the 30-second local-test policy.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (latent effective-sample and nested-estimator contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1291.

## D-0061 · Publication is bound to one signed and approved release candidate

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** A published GitHub Release is a publication request, not sufficient authorization.
  Before building, the workflow requires an annotated tag equal to `v` plus the package version,
  GitHub-verified tag-signature evidence, and a commit identical to the current
  `release/0.8.0` tip. A version-controlled policy names every required hosted check; the latest
  check run of each name must be completed successfully on that full SHA. The workflow checks out
  that immutable SHA, fingerprints both artifacts, retains the selected check-run IDs and candidate
  identity, re-verifies downloaded hashes, and attaches the evidence to the GitHub Release before
  entering the protected Trusted Publishing environment. Manual dispatch can build but never
  publish.
- **Alternatives considered:** trust the release event (rejected because a stale or unrelated tag can
  trigger it); accept branch ancestry without exact-tip identity (rejected because unchecked commits
  could follow the approved candidate); trust a prior successful run by name alone (rejected because
  it may describe another SHA or be superseded by a failed rerun); and retain workflow artifacts only
  (rejected because their expiration is shorter than the release's evidentiary lifetime).
- **Evidence:** focused negative controls reject missing checks, other-commit checks, in-progress
  checks, failed latest reruns, malformed policy records, and partial check sets. Static workflow
  contracts pin version, annotated-tag/signature, exact ancestry, artifact-digest, retained-evidence,
  protected-environment, and verify-before-upload ordering.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0073
- **Expiration:** 0.8.0 publication; the generic publication invariant remains permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1292 and MXR-080-1293.

## D-0062 · Reproduction is an installed, artifact-bound, fail-closed protocol

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The wheel ships `mixle-reproduce`; the checkout script is only a compatibility
  wrapper. Release reproduction retains the exact wheel, verifies its SHA-256 and installed
  name/version, requires embedded clean-source commit/tree/content provenance, verifies installed
  files against `RECORD`, binds every measurement to a required value or tolerance, records
  per-check verdicts, and exits nonzero unless artifact identity and every check pass. Explicit
  development-source mode resolves Git against the repository containing the module, never the
  caller's working directory.
- **Alternatives considered:** print raw measurements and leave comparison to the caller (rejected
  because automation then certifies known failures); infer the original wheel hash after pip deletes
  the archive (impossible from ordinary installed metadata, so the protocol retains the wheel);
  identify provenance through ambient Git (rejected because an unrelated repository becomes false
  evidence); and require a separate source checkout (rejected because it can drift from the installed
  package).
- **Evidence:** focused tests prove deterministic successful checks, false Boolean and wrong-family
  failures, nonzero process status, repository-root binding, clean versus dirty embedded provenance,
  wheel hash capture, console-entry packaging, and strict JSON receipts. A bounded wheel build
  confirms that both the entry point and generated provenance record are present.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (artifact and reproduction evidence contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1294, MXR-080-1295, and MXR-080-1296.

## D-0063 · The reproduction bundle is an executable content-addressed closure

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Bundle v2 binds each entry's argv, bounded configuration, 30-second execution
  limit, executable digest, dataset record where applicable, and exact-text or structured-JSON
  expectation. Its global closure hashes the package definition, numerical dependency lock,
  environment contract, dataset license/integrity record, and runner. Every local entry executes
  under the focused gate; the network entry executes in the required optional hosted run. The
  publication workflow retains all local receipts, downloads the Banking77 receipt from that exact
  hosted run, and attaches them with the candidate/check/wheel records to the release. The checked
  bundle specifies these final dynamic records rather than attempting the impossible self-reference
  of embedding the commit that contains its own literal commit hash.
- **Decision:** BANKING77 is loaded from the immutable source-data commit
  `9d081458ff52e53cf7e848f414e6e9344e4e6696`; its two authoritative CSV SHA-256 values, row
  counts, and text/category schema are verified before training. Its resolved CC-BY-4.0 record
  names the authoritative metadata revision, license, citation, source revision, splits, and file
  digests. Download unavailability is distinct from an integrity failure, and integrity failures
  cannot become hosted skips.
- **Decision:** A benchmark is release-current only when its exact package version, full 40-digit
  approved commit, and 64-digit artifact SHA-256 all match. Missing, abbreviated, malformed,
  unrelated, or merely same-minor provenance fails closed; unbound local measurements remain
  visibly `unverified`.
- **Alternatives considered:** refresh script hashes only (rejected because it pins neither data,
  environment, configuration, nor result); run one convenient example (rejected because broken
  later entries still pass); accept a nonempty license string (rejected because placeholders look
  complete); load a moving dataset alias (rejected because source bytes can change); and treat a
  matching minor version as benchmark provenance (rejected because many candidate commits and
  artifacts share it).
- **Evidence:** four local entries reproduce and match their complete output hashes plus human-readable
  numeric invariants in 13.5 seconds total; negative contracts reject closure drift, invalid tiers,
  over-budget entries, unresolved placeholders, malformed output, and missing candidate records.
  Dataset contracts pin both source files and split geometry. Benchmark contracts reject missing,
  shortened, wrong-commit, wrong-artifact, and wrong-version records. The focused tranche reports
  20 passing tests.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0133
- **Expiration:** 0.8.0 numerical pins and Banking77 revisions; bundle and provenance policies are
  permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1297, MXR-080-1298, MXR-080-1299, MXR-080-1300, and MXR-080-1301.

## D-0064 · Benchmarks time fitting and fail closed on scientific invalidity

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Benchmark adapters separate preparation, fitting, and evaluation. Only fitting is
  timed, every warmup and measured repetition is evaluated, and non-finite or non-reproducible
  results fail. Scientific parity requires one successful Mixle result and at least one successful
  independent implementation; Mixle is compared directly with each independent result. Missing,
  failed, or mismatched implementations abort the command before a report is written. Output
  directories are created and write-probed before expensive CPU or GPU work starts.
- **Alternatives considered:** accept agreement between any two implementations (rejected because
  Mixle could be the failed third result); time preprocessing and evaluation (rejected because
  adapter-specific overhead ceases to measure fit cost); validate only the final repetition
  (rejected because unstable fits disappear from the receipt); and create output paths after compute
  (rejected because a predictable filesystem error can discard expensive work).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts exercise failed and mismatched references, missing independent
  evidence, repeated-result validation, fit-only timing, a real pre-encoded Mixle fit, and early
  output-path failure.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (benchmark scientific-validity contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1302, MXR-080-1303, and MXR-080-1304.

## D-0065 · Local smoke evidence is collection-light and centrally classified

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The sub-30-second local gate runs only explicit test files from a versioned manifest;
  it does not discover the full suite and then deselect by marker. Its runner rejects directories,
  globs, repository escapes, duplicate entries, and budgets above 30 seconds, and CI retains its
  receipt as a required release check. Test modules may not assign themselves to the ``fast`` tier:
  the central registry is the sole authority and every registry filename must resolve to a collected
  ``*_test.py`` or ``test_*.py`` module at any test-directory depth. The optional-import guard is
  derived from every project extra through an explicit distribution-to-import map and recursively
  checks executable module scope while recognizing only real import-error and type-checking guards.
- **Alternatives considered:** broad ``pytest -m smoke`` (rejected because deselection follows
  collection); maintain a partial hand-written heavy-import list (rejected because extras drift);
  permit file-local ``fast`` marks (rejected because they can override central retriage); and trust
  stale registry filenames (rejected because silently dead policy is not enforcement).
- **Evidence:** the actual manifest runner completes under its hard local budget; focused contracts
  reject globs, escapes, over-budget execution, dependency-map drift, nested unguarded imports,
  self-applied fast markers, and nonexistent registry entries.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (test-selection and optional-import governance).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1305, MXR-080-1306, MXR-080-1307, and MXR-080-1308.

## D-0066 · Persistence compatibility is versioned, profiled, and explicitly narrower than registration

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every newly serialized object carries state schema version 1. The manifest records exact
  base and full generation profiles, each with a required import inventory and per-type version,
  stability, codec, fields, and migration metadata. Profiles are generated independently; a partial
  environment cannot overwrite another profile. Runtime registration continues to support all
  discovered types, but cross-version persistence is stable only for Gaussian, Poisson, Exponential,
  Categorical, and Mixture. Those five have explicit state fields, reviewed ``0->1`` migrations,
  digest-bound 0.7 fixtures, complete fixture-to-stable-schema coverage, and a nested Mixture/Gaussian
  composition. Unversioned provisional objects fail with actionable migration guidance.
- **Alternatives considered:** declare arbitrary ``__dict__`` state stable (rejected because a field
  rename changes behavior without changing the type ID); let a base environment rewrite a full
  manifest (rejected because missing types look deleted); claim 381 compatible schemas from five
  fixtures (rejected because registration is not compatibility evidence); and remove provisional
  serialization (rejected because same-release round trips remain useful).
- **Evidence:** exact 368-type base and 382-type full profiles regenerate and check in the declared
  environment. Focused schema, historical-fixture, strict-decoder, and representative serialization
  suites pass, including unknown-version rejection, provisional legacy rejection, digest validation,
  every stable schema, every declared stable family, and nested composition.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0132
- **Expiration:** stable fixture baseline is reviewed each release; version/profile policy is permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1309, MXR-080-1310, and MXR-080-1311.

## D-0067 · Stability is an explicit module allowlist with exact callable contracts

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** ``mixle.stats`` is provisional by default. Statistical stability is limited to the
  seven fully invariant-catalogued scalar modules (Gaussian, Gamma, Exponential, LogGaussian, Poisson,
  Geometric, and Categorical) plus parameter packing. Semantics and the narrow direct-estimation/EM
  modules remain stable. Every importable stable module must have a linked generated API page. Every
  function, class constructor, and class-owned public method on those modules has a committed exact
  signature record covering parameter order, kinds, defaults, and annotations; intentional exceptions
  are an explicit, currently empty list.
- **Alternatives considered:** inherit stability from ``mixle.stats`` (rejected because hundreds of
  families then acquire an unreviewed promise); call all tested modules stable (rejected because test
  presence is not complete compatibility evidence); and freeze only five headline functions (rejected
  because constructors and the rest of the allowlist could drift silently).
- **Evidence:** maturity, API, documentation coverage, signature generation, standalone checkout, and
  docstring sync contracts pass. The maturity manifest records the allowlist, both formerly omitted
