  stable API pages are generated and linked, and 452 callable signatures are exact.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** stable allowlist is reviewed each release; explicit-only promotion is permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1312, MXR-080-1314, and MXR-080-1315.

## D-0068 · Example evidence is an exact-candidate artifact, not a dated narrative

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The release's authoritative example record is generated during publication after all
  required local and hosted reproduction entries pass. It binds the final full commit, version, tag,
  workflow run, wheel digest/size, dependency-profile digest, and for every required example its exact
  argv, tier, duration, validated-output contract, stdout digest, and receipt digest. Missing,
  duplicate, failed, stale-contract, wrong-command, or over-budget evidence fails closed. The existing
  documentation narrative remains historical context and is explicitly non-authoritative.
- **Alternatives considered:** update a prose page by hand (rejected because it is neither complete nor
  artifact-bound); count process exit alone (rejected because advertised results may be wrong); and
  count optional skips as execution (rejected because absence is not evidence).
- **Evidence:** focused negative controls reject incomplete receipt sets and malformed candidate
  identity, while the positive contract covers every bundle entry. The publish workflow downloads the
  exact hosted receipt, builds the manifest before credentials are requested, and uploads it with the
  release evidence.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0133
- **Expiration:** regenerated for the final 0.8.0 candidate and every later release.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1313.

## D-0069 · Hosted dependency and real-data evidence belongs to one exact candidate

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The SBOM inventories an isolated environment containing the built wheel; ``pip-audit``
  runs from a different tool environment. The bound SBOM names the full candidate commit and exact
  wheel digest and is regenerated by publication for the artifact actually uploaded. The optional
  real-data job runs on release-tip updates, and required Adult/sunspots cases fail on any skip while
  retaining command, duration, counts, commit, and resolved dependencies. Banking77 remains
  source/digest pinned. Every extras-matrix cell retains its exact commit and resolved graph; a required
  aggregate accepts only the complete 24-profile set from one commit.
- **Alternatives considered:** inventory the environment containing ``pip-audit`` (rejected because it
  describes the scanner); count skipped network tests as green (rejected because no case executed);
  cite a scheduled run from an earlier commit (rejected because dependency and source evidence drift);
  and rely on matrix job names alone (rejected because they omit resolved graphs).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts reject an SBOM without Mixle, incomplete/wrong-SHA profile sets, and
  non-executed required cases. Workflow contracts require isolated ``--path`` auditing, non-skipping
  real-data wrappers, release-branch triggers, retained receipts, and the exact-candidate aggregate.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0133
- **Expiration:** dependency receipts and SBOM regenerate for every candidate.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1316, MXR-080-1318, and MXR-080-1319.

## D-0070 · Installer claims, public artifact identity, and review authority fail closed

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** 0.8.0 supports only CPython 3.11 and 3.12; package metadata caps later interpreters
  until they enter the hosted matrix. Post-publication verification downloads every wheel/sdist from
  PyPI, requires its exact filename and SHA-256 to match retained pre-publication sums, and installs
  that verified wheel rather than resolving the version again. Release decisions are not self-approved:
  pending entries remain explicit external blockers, and a required final-tip check accepts only a
  named reviewer, date, and PR/URL/commit evidence or a named superseding decision.
- **Alternatives considered:** say “3.11 and newer” (rejected because future versions are untested);
  behavior-smoke a package selected only by version (rejected because different bytes can behave
  similarly); and infer approval from merge state (rejected because merge is not independent review).
- **Evidence:** package metadata and public docs agree on ``>=3.11,<3.13``; artifact-verifier negative
  controls reject changed bytes and incomplete file sets; decision-review controls reject pending or
  malformed records. The checklist honestly retains decision acceptance as ``EXTERNAL``.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** Python range reviewed at 0.9.0; public identity and review-authority policies permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1317, MXR-080-1320, and MXR-080-1321.

## D-0071 · Release-facing claims require candidate evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Tracked examples may print raw measurements from the current run, but their source,
  documentation, and release notes do not publish fixed performance numbers or superiority
  conclusions without an exact-candidate retained receipt. The 0.8.0 changelog describes
  exactness-preserving implementation changes without timing claims. Its comparison references
  start at the correct release boundaries. Package-index metadata describes the bounded statistical
  inference, automatic modeling, and evidence-aware scientific workflow scope. Documentation does
  not suppress the broad ``docutils`` warning class.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain old laptop/GPU numbers as illustrative prose (rejected because
  readers cannot distinguish them from release claims); cite an unbound historical run (rejected
  because model, inputs, environment, and artifact identity are incomplete); and suppress inherited
  formatting warnings wholesale (rejected because author-maintained page defects disappear too).
- **Evidence:** a focused release-surface gate rejects the retracted numbers, broad warning
  suppression, stale changelog boundaries, and unrestricted or unauthenticated vision loads. The
  vision verifier now authenticates three required SHA-256 values before restricted tensor-only
  deserialization.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0133
- **Expiration:** none (public-claim and artifact-trust policy).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1322, MXR-080-1324, MXR-080-1327, MXR-080-1328,
  MXR-080-1329, and MXR-080-1330.

## D-0072 · Purpose-named test tiers are centrally routed and hard bounded

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Central collection routing, not file-local tier marks, populates every execution
  tier. ``core`` is quick dependency-light base correctness; ``full`` is all non-optional,
  non-benchmark correctness; backend cases are ``optional``; stochastic cases are ``numerical``;
  and MPI/torchrun cases are ``hardware``. Hosted execution uses the named tier, a reviewed hard
  subprocess deadline, an enclosing job deadline, and an exact-commit JSON timing receipt. GPU
  validation is not inferred from CPU-hosted MPI/torchrun evidence.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep four empty marker names while calling the gate complete
  (rejected as false evidence); hand-mark thousands of files (rejected because routing drifts and
  local tier marks can defeat retriage); and treat budgets as advisory prose (rejected because a
  hung collection or fit then consumes the platform default).
- **Evidence:** focused collections select real core, numerical, and hardware cases; taxonomy and
  workflow contracts pass; every executable workflow job has an explicit deadline; and the release
  required-check list names all final-candidate tiers. Broad local collection exceeded the
  development-session 30-second cap and was terminated rather than waited on; hosted tier receipts
  remain required.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** budgets reviewed each release; central routing and hard enforcement are permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1325 and MXR-080-1326.

## D-0073 · Publication prepares immutable artifacts before any public release

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Publication is a manually dispatched two-phase protocol run from the signed
  candidate tag. ``prepare`` re-verifies exact-tip required checks, builds and attests artifacts,
  retains their digests, and creates only a non-public draft GitHub Release. ``promote`` must name
  that prepare run, downloads and re-verifies its exact immutable artifact, publishes those bytes
  to PyPI, and makes the GitHub Release public only after upload succeeds. A rerun accepts only the
  same files and may skip already-present PyPI files to recover after a final-transition failure.
- **Alternatives considered:** trigger builds from ``release: published`` (rejected because a broken
  build follows a public release); rebuild during promotion (rejected because validated and
  published bytes can differ); and publish the GitHub Release before PyPI (rejected because a PyPI
  failure leaves a partially public release).
- **Evidence:** workflow contracts require signed-tag/exact-branch/exact-check binding, a numeric
  prepare run, draft-state verification, cross-run artifact download, digest verification, PyPI
  publication ordering, and the final ``--draft=false`` transition. The former public-release event
  trigger is absent.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0086
- **Expiration:** none (release transaction protocol).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1323.

## D-0074 · Governance and public claims have machine authority

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** One development-policy artifact owns the active release target, maturity tiers,
  deprecation minimum, and blocking/advisory validation split; contributor prose is rendered from it.
  Dependabot targets that release line. Public quantitative and comparative claims live in a separate
  machine inventory with evidence grades and retained-evidence references. Its gate scans README,
  changelog, Sphinx prose, example module docstrings, and committed benchmark results rather than
  treating README as the whole public surface. Duplicate ``__all__`` entries are rejected before
  normalization. The pre-commit hook aborts partial staging before formatting can mutate the index.
- **Alternatives considered:** synchronize several prose policies by hand (rejected because they had
  already contradicted each other); retain README-only overclaim substrings (rejected because most
  public claims live elsewhere); and normalize exports through a set (rejected because it erases the
  defect the compatibility gate must expose).
- **Evidence:** focused governance, public-API, claim-inventory, and installed-wheel invariant tests
  pass. The claim checker resolves eleven ledger claims across all declared public surfaces and has a
  planted unregistered-overclaim negative control.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** release target changes after 0.8.0; other policy is reviewed at each minor release.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1331 through MXR-080-1336.

## D-0075 · External evidence assets are content addressed

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every evidence-oriented Hugging Face model and dataset load names a full upstream
  commit revision. Model identities are available as a machine mapping, examples print or persist the
  exact repository/revision pair, dataset runs also preserve the generated dataset fingerprint, and
  the vision round-trip verifier rejects metrics produced from another asset identity. Model-weight
  loads prefer safetensors where the repository supplies them.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on mutable repository names and local cache state (rejected because
  later upstream changes can silently alter evidence); record only the downloaded model name
  (rejected because it is not content addressed); and trust a producer's metrics without rechecking
  its declared assets (rejected because authenticated bytes can still describe the wrong experiment).
- **Evidence:** a static negative-capable contract resolves every external loading call to a full
  forty-character revision and checks that producer and verifier bind the same asset record. The
  focused contract passes.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0133
- **Expiration:** revisions change only in a reviewed evidence update.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1337.

## D-0076 · Released documentation is an immutable release artifact

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The prepare phase builds strict Sphinx documentation from the exact candidate once,
  packages it deterministically with candidate/version metadata, and publishes the archive plus its
  SHA-256 beside the draft release. Pages builds only moving ``main`` documentation, downloads public
  stable archives, verifies every digest and embedded identity, safely extracts them, and selects the
  highest semantic stable version for the root redirect. Without a stable archive the root is a
  labeled development landing page and does not redirect.
- **Alternatives considered:** rebuild every tag with current dependencies (rejected because historical
  documentation then mutates); redirect users to main (rejected because installed stable APIs can
  differ); and trust release assets without adjacent digests or safe extraction (rejected because
  versioning does not itself authenticate bytes or prevent archive traversal).
- **Evidence:** focused contracts prove byte-identical deterministic packaging, digest rejection,
  traversal rejection, embedded-identity validation, stable-root selection, and the no-release
  development landing. Workflow contracts prove publication creates the archive and Pages never
  invokes sphinx-polyversion.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (released documentation immutability).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1339 and MXR-080-1340.

## D-0077 · Release preparation preserves evidence before replacing artifacts

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Normative scale-out guidance retains structural invariants but no unbound byte/latency
  measurements. GPU training modules are import-safe. The duplicate-body gate records qualified
  lexical scope and line identity. Sdist normalization writes, flushes, validates, and atomically
  replaces a sibling temporary file; failures preserve the original. A fuzz soak rejects nonpositive
  work and emits a machine count of completed properties. Pull-request docs run with read-only
  workflow permissions.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain environment-specific telemetry as prose (rejected because it
  drifts without a receipt); mutate the only sdist in place (rejected because interruption destroys
  the source artifact); and infer work from a zero-exit soak (rejected because zero samples proves
  nothing).
- **Evidence:** focused payload, import-safety, duplicate-scope, injected-replace-failure,
  fuzz-negative-control, workflow-permission, and publication tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none, except telemetry may be republished through candidate-bound receipts.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1341, MXR-080-1342, MXR-080-1344, MXR-080-1347,
  MXR-080-1348, and MXR-080-1349.

## D-0078 · Copyright provenance is an explicit external publication gate

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The original 2023 LLNS copyright notice is restored conservatively alongside the 2026
  notice and reproduced in NOTICE. This preservation is not treated as evidence of assignment,
  relicensing authority, or file-level ownership. The release record remains pending until a named
  independent reviewer cites retained authorization evidence; publication invokes the real verifier
  and fails closed until then.
- **Alternatives considered:** infer ownership from current repository control (rejected); remove the
  institutional notice based on an unsupported relationship statement (rejected); and mark the issue
  complete merely because both names are present (rejected because notice preservation and authority
  to publish are different questions).
- **Evidence:** notice-preservation and planted approved-record controls pass; the committed pending
  record is deliberately rejected by the publication mode.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** approval is release-specific; notice preservation is permanent unless documented
  authority requires a reviewed change.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1343.

## D-0079 · Runtime extras and example claims have explicit closure

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** ``all`` is the mechanically checked union of every runtime-feature extra while
  development-only docs/test/lint remain separate. A project-defined ``examples`` profile declares
  PEFT and external comparison dependencies and joins the exact-candidate resolver matrix. Example
  execution and claimed behavior are distinct fields; release evidence requires both a successful
  process and a machine acceptance contract. Examples that advertise heterogeneous-field or
  cross-feature edge recovery now fail when their actual oracle is false.
- **Alternatives considered:** call an intentionally incomplete convenience bundle ``all`` (rejected);
  ask users to assemble untested ad hoc comparison environments (rejected); and use exit zero as
  evidence for printed conclusions (rejected).
- **Evidence:** the extra-union checker, 24-profile policy check, manifest negative control, and real
  example acceptance-oracle probes pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** union and claim-oracle gates are permanent; dependency versions remain release-bound.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1345, MXR-080-1346, and MXR-080-1350.

## D-0080 · Neural evidence measures the promised trainable boundary

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The PEFT example defines a normalized fixed-length sequence law by assigning a
  uniform initial-token probability, uses disjoint deterministic training and held-out corpora, and
  snapshots every trainable LoRA tensor before optimization. Its receipt requires at least one
  adapter tensor to change, every frozen base tensor to remain exact, and held-out likelihood to
  improve. A missing external checkpoint is absence of evidence, not a passing execution.
- **Alternatives considered:** report merely that trainable tensors exist (rejected because no
  optimization effect is measured); evaluate the training strings again (rejected because memorized
  fit is not held-out improvement); and call continuation likelihood a normalized sequence density
  (rejected because it omits the first-token law).
- **Evidence:** static production-contract checks pass locally. The behavior test is pinned to the
  exact external asset and deliberately skips offline, so exact-checkpoint execution remains a
  hosted or retained external-evidence gate.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** external execution regenerates for every release candidate.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1351 and MXR-080-1352.

## D-0081 · Benchmarks and modality labels cannot outrun their measurements

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Device timings force result consumption and synchronize CUDA or MPS before and after
  every measured operation, after warmup, and only after host/device numerical parity succeeds. The
  example reports raw current-run timings without a predeclared conclusion. Synthetic pairs of
  numeric vectors are described as multi-vector features, not image, audio, video, signal, or
  cross-modal reasoning.
- **Alternatives considered:** time asynchronous dispatch (rejected because it excludes device
  execution); compare speed without result parity (rejected because a wrong result is not an
  optimization); and preserve a cross-modal label as aspirational wording (rejected because no
  modality inputs or encoders exist).
- **Evidence:** focused timing-source, execution-manifest, documentation, and acceptance-artifact
  contracts pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none; retained candidate-bound hardware measurements are still required for claims.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1353 and MXR-080-1354.

## D-0082 · Release evidence exercises production gates and bounded dependency graphs

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Adverse controls invoke the real fit, API, optional-union, public-claim,
  reproducibility, license, and import boundaries. Publication accepts artifacts only after two
  independent clean source-tree builds produce byte-identical normalized wheel/sdist sets.
  Every runtime dependency has a reviewed compatible ceiling, and every release profile must pass
  both its current resolved graph and an exact declared-floor graph. Security has distinct required
  base vulnerability, complete-runtime vulnerability, and scoped source-analysis jobs; both
  dependency inventories are bound to and published with the candidate artifact.
- **Alternatives considered:** test copied helper logic (rejected because production gates can drift);
  infer reproducibility from one normalized build (rejected because no comparison occurs); leave
  future major versions unbounded (rejected because an old release would silently claim them); and
  audit only the lean base graph (rejected because opt-in runtime features ship as supported code).
- **Evidence:** 42 focused contracts passed locally, including planted production-gate failures,
  dependency-union/ceiling/floor checks, workflow contracts, and reproducible-build mismatch
  rejection. Exact full environment jobs remain hosted.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** graphs, SBOMs, and reproducibility receipts regenerate for every candidate;
  production-gate and dependency-bound policies are permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1355, MXR-080-1356, MXR-080-1357, MXR-080-1359,
  MXR-080-1360, and MXR-080-1361.

## D-0083 · Release automation executes immutable, least-privilege dependencies

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every third-party action in the six release workflows is selected by a reviewed full
  commit and carries a non-executable version comment. All workflows declare top-level permissions and
  disable persisted checkout credentials; publishing jobs add only their scoped authority. Manual
  values reach shell code only through environment variables after validation. A PR lint gate parses
  every workflow and enforces these rules. Gitleaks scans full history, while current-tree formats remain
  a fast local adverse control. This supersedes D-0002's free-form waiver file: vulnerability waivers
  are structured, owned, reasoned, profile-scoped, reviewed, expiring records embedded in SBOM evidence.
- **Alternatives considered:** trust moving major tags (rejected because unchanged source would execute
  changed code); inherit repository token defaults (rejected because authority then changes outside
  review); and interpolate dispatch strings into shell source (rejected because data becomes syntax).
- **Evidence:** workflow parser, immutable-action inventory, current-tree credential scan, input-boundary,
  YAML, and permission contracts pass locally. Full-history execution remains a required hosted check.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** action commits change only through reviewed dependency updates; scanner evidence
  regenerates on every candidate.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1358, MXR-080-1362, MXR-080-1363, MXR-080-1370,
  MXR-080-1371, MXR-080-1373, MXR-080-1379, MXR-080-1380, and MXR-080-1387.

## D-0084 · Both release artifacts share one exact builder and behavior contract

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Build-system requirements and the release frontend, installer, wheel, and metadata
  checker are exactly locked. Publication records their installed versions and builds twice from
  independent exported source trees without hidden build isolation. Sdist normalization uses a fully
  specified stdlib gzip stream rather than a runner executable. Wheel and sdist install into separate
  fresh environments and both run import, stable-family, representative-fit, serialization, and exact
  schema-removal checks outside the checkout. The full serialization profile is a required optional-lane
  check rather than a dependency-conditioned skip.
- **Alternatives considered:** validate only wheel behavior (rejected because sdist is a separately
  published artifact); record one normalized build as reproducible (rejected); and let build isolation
  resolve future backends (rejected because artifact bytes would depend on build date).
- **Evidence:** normalization, reproducibility mismatch, build-lock drift, schema-profile, workflow, and
  artifact-lane contracts pass locally; exact clean installations remain hosted.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** builder lock and artifact receipts are release-specific; dual-artifact behavior is
  permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1365, MXR-080-1366, MXR-080-1367, and MXR-080-1368.

## D-0085 · Core 0.8.0 is standalone and external evidence is candidate-bound

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Core 0.8.0 makes no release, support, co-installation, or compatibility claim for any
  related project. A future coordinated release must add a versioned family manifest. README links are
  tag-bound, the download-free statement applies only to the five named offline examples, and other
  examples point to network/cache/asset disclosures. Every independent review instrument names the same
  full source commit and wheel/sdist digests. Identity mismatch, NO-GO, or unresolved blocking caveats
  cannot authorize release, and repairs require independent retest against the new bytes. The obsolete
  contradictory documentation requirements file is removed; the project docs extra is authoritative.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on an unspecified external family ledger (rejected because local
  sign-off cannot inspect it); retain a vague sibling roster (rejected because it implies coordination);
  accept reports by version string (rejected because rebuilt candidates can differ); and keep two docs
  dependency authorities (rejected because local and hosted builds diverged).
- **Evidence:** public-claim, support-scope, versioned-link, workflow, and review-template contracts pass
  locally. Human reviews remain explicit external blockers.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** standalone scope applies to 0.8.0; evidence identity rules are permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1372, MXR-080-1375, MXR-080-1376, MXR-080-1377,
  MXR-080-1378, MXR-080-1382, and MXR-080-1390.

## D-0086 · Publication consumes final receipts, not preparation labels

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** ``IMPLEMENTED`` means local development work exists and never clears a release gate.
  Only ``DONE`` with a schema-validated exact-candidate command/date/measured-result receipt can do so;
  publication rejects every remaining implemented, hosted, or external row. It also rejects
  unreleased document markers. A protected TestPyPI phase publishes the prepared bytes, downloads both
  artifacts, verifies their digests, installs and sweeps the wheel, and emits a receipt that production
  promotion must bind. Pages uploads the already strict development build plus authenticated immutable
  release archives only after validating the assembled root, navigation, and embedded identities.
- **Alternatives considered:** treat prose ``COMPLETE`` as evidence (rejected because it named neither
  candidate nor command); make TestPyPI a checklist instruction without an executable path (rejected);
  and upload an assembly not checked byte-for-byte as a site (rejected because the deployed artifact
  would differ from the gate).
- **Evidence:** checklist adverse controls reject local-only status and wrong-commit receipts;
  document-state controls reject the current deliberate prerelease markers; TestPyPI/promotion and
  assembled-site workflow contracts pass. Exact hosted/external receipts remain blocking.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none; candidate receipts regenerate for each release.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1381, MXR-080-1383, MXR-080-1385, and MXR-080-1386.

## D-0087 · Support and independent review follow the risk-bearing surface

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Stable-core behavior runs on Ubuntu/macOS arm64 and Python 3.11/3.12; the exhaustive
  platform-independent and clean-artifact lanes run on the declared Ubuntu/Python 3.12 representative
  cell. Optional stacks claim only the cells with exact receipts. Systems review cannot credit comments
  as backend execution: the scaling example runs and asserts only local/multiprocessing agreement, while
  each supported/optional-CI backend needs its own command and receipt. Statistical review covers every
  stable family across normalization, support, parity, sampling, estimation, weights, adverse inputs,
  posterior validity, and serialization, including reviewer-authored probes. Scientist and quotient-leaf
  evidence installs its complete profile before collection and treats any pinned-asset skip as failure.
- **Alternatives considered:** duplicate every platform-independent stochastic test in every cell
  (rejected as cost without additional platform coverage); infer optional support from resolver success
  (rejected); accept one backend/benchmark as systems review (rejected because it misses distinct failure
  boundaries); and review only claim wording (rejected because statistical laws can still be wrong).
- **Evidence:** support-policy consistency, fail-closed scaling parity, review-instrument, optional-profile,
  pinned-asset, and required-receipt contracts pass locally. External and hosted execution remain gates.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** support cells and optional assets are reviewed every release; risk-based evidence rules
  are permanent.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1369, MXR-080-1384, MXR-080-1388, MXR-080-1389,
  and MXR-080-1391.

## D-0088 · Coverage estimators fail closed on unidentified samples

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** The provisional analysis surface adds
  ``CoverageInsufficientDataError`` as the one public failure contract used by every coverage,
  richness, diversity, and rarefaction estimator when no observations exist. Abundance vectors and
  incidence matrices retain their declared axes, abundance totals use Python integers, and scientific
  controls are validated before computation. This is an approved post-freeze addition because callers
  need a stable way to distinguish unidentified estimates from malformed inputs.
- **Alternatives considered:** preserve conflicting sentinel dictionaries and numerical defaults
  (rejected because return types and scientific meanings disagree); return a new result union from
  every estimator (rejected as a much larger provisional-API change); and expose only a private
  exception (rejected because callers could not reliably handle the documented state).
- **Evidence:** focused coverage tests exercise every empty-sample path, signed-integer overflow,
  dimensional misuse, invalid controls, and ordinary estimates; the generated API manifest records the
  added exception.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none; reconsider only in a major result-protocol revision.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1395, MXR-080-1396, MXR-080-1397, and MXR-080-1398.

## D-0089 · Covariance estimation errors stay outside the distribution protocol

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** ``LedoitWolfEstimator.estimate`` either returns a valid
  ``MultivariateGaussianDistribution`` or raises ``LedoitWolfInsufficientData`` as a specific
  ``ValueError``. It never returns an object that violates ``ParameterEstimator``'s distribution
  contract. Batch updates require an exact observation matrix and one weight per row. Zero-dispersion
  data fail as unidentified; nonzero rank-deficient estimates receive only a scale-relative numerical
  eigenvalue floor whose exact contribution is exposed as ``regularization``.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain a sentinel return union (rejected because generic and composite
  estimation accept only distributions); let weights broadcast (rejected because sufficient statistics
  and count then disagree); and let the Gaussian constructor invent hidden variance (rejected because
  that turns identical data into unsupported evidence).
- **Evidence:** focused estimator tests cover generic sparse-data failure, identical observations,
  weight-shape adverse controls, streaming/distributed equivalence, and the closed-form reference.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1399, MXR-080-1400, and MXR-080-1401.

## D-0090 · Reference-dose evidence has one scalar, immutable state contract

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** ``BMDResult`` is an immutable closed state machine: only internally consistent
  ``ok``, ``unidentifiable``, and ``bmdl_unavailable`` states can be constructed, and coefficient
  storage is owned and read-only. Reference-dose calculations accept only a validated ``ok`` result,
  a non-Boolean positive scalar uncertainty factor, an exact sample count, and exactly one finite
  nonnegative exposure scalar per draw. Posterior pushforwards are normalized into the locally
  validated derived-quantity carrier before return.
- **Alternatives considered:** infer validity from a finite ``bmdl`` alone (rejected because status and
  other fields can contradict it); threshold arbitrary array axes elementwise (rejected because the API
  defines one exceedance probability); and trust a posterior's wrapper without validating its returned
  samples (rejected because invalid physical draws would become false threshold indicators).
- **Evidence:** focused developmental-risk tests cover direct impossible states, attempted mutation,
  Boolean/fractional controls, malformed posterior and array axes, invalid draws, and ordinary fits.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1402, MXR-080-1403, and MXR-080-1404.

## D-0091 · Cancer potency and risk carriers do not acquire implicit axes

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Each ``SlopeFactor`` potency and uncertainty field is a finite nonnegative scalar,
  and the validated carrier is immutable. ``RiskQuantity`` owns one immutable one-dimensional sample
  vector and a Boolean provenance flag; later mutation cannot invalidate its mean or interval.
  Separate multi-chemical batching must use an explicit future API rather than NumPy broadcasting
  through the one-chemical model.
- **Alternatives considered:** accept coefficient arrays and infer a chemical axis (rejected because
  no chemical labels or axis contract exist); revalidate mutable results only when read (rejected
  because invalid state could still cross module boundaries); and copy without freezing (rejected
  because callers could still mutate the public array).
- **Evidence:** focused cancer-risk tests cover array/Boolean coefficients, immutable defensive
  copies, invalid sample ranks and provenance, posterior pushforwards, and reference calculations.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1405 and MXR-080-1406.

## D-0092 · Epidemiology reports only identifiable cohort effects

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Cohort attribution rejects empty or post-latency-empty designs, fewer cause-one
  events than coefficients plus one, rank-deficient covariates, and event risk sets without
  full-rank exposure comparisons. A main Cox result must explicitly converge with a finite positive
  standard error and finite hazard-ratio interval; bootstrap fits satisfy the same condition.
  ``n_boot`` is a positive exact non-Boolean integer, while requests too small for a percentile
  interval retain the existing explicit insufficient-bootstrap result.
- **Alternatives considered:** map unidentified designs to ``HR=1`` (rejected because it fabricates a
  precise null); trust a solver's numeric return without its convergence flag (rejected); and silently
  disable bootstrap for nonpositive draws (rejected because the return still claims an interval
  request).
- **Evidence:** focused adverse tests cover empty/no-event/constant/post-latency/risk-set-degenerate
  cohorts and invalid draw counts; separate bounded probes cover known-hazard, latency, and competing
  risk fits.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1407 and MXR-080-1408.

## D-0093 · Emissions economics fails closed before publishing risk

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Emissions uncertainty accepts only an exact non-Boolean nonnegative draw count, with
  zero explicitly selecting a point estimate. Scope totals, Monte Carlo contributions, discounted
  prices, carbon costs, adjusted NPV samples, and scenario means must all remain finite. Climate
  terms require a finite footprint total and a finite nonnegative carbon price. Directly constructed
  transition-risk results must have one finite mean and cost per sample column plus a complete
  scenario-index permutation.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on NumPy warnings and downstream result validation (rejected
  because infinities had already become authoritative outputs); silently treat invalid draw counts as
  no uncertainty (rejected because it misstates the requested evidence); and interpret a negative
  price as a credit (rejected because this API declares a carbon cost and has no credit provenance
  model).
- **Evidence:** focused emissions and transition-risk tests cover invalid draw controls, the explicit
  zero-draw path, non-finite and negative prices, finite-input overflow in each economic reduction,
  adjusted-value overflow, malformed direct result construction, and ordinary scenario ranking.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1409, MXR-080-1410, and MXR-080-1411.

## D-0094 · POT fits retain valid raw evidence and tail applicability

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Raw peaks-over-threshold and mean-residual-life helpers reject non-finite
  observations and thresholds before comparison. ``GPDFit`` is an immutable validated result:
  parameters and threshold are finite, scale is positive, counts are exact and consistent, and the
  method is declared. Return levels accept only finite positive scalar periods in the recurrence
  region where ``period * exceedance_rate >= 1`` and must remain finite and at or above the fitted
  threshold.
- **Alternatives considered:** silently remove non-finite raw observations (rejected because it
  falsifies the full-sample count and exceedance rate); validate only fits returned by ``gpd_fit``
  (rejected because ``GPDFit`` is public); and extrapolate a tail model below its threshold (rejected
  because the POT approximation makes no claim about the body distribution).
- **Evidence:** focused extreme-value tests cover NaN/Inf raw evidence and thresholds, fractional and
  Boolean counts, inconsistent direct result construction, invalid periods, threshold recurrence,
  ordinary MLE/PWM recovery, endpoints, and tail-index estimators.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1412 and MXR-080-1413.

## D-0095 · Habitat exclusions preserve topology and mask identity

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Critical-habitat controls require a finite scalar cut, an exact non-Boolean
  nonnegative buffer radius, a nonempty one-dimensional finite habitat mean, and an exact Boolean
  returned mask with one entry per cell. Network application accepts only a one-dimensional Boolean
  or exact numeric-binary exclusion mask. Node identities are numeric exact integers and can never
  be Boolean values.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on NumPy truth casting (rejected because NaN and arbitrary
  nonzero values become legal prohibitions); broadcast a scalar habitat decision (rejected because it
  invents per-cell evidence); and truncate radius or node controls (rejected because it changes the
  declared geometry/topology).
- **Evidence:** a dependency-free focused test module covers negative, fractional, and Boolean buffer
  controls; invalid cuts; malformed habitat axes and returned masks; arbitrary exclusion values;
  Boolean node identities; exact binary masks; ordinary buffering; and network exclusion.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1414 and MXR-080-1415.

## D-0096 · Health risk retains physical values and explicit axes

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** All dose-response models share one finite nonnegative dose gate before model
  arithmetic. Bare dose ensembles are scalar or one-dimensional; posterior probability draws are
  ``(n,)`` or ``(n, n_receptors)``, population-risk posterior draws are explicitly
  ``(n, n_receptors)``, and every draw count is an exact non-Boolean positive integer. Cumulative
  exposure integrates only a one-dimensional finite nonnegative series. Health liability accepts
  nonempty finite nonnegative scalar-risk or receptor-risk draw ensembles and fails on overflow.
  Safety-risk posterior means, draws, tilt, binary results, shapes, and provenance flags are all
  validated at their respective boundaries.
- **Alternatives considered:** clip negative doses to zero (rejected because it launders invalid
  physical evidence); flatten receptor/time axes (rejected because it changes the statistical
  estimand); interpret negative cases as benefits (rejected); and trust comparisons or posterior
  wrappers to sanitize NaN deformation (rejected because ``NaN > limit`` is false and appeared safe).
- **Evidence:** focused health, liability, and safety tests cover every dose model, negative and
  multidimensional dose, invalid draw controls, posterior dose and deformation failures, cumulative
  exposure axes/domain, negative and malformed risk samples, invalid returned derived quantities,
  ordinary calibration, population aggregation, and deterministic/posterior safety surfaces.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1416, MXR-080-1417, MXR-080-1418, and MXR-080-1419.

## D-0097 · KDE fits are normalized immutable statistical objects

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** One-sided bounded KDEs use exactly normalized reflection; finite-interval KDEs use
  per-kernel truncated-Gaussian normalization, including when bandwidth is large relative to the
  interval. Training observations must lie on the declared support. Evaluation designs are nonempty
  finite ``(m,d)`` arrays before support masking. A fit owns read-only training, resolved-bandwidth,
  and local-bandwidth arrays. Bandwidth-rate dimensions are exact non-Boolean positive integers, and
  point-process grids are nonempty finite one-dimensional arrays.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain one reflection at both finite boundaries (rejected because
  omitted repeated images lose mass); normalize the final grid numerically (rejected because density
  would depend on the caller's evaluation grid); retain caller array views (rejected because the
  kernels could change while bandwidth state stayed stale); and allow NaN support masking (rejected
  because it labels missing coordinates as zero density).
- **Evidence:** focused KDE tests integrate finite-interval densities across bandwidth/domain ratios
  from 0.05 through 100, retain half-line edge behavior and total mass, reject empty and non-finite
  queries and intensity grids, verify caller mutation cannot alter a fit, verify public arrays are
  read-only, and exercise valid multivariate/adaptive densities and exact dimension controls.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1420, MXR-080-1421, MXR-080-1422, and MXR-080-1423.

## D-0098 · Kriging calibration and drift are identifiable by construction

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Empirical variograms validate exact finite ``(n,d)``/``(n,)`` geometry, at least two
  distinct locations, exact positive bin counts, finite positive cutoffs, and finite pairwise
  distances/semivariances before binning. Universal kriging supports only exact non-Boolean degrees
  0, 1, and 2 and requires enough observations for a full-rank drift basis. Variance calibration
  accepts only paired one-dimensional validation samples and computes the required empirical
  order-statistic factor without fixed search bounds. Zero-residual identification failures and
  factors outside representable range use public typed exceptions.
- **Alternatives considered:** return an insufficient-evidence variogram for invalid raw geometry
  (rejected because NaN and mismatched evidence are not valid scarcity); let polynomial comparisons
  choose an approximate degree (rejected because it silently changes the drift model); and return
  exhausted binary-search caps (rejected because those constants are not estimates).
- **Evidence:** focused kriging tests cover mismatched and non-finite geometry, non-distinct sites,
  invalid bins/cutoffs, supported bin-boundary behavior, invalid degrees, rank-deficient drift,
  multidimensional calibration arrays, zero residuals, unrepresentable factors, factors above the
  former cap, ordinary fitted variograms, universal trends, and target empirical coverage.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1424, MXR-080-1425, MXR-080-1426, and MXR-080-1427.

## D-0099 · Smith dependence, simulation, and diagnostics share valid state

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** ``SmithMaxStable`` owns a read-only covariance and derives its immutable cached
  inverse from that same copy. Lag vectors are finite before Mahalanobis evaluation, and every
  published extremal coefficient and bivariate CDF is checked against its probability range.
  Samplers require a nonempty exact location matrix; requested field, storm, and diagnostic
  cardinalities are exact non-Boolean positive integers. Approximation levels are a nonempty finite
  vector strictly inside ``(0,1)``. ``FrechetApproximationDiagnostic`` is an immutable validated
  result with finite bounded, internally consistent error summaries.
- **Alternatives considered:** recalculate the inverse lazily from caller-owned covariance (rejected
  because mutable model state remains incoherent); allow NaN comparisons to propagate (rejected
  because probability-returning APIs cannot publish NaN); accept empty simulation requests (rejected
  because they are not evidence); and validate diagnostics only when plotted (rejected because the
  invalid certification already crossed the public boundary).
- **Evidence:** focused max-stable tests cover caller covariance mutation, read-only state, finite
  and non-finite lags, empty locations, Boolean/fractional/nonpositive cardinalities, malformed
  levels, inconsistent direct diagnostic construction, finite bounded small diagnostics, valid
  exact dependence probabilities, and positive sampled fields.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1428, MXR-080-1429, MXR-080-1430, and MXR-080-1431.

## D-0100 · Consensus models disclose nonempty identity and search guarantees

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Every permutation and aggregation request contains at least one ranking over at
  least one common item. Item identities are numeric exact integers and never Boolean. The Kemeny
  factorial boundary is an exact non-Boolean nonnegative integer; exact enumeration explicitly
  reports ``search_mode="exact_enumeration"``, while the adjacent-swap approximation reports its
  heuristic mode. Mallows results retain the Kemeny exactness, search mode, boundary, and consensus
  distance used to estimate their center.
- **Alternatives considered:** treat the empty permutation as a mathematical identity (rejected
  because an aggregation result would falsely claim empirical consensus); map Boolean truth values
  to items 0 and 1 (rejected because topology was not declared); and expose only the Mallows
  concentration (rejected because its center may be heuristic rather than maximum likelihood).
- **Evidence:** focused rank-aggregation tests cover empty ranking matrices and item sets across all
  aggregation/distance APIs, Boolean identities, negative/fractional/Boolean/NaN factorial controls,
  exact and heuristic Mallows receipts, known permutation distances, and ordinary consensus cases.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1432 and MXR-080-1433.

## D-0101 · Spatial and habitat posteriors preserve exact topology and evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Spatial-mixture topology, component counts, and iteration controls are exact
  non-Boolean positive integers. Its current in-memory neighbor representation is explicitly bounded
  to one million cells. Every responsibility state is a finite row-wise simplex, is owned by the
  fitted model, and is exposed read-only. Habitat thresholds are finite nonnegative scalars at both
  model and network boundaries. ``HabitatModel`` owns immutable coefficient, covariance, design,
  and cell-area arrays. Derived quantities preserve the requested leading sample axis and contain
  only finite samples before becoming evidence.
- **Alternatives considered:** truncate fractional controls (rejected because it changes declared
  topology and computation); allocate arbitrary grids and rely on memory failure (rejected because
  the dense neighbor representation has a known operational bound); expose mutable posterior state
  (rejected because later caller mutation would rewrite fitted evidence); and let NumPy comparisons
  or quantiles interpret negative, non-finite, or malformed habitat results (rejected because they
  can turn invalid evidence into authoritative masks or intervals).
- **Evidence:** focused spatial-mixture, habitat-model, and dependency-free habitat-constraint tests
  cover fractional and Boolean controls, the allocation cap, malformed or mutable responsibilities,
  immutable fitted inputs, invalid thresholds, callback sample-axis and finiteness failures, and
  ordinary posterior, labeling, entropy, sampling, buffering, and exclusion behavior.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1434, MXR-080-1435, MXR-080-1436, MXR-080-1437, and MXR-080-1438.

## D-0102 · Habitat covariance is bounded and matrix-free at survey scale

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Habitat posteriors return a validated dense field covariance only for domains of at
  most 2,048 cells. Larger domains expose the same symmetric delta-method covariance as a
  ``LinearOperator`` whose storage is linear in cell count and coefficient dimension. Jacobian,
  dense covariance, operands, and matrix-free products must remain finite; unrepresentable float64
  results fail explicitly instead of becoming infinite posterior evidence.
- **Alternatives considered:** always materialize ``K × K`` covariance (rejected because field-scale
  storage is quadratic); return only a diagonal (rejected because it discards spatial covariance);
  and silently scale or clip infinite results (rejected because that changes the posterior without a
  declared numerical model).
- **Evidence:** focused habitat tests retain the ordinary dense covariance contract, reject the
  one-cell finite-input overflow probe, and verify a 2,049-cell covariance is a correct matrix-free
  operator that rejects non-finite operands.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1440 and MXR-080-1441.

## D-0103 · Real-option and VOI evidence preserves declared economics

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Decision:** Real-option maturity boundaries are derived from each named action: zero for
  deferral, ``expansion_cost / expand_fraction`` for expansion, and salvage value for abandonment.
  Running the base project is not reported as exercising expansion. Horizons, lattice steps, and VOI
  sample counts require integral non-Boolean scalars; at least two outer replicates are required when
  reporting Monte Carlo standard error. Expansion fractions and costs are nonnegative. Every lattice
  intermediate and published price must be representable as finite float64. Variance reduction is a
  validated ``[0, 1)`` fraction, including PDE-hook results, and is never clipped into a different
  experiment. Gaussian EVSI requires a finite one-dimensional posterior mean, symmetric PSD
  posterior covariance, and symmetric positive-definite observation covariance. All covariance
  calculations use those same validated matrices. Decision callbacks must return finite scalars with
  replicate/side attribution, and Gaussian-shape diagnostics run on a cloned random stream.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain a kind-independent zero boundary (rejected because it gives
  controllers a false policy); coerce counts and variance reductions (rejected because that silently
  changes the requested computation); symmetrize material asymmetry (rejected because simulation and
  conditioning would describe different models); clip non-finite prices or decisions (rejected
  because it fabricates economic evidence); and share diagnostic randomness with estimation
  (rejected because warning-only work must not change an estimate).
- **Evidence:** 30 focused adverse probes cover policy boundaries, base-versus-expansion identity,
  count types, reversed expansion controls, lattice overflow, direct and PDE-derived variance
  reductions, covariance symmetry/definiteness, posterior mean rank/finiteness, callback failures,
  and diagnostic stream independence. A further 63 existing real-option/VOI tests pass unchanged
  apart from the deliberately tightened integral-count contract.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1442 through MXR-080-1451.

## D-0104 · Valuation preserves period axes and representable evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Cost and capital parameter mappings reject unknown names at every public boundary.
  Unit-cost terms, period costs, tonnage and capital roll-ups, DCF discount factors, revenues, cash
  flows, NPVs, summaries, variances, and sensitivity outputs must remain finite; failures identify
  the derived term and first invalid index. Tonnage and capital schedules are nonempty
  one-dimensional vectors with exactly the same period shape. Cost-model dispatch uses signature
  binding and accepts exactly one of ``cost(period)`` or ``cost(period, tonnage)``. An
  ``NPVDistribution`` owns a read-only one-dimensional sample array, validates its summaries against
  those samples, and exposes a copied immutable finite sensitivity mapping.
- **Alternatives considered:** ignore unknown parameter names (rejected because a typo can erase a
  cost); rely on NumPy overflow warnings (rejected because infinite economics had already crossed the
  result boundary); flatten or sum arbitrary schedule ranks (rejected because it destroys period
  identity); infer callable arity from parameter count or retry after ``TypeError`` (rejected because
  keyword-only controls and internal errors are misclassified); and retain mutable nested evidence
  (rejected because samples and sensitivity could diverge from stored summaries).
- **Evidence:** focused release probes cover cost, capital, revenue, discount, and variance overflow;
  misspelled parameters; misaligned and multidimensional schedules; keyword-only and ambiguous
  callable signatures; inconsistent direct result construction; and caller mutation. All 17 existing
  cost tests and all 25 existing valuation tests also pass under the tightened contracts.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1452 through MXR-080-1458.

## D-0105 · Propagation methods share one covariance model

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Propagation means are finite nonempty one-dimensional arrays and Monte Carlo counts are
  exact positive non-Boolean integers. Covariances are finite square, symmetric within an explicit
  ``1e-10`` relative roundoff tolerance, and PSD. The validated symmetric matrix—not the caller's
  original—is passed to every propagator. Unscented parameters are finite non-Boolean scalars with
  ``alpha > 0`` and ``beta >= 0``. Derived means, covariance, standard deviations, and Monte Carlo
  summaries must remain finite; unscented covariance is validated PSD, with only tolerance-scale
  negative eigenvalue roundoff projected to the closed PSD cone.
- **Alternatives considered:** silently symmetrize material asymmetry (rejected because methods would
  model different distributions); coerce scalar or multidimensional means and Boolean/float counts
  (rejected because that changes axes or work budgets); allow arbitrary negative unscented weights and
  clip the resulting variance (rejected because impossible uncertainty becomes false certainty); and
  trust finite callback values to imply finite summaries (rejected because reductions can overflow).
- **Evidence:** 14 focused adverse probes cover asymmetric covariance, common validated state,
  Boolean/floating counts, malformed means, negative/Boolean beta, and PSD nonlinear output. All 25
  existing propagation tests and both singular-covariance stability tests also pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1459, MXR-080-1460, and MXR-080-1461.

## D-0106 · Sensitivity estimators preserve axes, uncertainty, and sampler identity

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Vectorized sensitivity callbacks return exactly ``(n,)`` or ``(n,1)``; validation
  occurs before singleton-axis normalization. Sobol uncertainty requires at least two finite,
  nondegenerate bootstrap replicates. Output variance, index arithmetic, spectra, elementary effects,
  derivatives, normalization, standard errors, and intervals must remain finite. DGSM's relative
  step is a finite strictly positive non-Boolean scalar and every effective stencil is finite and
  nonzero. RBD-FAST publishes its raw bias-corrected estimate, a separately named clipped view, and
  circular-block-bootstrap uncertainty. Sobol/QMC fallback catches only missing optional capability;
  internal failures propagate, and Sobol/DGSM results disclose their actual sampling method.
- **Alternatives considered:** flatten arbitrary callback shapes (rejected because transposed batches
  lose sample identity); accept one bootstrap draw and publish NaN uncertainty (rejected); rely on
  finite inputs while derived arithmetic overflows (rejected); substitute one for a zero DGSM
  denominator (rejected because it fabricates a derivative); clip FAST as the sole estimate
  (rejected because sampling noise becomes false boundary certainty); and catch every QMC exception
  (rejected because programming and dependency failures silently downgrade the estimator).
- **Evidence:** 13 focused adverse probes cover transposed and singleton callback axes, one-resample
  bootstrap, overflow in Sobol/FAST/DGSM, invalid finite-difference controls, negative raw FAST
  estimates with uncertainty, sampler disclosure, and internal QMC failure propagation. Twenty-two
  existing sensitivity and stability tests plus 32 subtests also pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1462 through MXR-080-1467.

## D-0107 · DoE analysis reports only identified contrasts and surfaces

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Factorial alias groups retain each column's sign relative to the defining column and
  publish the exact signed contrast in public effect units. Quadratic response surfaces require
  nonempty finite ``(n,d)`` evidence, exactly one finite response per row, a finite model matrix, and
  full column rank before coefficient fitting or canonical classification. Returned surfaces report
  rank, parameter count, residual degrees of freedom, and estimability. Design diagnostics validate
  raw and transformed sample axes, finiteness, nonempty features, information arithmetic, prediction
  variance, and every numeric result. Rank-zero models are rejected; partially estimable models report
  explicit rank/full-rank state, zero full-model efficiencies, ``condition_number=None``, and a finite
  effective-subspace condition number instead of NaN/Inf.
- **Alternatives considered:** label opposite columns as a sum (rejected because it assigns a
  difference estimate to the wrong scientific contrast); use minimum-norm quadratic coefficients
  (rejected because arbitrary unidentified curvature becomes a false optimum); let LAPACK diagnose
  malformed evidence (rejected because low-level failures lose the evidence contract); and publish
  NaN/Inf diagnostic values for singular or invalid designs (rejected because quality evidence must
  be machine-actionable and explicit).
- **Evidence:** 17 focused probes cover opposite-sign aliases, underdetermined quadratics, invalid
  response evidence, rank/DoF metadata, malformed and overflowing diagnostic matrices, rank-zero
  refusal, and explicit finite rank-deficient diagnostics. All 21 existing analysis tests and two
  zero-variance diagnostic stability tests also pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1468, MXR-080-1469, MXR-080-1470, and MXR-080-1471.

## D-0108 · Optimal design preserves budgets and evidence axes

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Requested design size, generated candidate count, restart count, and exchange
  iterations use the shared exact-positive-non-Boolean integer validator; no zero/default truthiness
  coercion remains. Explicit candidates and returned model matrices are finite nonempty
  two-dimensional arrays with exactly one feature row per candidate. Reference model matrices contain
  at least one finite row of matching width. Contrast vectors are finite, nonempty, and
  one-dimensional. Every built-in or registered criterion returns one numeric scalar merit before the
  exchange loop compares it; NaN, positive infinity, Boolean, and nonscalar merits fail, while
  documented negative infinity remains the explicit singular-design sentinel.
- **Alternatives considered:** truncate counts or force at least one restart (rejected because it
  changes declared computational budgets); coerce candidate axes with ``atleast_2d`` (rejected because
  scalar/one-dimensional evidence gains invented semantics); allow empty reference/contrast evidence
  (rejected because mean/max/quadratic criteria are undefined); and let NumPy comparison order NaN
  custom merits (rejected because optimizer choices would depend on invalid evidence).
- **Evidence:** 29 focused probes cover every count control, malformed candidates and feature
  matrices, empty references, invalid contrasts, and invalid custom merits. All 32 existing optimal
  design tests and five optimal-criteria integration tests also pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1472, MXR-080-1473, and MXR-080-1474.

## D-0109 · Experiment designs preserve finite geometry and publish real alias chains

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Unit and coded designs are mapped with overflow-safe half-endpoint arithmetic and every
  mapped result must be finite. Maximin selection occurs in unit coordinates, so extreme but finite
  physical bounds cannot corrupt its distance comparisons; defensive runtime failures replace
  optimized-away assertions. CCD Boolean alpha values are rejected. Fractional-factorial alias
  evidence now contains public factor/token mappings, signed compound generators, signed defining
  relations, the generated defining group, and every induced alias through a caller-selected
  interaction order.
- **Alternatives considered:** reject all bounds whose direct span overflows (rejected because many
  useful points, including both endpoints, remain representable); retain physical-coordinate
  normalization in maximin selection (rejected because it recreates the overflowing span); treat
  Boolean alpha as numeric one (rejected because it silently selects a design mode); and keep the old
  compound-token fragment map under an alias-structure name (rejected because it cannot identify
  confounded fitted terms).
- **Evidence:** 12 focused assertions cover six design families over extreme finite bounds, a
  same-sign extreme midpoint, exact fractional-factorial endpoints, Boolean CCD modes, signed
  defining relations, generated alias chains, interaction-order filtering, and unambiguous base
  labels. All 56 DoE design/factorial tests and 54 subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1475, MXR-080-1476, and MXR-080-1477.

## D-0110 · Sequential side effects require durable decisions

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Every callback, audit-record validation, and history-copy stage is named and handled by
  the same partial-result error policy. Summary and decision records accept only the portable JSON
  value model: string object keys, arrays, strings, exact JSON scalars, and finite numbers; records
  are serialized and decoded at admission to detach identity. Controller decisions require an actual
  Boolean ``keep_going`` and a nonempty string ``reason`` before proposal or acquisition can occur.
- **Alternatives considered:** let summary/controller exceptions escape directly (rejected because the
  most important failure evidence disappears); treat deep-copyability as JSON durability (rejected
  because arbitrary objects and non-finite numbers still cannot form a portable record); coerce
  truthy controller values (rejected because the string ``"false"`` can trigger a real-world side
  effect); and default a missing reason (rejected because an unexplained decision is not auditable).
- **Evidence:** 18 focused adverse cases cover summary/controller exceptions, non-dict results,
  string/integer/null/NumPy-Boolean decisions, arbitrary nested objects, NaN, non-string keys, missing
  reasons, and history-copy failure. All 34 sequential-design tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1478, MXR-080-1479, and MXR-080-1480.

## D-0111 · Multi-fidelity optimization separates spend, evidence, and model failure

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Initialization and candidate counts use exact positive non-Boolean integer contracts,
  with only ``None`` selecting the initialization default. Every attempted objective call is charged.
  A call that raises or returns a non-scalar, Boolean, or non-finite observation is excluded from
  training/optimum evidence and retained in ``failed_evaluations`` with point, fidelity, real cost,
  status, and error. Surrogate means and covariances must have exact requested cardinality, finite
  values, symmetry, and PSD before EI or fidelity selection; invalid predictions stop with
  ``surrogate_prediction_failed`` and cannot trigger another objective call.
- **Alternatives considered:** truncate controls or treat zero as a default sentinel (rejected because
  it changes simulator budgets); include failed observations and clip them later (rejected because an
  unusable call is not optimization evidence); omit failed-call costs (rejected because spend already
  occurred); and clip negative posterior variance (rejected because impossible uncertainty must be a
  model failure, not false confidence).
- **Evidence:** 13 focused adverse cases cover zero/fractional/Boolean budgets, non-finite and raised
  objective calls, empty usable histories with preserved axes, wrong posterior cardinality, non-PSD
  target covariance, and invalid fidelity covariance. All 20 focused multi-fidelity/stability tests
  and five subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1481, MXR-080-1482, and MXR-080-1483.

## D-0112 · ParEGO preserves declared budgets and Pareto order

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Initial evaluations and candidates require exact positive non-Boolean integer counts;
  adaptive iterations require an exact nonnegative non-Boolean count. All controls are validated even
  when zero iterations are requested. The ParEGO augmentation is a finite nonnegative non-Boolean
  real, weights are finite nonnegative one-dimensional evidence with positive mass, and normalized
  spans plus final scalar scores must remain finite.
- **Alternatives considered:** rely on ``range(int(n_iter))`` coercion (rejected because it silently
  cancels, truncates, or invents evaluations); skip validation for controls unused under a zero
  iteration budget (rejected because the returned run would misrepresent its declared policy); allow
  negative augmentation (rejected because it can rank a dominated point ahead of its dominator); and
  return Inf/NaN scalar scores after finite input overflow (rejected because the optimizer cannot
  order invalid evidence).
- **Evidence:** 15 focused adverse cases cover every count control, zero iterations, negative,
  non-finite, and Boolean augmentation, dominance preservation, and overflowing normalization. All 22
  multi-objective tests and 14 subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1484 and MXR-080-1485.

## D-0113 · Oracle verification is immutable, bounded, and cost-reconciled

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Oracle abstention is an exact Boolean state with a distinct ``-inf`` non-observation
  marker and a required reason/status receipt. Results, recursively frozen copied receipts, candidate
  points, candidate records, and public run history are immutable; runs expose one append-only method.
  Reports return ``no_verified_result`` with nullable best fields for empty/all-abstention evidence.
  Initial and adaptive call budgets are exact, non-Boolean, and tracked separately. Every oracle call
  receives an immutable call ID. Late outcomes reconcile by ID into provisional/settled/unresolved
  cost totals. Non-cooperative timeouts count against a configurable finite worker cap; reaching it
  returns an ``oracle_quarantined`` abstention and stops optimization without creating another thread.
- **Alternatives considered:** truthiness for abstention (rejected because ``"false"`` becomes an
  abstention); shallow copy or mutable dataclasses (rejected because verified history remains
  rewriteable); make all-abstention reports raise (rejected because failure runs most need a report);
  merge call budgets through coerced arithmetic (rejected because negative adaptive work can cancel
  initialization); report known cost as final while late work remains (rejected because it
  understates spend); and create one daemon per timeout indefinitely (rejected because daemon threads
  still consume live resources).
- **Evidence:** 30 focused adverse assertions cover abstention types/schema, nested receipt and result
  immutability, candidate/history immutability, all-abstention reports, initial/adaptive budgets,
  fractional/Boolean/negative controls, bounded non-cooperative workers, circuit receipts, late result
  identity, and provisional-to-settled cost reconciliation. All 61 oracle tests, 15 proposal-loop
  tests, nine oracle subtests, and four focused amplification abstention tests pass. A surfaced
  Gaussian-process integration regression was also reconciled by certifying covariance against an
  infinity-norm roundoff tolerance and projecting only tolerance-scale negative modes to PSD; all 16
  focused GP tests and 21 subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1486 through MXR-080-1491; integration reconciliation for MXR-080-0371.

## D-0114 · Calibration binds predictions to immutable identifiable fitted state

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Calibrated theta, field inputs, observations, residuals, uncertainty arrays, and
  training Cholesky state are copied and frozen; public array properties return detached copies.
  Prediction uses the exact fitted covariance model, including the recorded
  ``noise**2 + _NOISE_VAR_FLOOR`` diagonal term. Duplicate training inputs therefore remain solvable.
  Calibration now computes a relative-coordinate finite-difference simulator Jacobian at the fitted
  theta, publishes its rank, singular values, and condition number, and raises a public
  ``CalibrationIdentifiabilityError`` carrying the null directions when rank is deficient.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain caller arrays and merely document them read-only (rejected
  because mutation makes cached residuals disagree with theta); reconstruct prediction covariance
  without the fit floor (rejected because it changes the likelihood and can be singular); use
  ``n > p`` as identifiability evidence (rejected because a parameter can have zero or redundant
  simulator effect); and return a point with NaN uncertainty on a flat ridge (rejected because an
  arbitrary optimizer location is not a calibrated parameter).
- **Evidence:** four focused adverse tests mutate every caller/public array path, solve a duplicated
  zero-noise design against the exact floored covariance, reject a constant parameter direction with
  its null vector, and verify full-rank sensitivity evidence. All 30 calibration tests and 14 public
  API manifest tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1492, MXR-080-1493, and MXR-080-1494.

## D-0115 · TuRBO admits only verified finite optimization evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Trust-region state is immutable and advances through an exact-Boolean transition that
  returns a new validated state. TuRBO counts every objective call, stops with
  ``objective_failed`` on a non-finite observation, records that call separately, and never admits it
  to ``X``/``Y`` or publishes it as an optimum. Thompson sampling requires a finite candidate matrix,
