  one finite posterior mean per candidate, a finite square symmetric covariance, and exactly the
  requested number of distinct finite picks.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep a mutable dataclass with one-time validation (rejected because
  callers can invalidate geometry after construction); accept truthy improvement signals (rejected
  because strings and integers become evidence); discard or rank NaN/Inf observations (rejected
  because neither is optimization evidence); and flatten/broadcast malformed posterior arrays
  (rejected because that can silently shorten a batch and prevent budget progress).
- **Evidence:** focused tests reject non-Boolean transitions and post-construction mutation, preserve
  the prior immutable state, stop and ledger initial and later non-finite objective calls without
  contaminating verified history, reject malformed/non-finite/asymmetric posterior outputs, and
  require exact distinct batch cardinality. All 31 TuRBO tests and four focused stability tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1495, MXR-080-1496, and MXR-080-1497.

## D-0116 · Joint batch acquisition preserves covariance and finite merit contracts

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** All batch counts use the shared exact non-Boolean integer contract. Joint covariance
  must be finite, square, symmetric within a quantified roundoff tolerance, and PSD within the
  existing numerical tolerance before factorization; accepted roundoff-level asymmetry alone is
  normalized. Zero-scale deterministic covariance is jitter-healed with an absolute diagnostic.
  Monte-Carlo q-EI requires a finite incumbent, exact Boolean optimization sense, and finite draws,
  improvements, and result. Greedy q-EI requires ``q <= n_candidates`` and removes a selected point
  and every exact duplicate from later steps.
- **Alternatives considered:** numeric coercion of Boolean counts (rejected because ``True`` invents
  one sample); unconditional covariance symmetrization (rejected because it replaces model evidence);
  divide diagnostic jitter by a zero scale (invalid); allow overflow to become acquisition merit
  (rejected because candidate order becomes meaningless); and distinguish only candidate row indices
  (rejected because duplicate coordinates are the same experiment).
- **Evidence:** focused adverse tests cover Python/numpy Boolean counts, exact-zero covariance,
  materially asymmetric covariance, non-finite incumbents, non-Boolean direction, overflowing finite
  arithmetic, an impossible batch size, and output uniqueness. All 32 batch tests and five focused
  TuRBO integration tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1498 through MXR-080-1502.

## D-0117 · Active design and information gain publish only certified evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** ALM/ALC require exact finite posterior dimensions, quantified symmetry, and
  positive-semidefinite covariance before scoring; only roundoff-scale negative modes may be
  projected to the PSD boundary. Active-learning objective calls are counted and any non-finite
  observation stops with ``objective_failed`` in a separate failure ledger, never in the learned
  design. Linear EIG certifies the finite design, every derived information-matrix stage, its
  Cholesky factor, and the finite nonnegative result. Nested-Monte-Carlo likelihoods must return exact
  one-dimensional row-aligned vectors; every log evidence, information term, estimate, standard
  error, and interval must be finite. Uncertainty from one outer replicate is explicitly ``None``.
- **Alternatives considered:** clip negative posterior variances or floor ALC denominators (rejected
  because invalid covariance becomes extreme merit); retain NaN responses in a completed design
  (rejected because they are failed calls, not observations); rely on Cholesky alone to discover bad
  design arithmetic (rejected because it gives indirect diagnostics and can admit Inf results);
  flatten likelihood output (rejected because transposed evidence loses row semantics); and publish
  NaN for unavailable one-replicate uncertainty (rejected in favor of an explicit nullable state).
- **Evidence:** focused adverse tests cover malformed, asymmetric, indefinite, and non-finite
  posteriors; initial non-finite objectives; non-finite and overflowing design matrices; transposed
  likelihood batches; finite likelihoods whose subtraction overflows; and explicit single-replicate
  uncertainty. All 57 active-learning/EIG tests and one focused cross-module stability test pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1503 through MXR-080-1507.

## D-0118 · MES fails closed on unrepresentable extrema and malformed posteriors

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Maximum-value sampling validates that its search bracket, standardized values, CDF,
  quantiles, Gumbel parameters, and every returned sample remain finite. Finite inputs whose implied
  bracket is not representable in float64 are rejected explicitly. The MES proposal uses the shared
  exact non-Boolean count validator and joint-covariance validator, requires exact posterior
  mean/covariance dimensions and finite means, nonnegative marginal variance, and an exact Boolean
  optimization sense before entropy scoring.
- **Alternatives considered:** allow overflow to propagate as NaN samples (rejected because NaN is not
  optimum-value evidence); clip an infinite bracket (rejected because it invents a distribution);
  truncate candidate counts with ``int`` (rejected because fractions and Booleans change declared
  work); and derive standard deviations from clipped malformed covariance (rejected because that
  converts invalid model evidence into acquisition merit).
- **Evidence:** focused adverse tests reject the finite ``1e308`` bracket reproduction, fractional and
  Boolean candidate counts, wrong posterior dimensions, asymmetric and indefinite covariance, and
  non-finite posterior means; a valid injected posterior still returns one finite point. All 25 MES
  tests with seven subtests and one focused stability test pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1508 and MXR-080-1509.

## D-0119 · Sequential BO separates failed calls from certified posterior evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** The shared BO posterior boundary requires exact finite dimensions, quantified
  symmetry, and PSD covariance, projecting only roundoff-scale negative modes. Public knowledge
  gradient reuses that boundary, requires finite nonnegative observation noise, handles deterministic
  candidates explicitly, and publishes only finite nonnegative merit. Training ``x`` must be an
  explicit two-dimensional matrix. Sequential optimization validates initialization, iteration, and
  candidate counts as exact non-Boolean integers, counts every objective call, and stops with
  ``objective_failed`` while placing a non-finite call in ``failed_evaluations`` rather than ``x/y``.
  Result status and call evidence propagate through noisy optimization and ask/tell result adapters.
- **Alternatives considered:** clip negative posterior diagonals (rejected because an indefinite
  matrix is not covariance); floor negative KG predictive variance (rejected because it creates large
  false merit); infer whether one-dimensional ``x`` means one multivariate row or many scalar rows
  from ``y`` (rejected as ambiguous); and return a partially finite history containing NaN/Inf
  (rejected because failed calls are not training observations).
- **Evidence:** focused tests cover indefinite/asymmetric covariance, invalid KG mean/noise and finite
  merit, explicit one-dimensional reshaping, a partial initialization failure ledger, and
  negative/fractional/Boolean iteration and candidate counts. All 64 BO tests with 27 subtests and 21
  focused optimizer, robust-incumbent, and constrained-result integration tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1510 through MXR-080-1514.

## D-0120 · Constrained BO preserves constraint geometry and failed-call provenance

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Objective and constraint posterior standard deviations derive only from the shared
  exact finite symmetric PSD posterior boundary. One-dimensional constraint arrays consistently mean
  multiple points under one constraint at every public boundary. Incumbent selection requires finite
  aligned objective and constraint evidence and an exact Boolean optimization sense. Initialization,
  iteration, and candidate counts are exact non-Boolean integers. A non-finite objective or constraint
  call stops the run with ``objective_or_constraint_failed`` and is recorded separately from
  ``x/y/c``; nullable best fields represent a run with no verified observation.
- **Alternatives considered:** clip negative posterior variance (rejected because malformed model
  evidence becomes certainty); use ``atleast_2d`` on a constraint vector (rejected because it swaps
  point and constraint axes); let NaN violation reach ``argmin`` (rejected because it selects an
  arbitrary row); and admit a partially invalid point to any model history (rejected because one
  point's objective/constraint record is an atomic observation).
- **Evidence:** focused adverse tests reject indefinite covariance and non-finite incumbent evidence,
  accept the natural one-vector single-constraint form, reject negative/fractional/Boolean budgets,
  and ledger a non-finite constraint call outside all learned histories. All 18 constrained BO tests
  pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1515 through MXR-080-1518.

## D-0121 · Ask/tell controls and proposal identity are exact

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Ask/tell construction requires an exact Boolean optimization sense, finite
  nonnegative ``xi`` and recognized acquisition margins, and exact positive non-Boolean
  initialization/candidate counts. ``ask(q)`` applies the same exact count contract before any state
  mutation. Pending proposals are resolved by exact binary64 coordinate identity, not a relative
  ``allclose`` comparison whose acceptance width grows with coordinate magnitude.
- **Alternatives considered:** defer validation until GP acquisition begins (rejected because invalid
  configuration can operate through the whole initial phase); coerce flags/counts (rejected because
  strings, Booleans, and fractions silently change policy); retain default ``allclose`` (rejected
  because points thousands apart near ``1e9`` can share an identity); and add approximate matching
  without proposal tokens (rejected because any tolerance can merge distinct valid designs).
- **Evidence:** focused tests reject string/integer/numpy-Boolean senses, NaN/Inf/negative acquisition
  margins, invalid candidate and batch counts, and NaN acquisition kwargs. A two-proposal large-value
  reproduction resolves the exact reported proposal and leaves the other pending. All 30 focused
  ask/tell, validation, and initial-boundary tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1519 and MXR-080-1520.

## D-0122 · Amplification binds claims to independent, sufficient evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Amplification validates exact budgets, polynomial degree, pool capacity, and a finite
  significance level strictly inside ``(0,1)`` before any oracle call. Search, baseline, permutation,
  and round-two pool use distinct concrete RNG streams recorded in the report. Baseline and round two
  assert exact matched call counts, and round-two candidates exclude round-one coordinates. Statistical
  inference uses only genuine results and requires at least two per group; reports expose effective
  sample sizes. Student fitting requires enough genuine rows, full design rank, and finite data and
  coefficients. Missing round evidence is explicit through nullable scores/p-values. Constrained
  acquisition additionally requires exactly one score per candidate before feasibility weighting and
  at least one finite final merit.
- **Alternatives considered:** restart baseline and search at the same seed (rejected because their
  first points can be identical); slice an undersized pool (rejected because it silently spends less
  than the matched budget); run invalid alpha levels (rejected because they predetermine the gate);
  treat abstention markers as numeric samples (rejected because they are non-observations); fit an
  underdetermined student (rejected because arbitrary coefficients are not captured knowledge); and
  rely on NumPy broadcasting/``argmax`` for acquisition output (rejected because malformed or NaN
  merit can win).
- **Evidence:** focused tests verify four distinct receipted streams and non-repeated points, zero
  oracle calls under invalid policy/pool capacity, exact baseline spend, abstention filtering,
  identifiable fit requirements, explicit insufficient-sample reports, deterministic round-two
  budget/disjointness, and malformed constrained acquisition rejection. Sixteen focused amplification
  tests and six constrained-evidence tests pass in two capped subsets.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1521 through MXR-080-1525.

## D-0123 · Compute-engine discovery is complete, bounded, and type-safe

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Arithmetic dispatch discovers engine ownership jointly across positional arguments
  and keyword values before invoking an operation. Array-type registration requires an actual type
  and ``ComputeEngine``, forbids catch-all ``object``, and requires ``override=True`` for a collision.
  Engine discovery and ownership inspection track the active container path and reject cycles with a
  stable boundary error; conversion therefore cannot recurse indefinitely. Every required operation
  on a concrete engine class must be callable, not merely non-``None``.
- **Alternatives considered:** inspect positional arguments only (rejected because keyword tensors
  silently route to the host); accept arbitrary global registry entries (rejected because strings and
  ``object`` corrupt process-wide dispatch); use a global visited set (rejected because repeated
  acyclic subcontainers are valid); preserve arbitrary cyclic structures during array conversion
  (rejected because no backend-neutral conversion contract defines them); and defer non-callable ops
  to kernel execution (rejected because engine conformance should fail at class definition).
- **Evidence:** focused tests dispatch a keyword Torch tensor correctly, reject mixed keyword/position
  engines, reject invalid/catch-all/colliding registrations while permitting explicit override,
  reject cyclic discovery/conversion, and reject a concrete engine with ``log=42``. Nine focused
  engine tests and two operation-contract tests with three subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1526 through MXR-080-1529.

## D-0124 · Precision sampling and numeric codecs enforce their advertised evidence bounds

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Automatic precision sampling treats ``sample_size`` as one global scalar-work budget,
  distributes that budget across nested observations, and uses a duplicate-free integer schedule that
  includes both ends of every sampled finite sequence. Numeric-format widths and counts require exact
  non-Boolean integers; error tolerances require finite positive scalars. Fixed-point encoding rejects
  non-finite inputs, exposes a finite half-step bound only as ``in_range_max_abs_error``, and reports
  its universal saturation-aware ``max_abs_error`` as infinite. Codebooks are copied, one-dimensional,
  finite, sorted, and read-only; indices and compressed wide payloads are exact, bounded, aligned, and
  capacity checked before decoding.
- **Alternatives considered:** flatten every selected observation (rejected because a single nested
  array defeats the sampling budget); use floating-point stride positions (rejected because the last
  observation can be omitted); coerce widths and counts with ``int`` (rejected because Booleans and
  fractions silently change the format); keep claiming a finite universal fixed-point error (rejected
  because saturation error grows without bound); alias caller codebooks (rejected because later caller
  mutation changes the codec); and rely on NumPy casts/indexing for payload validation (rejected
  because they truncate, wrap, accept negative indices, and ignore short payloads).
- **Evidence:** focused regressions cover the 513-row endpoint case, a million-scalar nested
  observation under a budget of one, invalid format controls and non-finite fixed-point values,
  saturation-aware bounds, immutable caller-independent codebooks, invalid indices, and malformed
  wide payloads. All 60 automatic-precision and numeric-format tests pass; all 53 packing,
  error-tracing, and spectrum integration tests plus 23 subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1530 through MXR-080-1535.

## D-0125 · Packed binary arithmetic admits only canonical, unambiguous representations

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Generic bit-packing widths and payload bytes require exact non-Boolean integers, with
  every payload byte inside ``[0,255]`` before conversion. Binary source values must belong wholly to
  either ``{-1,+1}`` or ``{0,1}``; their incompatible three-value union is not an alphabet. Public
  source packing and binary dot products accept only vectors and matrices, normalize vectors to one
  row, and reject all other ranks. Packed GEMM validates exact uint64 words, the word count implied by
  the true dimension, and zero-valued trailing padding bits. The Python boundary and direct Cython
  binary kernel both enforce canonical padding before popcount.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain equality-membership checks (rejected because ``True == 1``);
  cast arbitrary payloads to uint8/uint64 (rejected because fractions truncate and negative/large
  values wrap); accept ``{-1,0,1}`` in the binary packer (rejected because zero and negative-one then
  become the same bit); subtract a nominal dimension after counting dirty padding (rejected because
  results can leave the mathematical dot-product range); and let rank errors emerge from indexing or
  broadcasting (rejected because the public data model supports exactly vectors and matrices).
- **Evidence:** focused regressions reject Boolean widths, fractional/negative/oversized/Boolean
  payload bytes, mixed binary alphabets, scalar/rank-three sources, and the one-dimensional all-ones
  padding corruption reproduction. Canonical products at dimensions 1, 7, 9, 63, and 65 remain exact.
  All 32 packing and bitpacked tests plus six subtests pass; eight compiled-runtime tests are skipped
  because the optional extension is not built, while Cython 3.2.8 translates the edited kernel
  successfully. All 34 numeric-format integration tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1536 through MXR-080-1539.

## D-0126 · Affine evidence is immutable and endpoint precision allocation is explicitly heuristic

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Every affine center and coefficient must be finite and shape-compatible at
  construction. The form owns copies of all arrays, freezes them, and exposes coefficients through a
  read-only mapping. Uncertain forms reject non-finite radii before assigning a noise symbol.
  ``allocate_precision`` remains a useful low-cost dtype recommendation, but its statuses are now
  ``heuristic_fit`` and ``heuristic_insufficient``. ``heuristic_met_target`` exposes the estimate's
  comparison, while ``met_target`` is always false because endpoint magnitude and operation count do
  not constitute a propagated IEEE error proof.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain caller arrays/dictionaries (rejected because evidence changes
  after issuance); silently drop NaN radius terms (rejected because invalid uncertainty becomes exact);
  continue calling ``op_count*u*final_magnitude`` a certificate (rejected because it omits
  intermediate magnitudes, conditioning, cancellation, reduction order, underflow, and overflow); and
  remove the allocator entirely (rejected because its recommendation remains useful when labeled
  honestly).
- **Evidence:** focused regressions reject NaN/Inf centers and radii and incompatible coefficients;
  caller mutation cannot change center or radius and exposed state is read-only. The exact zero-center,
  trillion-operation reproduction may select a heuristic fit but cannot claim the target was met. All
  35 affine and interval error-tracing tests plus nine subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1540 and MXR-080-1541.

## D-0127 · Summation escalation preserves source precision and never certifies overflow

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Float64 summation accuracy requires finite inputs, a finite computed sum, and a finite
  error bound; overflow always returns false. Its enclosure becomes the sound whole real line when
  finite evidence cannot produce finite endpoints. ``accurate_sum`` materializes source scalars
  without converting them, uses float64/double-double only when every source value is exactly admitted
  by that boundary, and passes Decimal, string, large-integer, and backend-native values unchanged to
  arbitrary precision. A high-precision result outside the API's float64 value range returns
  ``status="overflow"`` with an infinite bound. ``sum_certificate`` similarly returns an explicit
  uncertified overflow record rather than NaN fields. Public ``fpN`` and integer spellings now use one
  exact non-Boolean total-width unit; ``HighPrecisionFormat(256)`` is named ``fp256`` and derives its
  MPFR significand width from that total.
- **Alternatives considered:** accept ``inf <= inf`` as success (rejected because it proves nothing);
  coerce source evidence to float64 before backend selection (rejected because escalation cannot
  recover destroyed digits); return a NaN relative bound with ``status="ok"`` (rejected because
  comparisons against NaN fail open); keep total-width spectrum labels but interpret the same number
  as an MPFR mantissa width (rejected because names and computation then describe different formats);
  and let NumPy integer widths fall through to dtype interpretation (rejected because equivalent
  public spellings route differently).
- **Evidence:** the finite ``[1e308,1e308]`` reproduction now returns an overflow result and an
  uncertified certificate with infinite bounds. The 80-digit-context Decimal cancellation reproduction
  returns the preserved ``1e-34`` result through MPFR. Boolean/fractional widths are rejected, NumPy
  integer widths route normally, and requested high-precision names are exact. All 51 spectrum,
  high-precision, and error-tracing tests plus 25 subtests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1542 through MXR-080-1544.

## D-0128 · Double-double values are normalized immutable pairs and large finite products fail closed

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** ``DoubleDouble`` broadcasts then normalizes caller components with an error-free sum,
  rejects non-finite or unrepresentable pairs, owns both resulting arrays, and freezes them. All
  reductions require finite source values. The pure-NumPy product transform power-of-two scales large
  operands before Veltkamp splitting, rejects product overflow and unrepresentable underflow, and
  refuses any non-finite residual. This makes the non-overlap invariant a construction guarantee
  rather than a caller promise.
- **Alternatives considered:** retain arbitrary ``hi/lo`` pairs (rejected because arithmetic accuracy
  assumes non-overlap); retain caller arrays (rejected because represented values can change after
  construction); preserve a prose caveat for large values (rejected because a public finite dot product
  returned NaN); use an ordinary unscaled split (rejected because the splitter itself overflows); and
  silently return non-finite transforms (rejected because downstream accuracy claims cannot distinguish
  failure from evidence).
- **Evidence:** direct construction normalizes ``(0,1e308)`` to a finite canonical pair, later caller
  mutation has no effect, both limbs are read-only, and non-finite/unrepresentable pairs are rejected.
  ``two_prod([1e308],[1])`` and ``dd_dot([1e308],[1])`` are finite and exact at float64 collapse. All 38
  extended-precision and spectrum tests plus 23 subtests pass; one optional compiled-kernel test is
  skipped. All 12 high-precision integration tests plus two subtests pass; the compiled-kernel module
  is unavailable and explicitly skipped.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1545 and MXR-080-1546.

## D-0129 · Quantized nonlinearities and LNS arithmetic preserve finite, canonical evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Quantized functions require a callable that returns exactly one finite output per grid
  code; direct lookup accepts only exact in-range integer codes. LSE bit counts are exact non-Boolean
  integers. Weighted quantized LSE divides finite weights by their maximum before histogram
  accumulation and restores the scale in log space, with finite positive mass required before issuing
  a value or bound. Table sizing shares construction's finite ordered-grid validation and requires an
  exact positive item size. LNS construction requires a finite positive step. Finite values outside its
  code range are rejected rather than saturated, so published rounding bounds apply to every accepted
  value. All public code operations require canonical exact codes, preserve the zero sentinel, saturate
  derived ordinary results to the declared range, and reject empty reductions. Log-softmax preserves
  impossible classes as ``-inf`` and rejects all-impossible slices.
- **Alternatives considered:** broadcast a scalar callback result (rejected because it hides a callback
  contract error); clip direct LUT codes (rejected because caller corruption becomes a valid boundary
  value); accumulate raw weights linearly (rejected because finite mass can overflow); include an
  unspecified saturation error in a constant LNS certificate (rejected because it is data dependent
  and unbounded); clamp malformed public codes (rejected because invalid state survives); and normalize
  an all-sentinel slice to uniform probability (rejected because zero mass has no probability
  distribution).
- **Evidence:** focused regressions reject scalar callback output, fractional/out-of-range/Boolean
  codes, Boolean LSE width, invalid table grids/item sizes, NaN step, out-of-range finite LNS inputs,
  invalid public codes, and empty axes. Weights ``[1e308,1e308]`` produce a finite weighted LSE and
  finite bound. Boundary logadd remains canonical; one impossible softmax class has probability zero,
  while an all-impossible slice raises. All 82 LUT, LNS, and LNS-neural tests pass. All 13 LNS-kernel
  and unified-quantizer integration tests plus 17 subtests pass; one optional native-kernel module is
  unavailable and explicitly skipped.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1547 through MXR-080-1552.

## D-0130 · Sum-product circuits own one immutable, rank-exact DAG

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Circuit construction canonicalizes the node sequence to a tuple, every child sequence
  to a tuple of exact non-Boolean integer indices, every log-weight vector to a finite one-dimensional
  tuple with exact child cardinality, and leaf identifiers to a recursively immutable value. The
  circuit exposes a frozen leaf-ID set and retains no mutable structural object supplied by its caller.
- **Alternatives considered:** validate then retain the original graph (rejected because later mutation
  bypasses every DAG invariant); accept Booleans as child indices (rejected because truth values are
  policy data, not node identity); validate weights only by ``len`` (rejected because a rank-two array
  broadcasts into a vector-valued circuit); and deep-copy arbitrary hashable leaf objects (rejected
  because a copy is not an immutability guarantee).
- **Evidence:** focused regressions reject Boolean children and a ``(1,1)`` weight array. Mutating the
  original node list, child list, and weight array after construction cannot change the canonical
  circuit or its scalar output. All 33 LNS-neural circuit and probability tests pass.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1553 and MXR-080-1554.

## D-0131 · Heterogeneous precision planning fails closed without workload-specific accuracy evidence

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** Workers copy precision capabilities to tuples and require nonempty names, modeled
  device/precision pairs, and finite positive non-Boolean throughput. Assignments validate precision,
  rows, throughput, Boolean flags, finite nonnegative estimates, and an evidence kind. A heuristic
  assignment can never set ``meets_target=True``. Plans require a nonempty tuple of valid assignments
  and the exact reduction depth implied by worker count, including depth zero for one worker. The
  planner requires actual Booleans for ``allow_infeasible``, rejects Boolean targets, and uses
  ``gamma_n=n*u/(1-n*u)`` only to rank formats. Because it has no operation graph, magnitude,
  conditioning, reduction-order, or communication evidence, every constrained plan fails closed by
  default. Explicit best effort returns ``evidence_kind="heuristic"`` and remains infeasible even when
  its score falls below the target.
- **Alternatives considered:** parse truthy strings as policy (rejected because ``"false"`` enables the
  unsafe path); call ``n*u`` a bound (rejected because it understates even the elementary gamma factor);
  call gamma-n a generic workload certificate (rejected because the planner lacks the assumptions and
  graph needed to apply it); retain mutable capability lists (rejected because frozen dataclasses do
  not freeze members); accept directly forged output records (rejected because receipts are evidence);
  and report one reduction stage for a single worker (rejected because no inter-worker reduction exists).
- **Evidence:** focused regressions reject string fail-open policy, Boolean targets/throughput, bogus
  precisions, string flags, NaN estimates, heuristic success claims, empty plans, and invalid reduction
  depths. Caller mutation cannot add a worker capability. A million-operation float32 score exceeds
  ``0.06`` under gamma-n and remains explicitly uncertified. All 31 heterogeneous-planning tests plus
  14 subtests pass. Executor integration reached 35 passing tests/subtests before the sandbox denied
  the unrelated OS semaphore query required by its one real-process test.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0139
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1555 through MXR-080-1558.

## D-0132 · Second consolidated manifest reconciliation for provisional-tier growth, on the same terms as D-0026

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Decision:** `manifests/api_manifest.json` has drifted again, in exactly the shape D-0026 anticipated
  and on the same four terms: every line is additive (`+[...] -[]` — nothing removed, renamed, or
  re-signatured), every symbol is provisional-tier, and every symbol traces to a specific audit-fix
  commit rather than to new feature work. Reconciling it in one commit, as D-0026 established, rather
  than filing ten per-symbol freeze exceptions.
- **Drift, with the commit each symbol entered on:**
  - `mixle.analysis` +`FrechetApproximationDiagnostic`, `GaussianObservationModel`, `SmithMaxStableFit`,
    `VoiEstimate`, `voi_estimate` (`a59c5460`, audit-fix work left uncommitted by interrupted runs);
    +`TransitionRiskResult` (`95c1e49c`, MXR-080-1575/1576/1585/1588/1595/1430/1580); +`WaterBudget`
    (`35a34601`, MXR-080-1583/1584).
  - `mixle.epistemic` +`mmd_squared` (`b3feffdb`, MMD sample-geometry canonicalization).
  - `mixle.represent` +`AutoencoderFitError` (`2579a8ce`, returning an autoencoder result only for a fit
    that actually trained).
  - `mixle.reason` +`posterior_ambiguity` (`20be46ca`). This one is not audit-fix fallout but a
    deliberate API split: `cycle_inconsistency` had been redefined from posterior dispersion to
    round-trip closure, which is anti-correlated with ambiguity on a collapsing observation map and so
    inverted the F5 abstention signal. The two questions now have two functions. Naming the new one is
    what keeps `cycle_inconsistency`'s closure contract intact.
- **Scope:** none of these are stable-tier. The freeze rule's purpose — no silent stable-surface change
  before release — is not engaged.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** manifest regeneration only; the underlying fixes are already committed and tested.

## D-0133 · The repository carries no direct dataset usage

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** mixle fetches, embeds, and pins no external datasets anywhere in the repository:
  no example downloads data, no test loads a public dataset, no CI lane or release artifact depends
  on one, and the `datasets` package is not a dependency of any extra. Real-data demonstrations
  live in notebooks outside this repository, where a dataset download is an explicit interactive
  act rather than repository behavior. Model-weight assets (CLIP, language models, sentence
  encoders, PEFT adapters) are unaffected and remain full-revision pinned.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep the pinned Banking77/Adult/sunspots flagships as release
  evidence (rejected by the release owner: dataset handling does not belong in the library repo);
  keep the examples but gate them off CI (rejected: unexercised dataset code is worse than none).
- **Evidence:** the removal commit deletes five dataset examples, the vision-distillation
  directory, seven dataset-driven test modules, the hosted-network bundle entry, three CI lanes,
  the publish-time receipt attachment, and the `datasets` dependency; all release gates re-run
  green after the cut.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit b81b304f9e183c0c386ac236850a93f20c4d8257
- **Expiration:** permanent policy; revisit only with an explicit owner decision.
- **Worklist:** G0.2

## D-0134 · Every declared dependency floor must be installable and importable

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** Fourteen optional-extra floors are raised to the earliest version that installs and
  imports on every supported interpreter and platform: pyarrow>=16, pymongo>=4.6,
  safetensors>=0.4.1, lightning>=2.4, scikit-learn>=1.4.2, passagemath-symbolics>=10.5.49,
  pandas>=2.2.2, h5py>=3.11, jax>=0.4.38, zarr>=3.0, numpyro>=0.16, datasets>=3.0 (since removed
  entirely by D-0133), fsspec>=2023.5.0, gmpy2>=2.2. The extras resolver matrix pins each profile
  to its declared floor and imports it, so a floor that is a fiction fails CI rather than a user.
- **Alternatives considered:** leave floors aspirational (rejected: ten of them could not install
  at all and four more crashed on import — every one was a guaranteed failure for a user running
  `pip install mixle[extra]` at the declared minimum).
- **Evidence:** each old floor reproduced failing and each new floor verified importing, locally
  and in the extras matrix, which is green across all 22 profiles.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** floors move forward as the ecosystem does; re-verify at 0.9.0.
- **Worklist:** S13.4

## D-0135 · Test-tier budgets are re-baselined to measured need

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** The core tier budget moves from 240 to 720 seconds and the full tier from 1200 to
  2400, in the runner's reviewed ceilings, the workflow flags, the enclosing job deadlines, the
  marker text, and the tiers document together. Budgets remain hard kills, priced at roughly twice
  the fastest observed completion so they trip on multiplicative regressions, not on runner draw.
- **Alternatives considered:** trim the suite to the old budgets (rejected for 0.8.0: the suite
  grew ~10k→15k tests through deliberate hardening; the budgets were killing lanes at 61% and 94%
  executed with zero failing tests, presenting as opaque teardown errors); a first re-baseline at
  ~1.2x median (480/1800) failed the same day — the same tier on the same interpreter completed in
  342s on one hosted runner and was killed at 480s on another, a 1.4x runner-variance spread that
  makes any near-median budget a per-lane coin flip.
- **Evidence:** fastest observed completions 342s (core) and 1279s (full); the 480s and 1800s kills
  with zero failing tests; the tiers doc's own rule is honored — the budget did not silently become
  a target, this is a stated re-measurement with the variance evidence recorded.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** superseded — D-0142
- **Expiration:** re-measure whenever the suite grows or shrinks materially.
- **Worklist:** T3.1

## D-0136 · Coarsening backs off to the largest feasible merge prefix

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** When the composed depth-merge candidate breaches the divergence budget, `coarsen`
  drops accepted merges from the end (largest feasible prefix) instead of rolling every merge back
  to the identity. Asking for a looser budget can no longer yield a strictly worse artifact than a
  tighter one when a feasible subset of the work exists.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep all-or-nothing rollback (rejected: made compression non-monotone
  in `budget` — ratio 1.0000 at budget 0.3 where 0.1 achieved 0.9946, dealt to different rungs on
  different platforms, which forced the ladder harness to tolerate halts it should never see).
- **Evidence:** budget sweep 0.05→1.0 on the ladder workload is monotone non-increasing
  (26832→26400 params); two regression tests pin the sweep and the identity-instead-of-prefix case.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none (behavioral contract).
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-2015

## D-0137 · Optimization-budget defaults must fit the estimator's own samples

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** `MatchingEstimator`'s `max_steps` default moves from 500 to 5000. Non-convergence
  raises, so a default that cannot fit data drawn from the estimator's own distribution family is a
  guaranteed `RuntimeError`, not a conservative setting. The loop breaks on convergence, so the
  larger ceiling costs nothing when unneeded.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep 500 and document (rejected: measured need at tol=1e-7 is 164 /
  869 / 1015 / 1172 iterations for n=3/4/5/8 — the default failed every dimension above three).
- **Evidence:** measured iteration counts above; regression tests pin the round trip at n=3/4/5;
  the gallery example runs on plain defaults.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-2016

## D-0138 · Categorical constructors admit unnormalized maps with a derived normalization flag

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** Recorded retroactively (the change shipped during the audit-fix waves without a log
  entry, which this entry corrects). `CategoricalDistribution` and the integer variant accept
  unnormalized non-negative weight maps and expose a derived `is_normalized_probability`; sampling
  from an unnormalized law and `scoring_only` composition remain refused. This deliberately
  reverses D-0029's constructor simplex enforcement, which broke legitimate constructions the
  library itself produces (the dominant guard-overreach defect class of this release).
- **Alternatives considered:** keep strict simplex enforcement (rejected in code at the enforcing
  commit, which the source cites as having broken callers).
- **Evidence:** `mixle/stats/univariate/discrete/categorical.py` and `integer_categorical.py`
  document and test the admitted forms; generative-mixture component rejection is unchanged.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1841

## D-0139 · meets_target is a numeric statement; certification is a separate axis

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** Recorded retroactively (shipped without a log entry, corrected here). A worker
  assignment's `meets_target` states whether the chosen format's modeled roundoff at the plan's
  operation count lands at or below the target; `evidence_kind` / `is_certified` state whether the
  planner can certify that number. A `"heuristic"` assignment may therefore legitimately report
  `meets_target=True` while remaining uncertified. This supersedes D-0131's coupling, under which a
  heuristic assignment could never set `meets_target=True`.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep the coupled semantics (rejected: it conflated "the number meets
  the bar" with "we can certify the number", losing information in both directions).
- **Evidence:** `mixle/engines/heterogeneous.py` documents the split at the dataclass and validates
  both axes; unmodeled formats (fp8) still can never meet a target.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-0134

## D-0140 · Numerical-agreement guards are scaled to the loosest supported arithmetic

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** Guards that compare two computations of the same quantity are bounded by the
  precision the library actually supports, not by fixed constants: the HMM responsibility-mass
  identity scales by sqrt(mass) at float32 roundoff; directional scatter symmetry is
  tolerance-checked and symmetrized rather than bit-exact; quantized-parity and analytic-distill
  residuals scale with float32 resolution and output magnitude; the selective-scan capability
  receipt is best-of-three seeds. Genuine corruption (percent-scale disagreement) is still refused
  everywhere.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep fixed bounds (rejected: each was tighter than the arithmetic it
  measured — rejecting float32 engines the library documents, failing on BLAS reassociation across
  platforms, and turning capability receipts into measurements of the runner's BLAS).
- **Evidence:** each guard's measured failure and fix live in the 2026-08-04 commits and their
  regression tests (MXR-080-2011 and the parity/symmetry/receipt changes).
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-2011

## D-0141 · The SBOM is a blocking gate bound to the exact audited artifact

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** Recorded retroactively (shipped during the audit-fix waves without a log entry,
  corrected here). SBOM generation is blocking, not best-effort: the CycloneDX inventory is bound
  to the exact wheel under audit (filename, SHA-256, candidate commit) by `scripts/bind_sbom.py`,
  upload failures fail the job (`if-no-files-found: error`), and an unpublished candidate that
  pip-audit cannot resolve is synthesized from wheel metadata and marked `mixle_component_audited:
  false` rather than waved through. This supersedes D-0015's best-effort, non-blocking SBOM.
- **Alternatives considered:** require the pip-audit inventory to contain mixle (rejected: made the
  gate unsatisfiable for the first release of any package, exactly when an SBOM matters most).
- **Evidence:** `scripts/bind_sbom.py`, `mixle/tests/release_evidence_contract_test.py`'s bound-SBOM
  contract tests, and the Security workflow's blocking steps, all green.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** MXR-080-1880

## D-0142 · Full-tier budget corrected after an evidence misread (supersedes D-0135 for the full tier)

- **Date:** 2026-08-04
- **Decision:** The full tier is SPLIT across four hosted runners by a deterministic file-hash
  shard hook in conftest (`--num-shards`/`--shard-id`; disjoint and exhaustive by construction,
  file granularity so fixtures and xdist locality are untouched), each shard under a 2700-second
  budget, with the 45 percent coverage floor enforced by a combine job on the union of the four
  shards' data -- the same measurement the old single job made. The tier ceiling stays 5400 for
  any single-runner invocation. D-0135's core-tier re-baseline stands unchanged and reaffirmed --
  every core lane on both platforms passed inside 720 seconds on the run that exposed this.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep one runner and raise the budget to ~5400 (rejected: a
  fifty-five-minute required lane, and the release owner set a fifteen-minute wall-time target for
  the suite); a timing-balanced split via stored durations (deferred to 0.9.0 -- the hash split
  balances within ~16 percent by test count, which the per-shard budget absorbs).
- **Evidence:** D-0135 priced the full tier off "fastest observed completion 1279s", and that
  number was a MISREAD: the 1279-second summary line belonged to the optional tier (1105 tests),
  not full. The full tier -- roughly fourteen thousand tests under coverage on 4-vCPU hosted
  runners -- has never completed inside any budget this cycle. The only genuine datum is the
  2026-08-04 kill at 2400 seconds with 72% executed, projecting roughly 3300 seconds; 5400 is
  ~1.6x that projection. This entry exists because the log is append-only and D-0135's evidence
  paragraph cannot be edited: the error is corrected by superseding, not by rewriting history.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (verdicts AI-verified, owner-reviewed and approved); 2026-08-04; commit 1da0b9d9b9a7729247c3b514494ec9577bf598dc
- **Expiration:** re-measure when the full tier first completes on a hosted runner; record the real number then.
- **Worklist:** T3.1

## D-0143 · Selective-risk certification uses a pre-specified grid with Bonferroni correction

- **Date:** 2026-08-05
- **Decision:** Both selective-risk implementations (`mixle/task/calibrate.py`,
  `mixle/reason/llm.py`) certify confidence thresholds from a 1001-point evenly spaced grid fixed
  in code, testing each grid point's answered slice with an exact Clopper-Pearson upper bound at
  Bonferroni level `delta/1001`, and serve the loosest certified threshold. The certified
  threshold persists in the artifact alongside the conformal one (`tau` next to `qhat`, canonical
  `"inf"` sentinel included), selective-only serving is supported (argmax label under the `tau`
  gate when `qhat` is unset), both calibration routes share one fail-closed input validator, the
  advertised alpha domain is the open interval, and correctness evidence must be Boolean or 0/1
  integers.
- **Alternatives considered:** keep testing data-derived candidate thresholds (rejected: the
  external re-review's STAT-R2 -- the cited Learn-Then-Test fixed-sequence proof requires a
  pre-specified hypothesis family, and candidates drawn from the sample that tests them re-open
  the selection effect the correction exists to close); an independent selection/testing split
  (statistically valid, rejected for this release: it halves the effective calibration set and
  adds a split-seed degree of freedom); reducing the claim to a heuristic (rejected by the
  release owner -- the answered-slice guarantee is the feature).
- **Evidence:** commit 9d8c3b9b87dec66db5c4ce2b24c5647faecac697: regression tests replay the
  reviewer's falsifying probes (a tau=0.9 abstention surviving save/load, selective-only serving,
  malformed matrices rejected on both routes, alpha endpoints, string correctness evidence), the
  fresh-draw risk bound holds on the adversarial two-cluster population (measured tau 0.667,
  fresh-draw slice error 0.075 at alpha=0.10), and the certified threshold is pinned to be a grid
  point rather than an observed confidence.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fix directed by the owner in response to the external re-review, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-05; commit 9d8c3b9b87dec66db5c4ce2b24c5647faecac697
- **Expiration:** revisit if a proof for rank-based candidates is ever supplied or the 0.001 grid
  granularity becomes a measured limitation.
- **Worklist:** external statistical review (re-review findings STAT-R1..R5).

## D-0145 · Serving validates one probability pass; malformed output raises, impossible evidence escalates

- **Date:** 2026-08-05
- **Decision:** Every adapter-probability ingestion -- serving included, not just calibration --
  enforces the matrix contract (shape, finiteness, [0, 1] range, row-stochastic sums) inside
  `CalibratedTaskModel._proba`, and each decision batch makes exactly ONE validated probability
  pass that carries an impossible-row mask shared by the candidate sets, the `tau` comparison,
  and the escalation flags. The two failure kinds are deliberately distinct: a malformed matrix
  RAISES (the adapter broke its contract; escalating would disguise a broken adapter as model
  uncertainty), while impossible structured evidence ESCALATES (it is data the calibrated
  contract must survive). The LLM route's correctness callback accepts only Booleans and exact
  0/1 integers, and both selective-risk implementations advertise the open-interval alpha domain.
- **Alternatives considered:** escalate on malformed matrices (rejected: the repair-pass review's
  NaN probe showed `top < tau` fails OPEN, and mapping contract breaches to escalation converts a
  defect into an invisible cost increase); validate only in the selective path (rejected: the
  conformal path consumes the same adapter output); trust the callback contract and document it
  (rejected: `bool("false")` is True -- the certified threshold 0.0 the reviewer produced is the
  guarantee inverting silently).
- **Evidence:** the repair-pass review's installed-wheel probes (STAT-NEW1..NEW4), reproduced as
  regression tests: malformed live matrices raise in both serving modes,
  `batch_decide(['good', 'bad'])` returns `['a', ESCALATE]` instead of crashing, string and
  fractional verdicts raise, endpoint alpha raises. Commits
  f85697f8d5549557a575b43493ea7beafa7110bd and c74d1842fab82af62027dc6a0b36066c8b589ae2.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fix directed by the owner in response to the external repair-pass review, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-05; commit f85697f8d5549557a575b43493ea7beafa7110bd
- **Expiration:** none -- the contract is pinned by the serving-validation regression tests.
- **Worklist:** external statistical review (repair-pass findings STAT-NEW1..NEW4).

## Review record · 2026-08-04 resolution campaign

All 132 entries above (D-0001–D-0132) were resolved on 2026-08-04. What this record is, stated
plainly so no later reader mistakes it:

- **Method.** Six parallel AI review agents verified every decision against the tree at commit
  `aba8d3bdf661b183d864e51e802163546db405c1` — mechanism existence, evidence consistency, and
  contradiction by later work — with targeted execution probes where cheap. The full per-decision
  verdict record was then reviewed by the release owner, who approved it and directed its
  application. Verdicts: 118 hold and are accepted; 14 are superseded, each pointing at the entry
  that superseded it. Two of those supersessions (D-0029 → D-0138, D-0131 → D-0139) record
  deliberate in-code reversals that had shipped without a log entry, and one (D-0073 → D-0086)
  records the publication pipeline's third phase; nine new entries (D-0133–D-0141) were written so
  that no pointer targets a decision that was never logged.
- **Attribution.** The reviewer of record is the release owner, on the strength of reviewing and
  approving the AI-produced verdict record. The verification labor was performed by Claude, an AI,
  which across sessions also implemented many of the decisions under review. This is a
  human-approved AI review, not the fully independent human review the gate's original text
  contemplated; the release owner chose to close the gate this way, and that choice, its scope, and
  its residual risk are what this record preserves.
- **Unresolved by this campaign.** Nothing — every entry is accepted or superseded. Entries whose
  pinned evidence counts have drifted (signature/manifest totals, probe counts) were accepted on
  their verified mechanisms; the counts are dated snapshots by the log's own append-only design.

## D-0146 · The notebook corpus is implementer usability evidence, not the independent-human gate

- **Date:** 2026-08-06
- **Decision:** The 131-notebook `mixle-notebooks` corpus, re-executed clean against
  `release/0.8.0`, is recorded as evidence that the public API is usable end to end across seven
  domains. It does NOT discharge the E14 external-use-case or independent-review gates, which stay
  `EXTERNAL`. The release owner authored the notebooks and is mixle's sole author, so this is the
  implementer exercising his own library through his own examples -- the same standing already
  recorded for the license-provenance record and the decision-review campaign.
- **Alternatives considered:** count the corpus as the external use case (rejected: the gate's
  entire content is that someone other than the implementer brings their own problem and their own
  data, and author-written notebooks encode what the author already knows, so they cannot measure
  whether an unfamiliar user reaches a first fit); treat it as worthless because it is not
  independent (rejected: it empirically found eight library defects a real user would hit, which is
  exactly the "blockers" the gate asks about, and discarding that evidence would be as inaccurate
  as overstating it).
- **Evidence:** full corpus re-execution 2026-08-06 against mixle `fcccc0b9`, one notebook per
  kernel from its own directory, 131/131 clean; recorded in
  `mixle-notebooks/docs/notebook-execution-manifest.rst` at `af63329`. The eight library defects it
  surfaced -- `keys=` unusable for the LDA/pLSI/PCFG families, `BernoulliSetDistribution` and
  `MarkovChainDistribution` unpicklable at every protocol (no Spark, Dask, multiprocessing or
  checkpoint round-trip), the automatic type-detection path building models the library then
  refused, and others -- are fixed in mixle at 8d075432, a12c28cc, e00b1972, 662052f7 and
  fcccc0b9, each with a regression test and a negative control.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (authorship of the corpus is the reason the gate stays open; disposition recorded rather than waived); 2026-08-06; commit af6332975fbb350deacd31a970d47fc739391e36
- **Expiration:** superseded when a real external user exercises the candidate on their own
  problem, which is what the E14 use-case gate is waiting for.
- **Worklist:** E14.4 (external use case), E14.2/E14.3 (independent review).

## D-0147 · Repair-review gaps closed; adverse tests now accompany every guard change

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The five findings of the 2026-08-07 adversarial repair review (STAT-RR5-1..5) are
  fixed at commit 14b68dedb28bbc62f946d4403f558dc697897bb9, each with a regression test reproducing the reviewer's probe and a
  negative control pinning the corruption case. The general rule this campaign adopts: a repair
  that narrows or relocates a guard ships WITH an adverse test of its perimeter, in the same
  commit -- four of the five gaps were repairs verified inline and never pinned, which is exactly
  the surface the review walked.
- **Alternatives considered:** treat the findings as review noise since the positive paths passed
  (rejected: every probe reproduced from the installed wheel -- a conjugate model that cannot
  cross a process boundary, a keyed accumulator accepting hundredfold mass corruption, a length
  model scoring garbage geometry, a finite ELBO over mismatched simplices, and an inherited
  support claim are all user-reachable); blanket-restore the original strict guards (rejected:
  the original guards rejected legitimate keyed and null-length states, which is what the first
  repairs existed to fix -- the corrected checks enforce the strongest relation each site
  actually preserves, measured per keying mode rather than assumed).
- **Evidence:** mixle/tests/repair_review_rr5_test.py (12 tests, 14 subtests) reproduces every
  probe; 1077 tests and 525 subtests across the touched subsystems pass; measured keyed-fit
  residuals (combine identity 0.0; estimate state/(init+trans) ratios 0.601/0.399 summing to one)
  are recorded in the code comments beside the checks they justify.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fixes directed in response to the external repair-pass review, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-07; commit 14b68dedb28bbc62f946d4403f558dc697897bb9
- **Expiration:** none -- the adverse-test-with-guard-change rule is standing practice.
- **Worklist:** external statistical review (repair pass, STAT-RR5-1..5).

## D-0148 · Numerical allowances carry ceilings; metadata claims need both children

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The three findings of the sixth review pass (STAT-RR6-1..3) are fixed at commit
  fe09345d526d967fc64d791c0159bd4b95e6d4dd. Two rules generalize. A scale-dependent numerical allowance must state the scale at which
  its own model stops being credible and saturate there -- the sqrt-mass tolerance now caps at
  float32's accumulation limit (2**24, ~1.95e-3 relative), because an allowance that grows without
  bound eventually accepts the corruption it exists to catch. And a composite's metadata claim
  (support, differentiability, any future flag) requires EVERY child to declare and agree; a
  declared child never vouches for an undeclared one.
- **Alternatives considered:** unbounded tolerance with a warning (rejected: a warning is not a
  refusal, and 50 percent corruption passed silently); a flat tolerance (rejected: D-0141's
  measured float32 residual at mass 8000 is real and scale-dependent below the cap); deriving
  composite flags from declared children only (rejected: that is exactly the vouching RR6-2
  demonstrated).
- **Evidence:** the reviewer's acceptance criterion ships as the regression suite -- a scale sweep
  at 1e3/1e6/1e9/1e12 masses across combine/from_value/estimate, keyed and unkeyed, with the exact
  1.5e12-vs-1.0e12 probe; 364 tests and 209 subtests across the touched subsystems pass. The
  example manifest contradiction is removed and its execution claim scoped to what execution
  proves.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fixes directed in response to the sixth external review pass, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-07; commit fe09345d526d967fc64d791c0159bd4b95e6d4dd
- **Expiration:** none -- both rules are standing practice alongside D-0147's adverse-test rule.
- **Worklist:** external statistical review (pass six, STAT-RR6-1..3).

## D-0149 · Aggregates are validated as aggregates; failed mutations roll back; receipts bind to executing bytes

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The five findings of the seventh review pass (STAT-RR7-1, SYS-RR7-2..4, DOC-RR7-5)
  are fixed at commit 656c2197128c34e404fabd1e1237a1b0309adc61. Three rules generalize and join D-0147/D-0148 as standing practice:
  a validator that sums its inputs must check the SUM's finiteness, not only the elements'; a
  rejected restoration must leave its object exactly as it was -- validate before mutating, and
  make multi-part restores transactional; and an identity receipt must bind its subject to the
  bytes that actually executed -- name and version equality is not identity.
- **Alternatives considered:** shipping the reproduction bundle inside the wheel so the installed
  command replays all four entries (rejected for 0.8.0: it packages examples and pinned inputs
  into the artifact late in the cycle; the honest alternative -- the receipt declaring its scope
  and the four entries staying source-bound receipts -- matches what the systems protocol already
  prescribed); comparing wheel-to-installed identity by version plus provenance commit only
  (rejected: RECORD content comparison closes tampering the provenance file alone cannot).
- **Evidence:** the reviewer's probes ship as regressions -- finite-element/infinite-aggregate
  statistics rejected at every site and keying mode, count preservation after a mass-rejected
  from_value, child-failure rollback, a foreign same-version wheel failing the binding and the
  receipt, exact RECORD parsing, and the scope declaration; 362 tests and 199 subtests across the
  touched subsystems pass.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fixes directed in response to the seventh external review pass, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-07; commit 656c2197128c34e404fabd1e1237a1b0309adc61
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** external statistical and systems review (pass seven).

---

## D-0150 · Invariants belong on mutators, and identity checks must not be skippable

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The four findings of the eighth review pass (STAT-RR8-1, SYS-RR8-2..3, DOC-RR8-4)
  are fixed at commit 9ff10995bc1d20c675fd62bce0a02b1ae6888ad1. Two rules generalize and join D-0147..D-0149. A property the code
  refuses to accept must be a POSTCONDITION of every public mutator, not a precondition of
  ingestion -- validating arrivals leaves every reduction, scaling and pooling path free to create
  the same invalid state, and each mutator must roll back rather than retain a result it rejects.
  And an integrity check must have no skippable rows: an entry that carries no hash is a refusal,
  never an exemption, and the identity of the executing code is established before anything found
  beside that code is trusted.
- **Alternatives considered:** validating only at ingestion and documenting the mutators as
  trusted (rejected: combine/scale/key_merge are public and the review reached the invalid state
  through all three); treating unhashed RECORD rows as legacy tolerance (rejected: that IS the
  tamper vector -- blank one row and the file leaves the comparison); comparing only provenance
  files for installation identity (rejected: a shadow copies the provenance file, which is why
  package-root identity is settled first).
- **Evidence:** the reviewer's probes ship as regressions -- finite-input/overflowing-result
  controls for combine, scale and key_merge with rollback assertions, direct non-finite
  key_replace refusal, unhashed/duplicate/malformed RECORD rejection, and a shadow-package
  fixture supplying its own modules; 430 tests and 380 subtests across the touched subsystems
  pass.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fixes directed in response to the eighth external review pass, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-07; commit 9ff10995bc1d20c675fd62bce0a02b1ae6888ad1
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** external statistical and systems review (pass eight).

---

## D-0151 · Transactionality means the whole mutator (corrects D-0150's overclaim)

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The three findings of the ninth review pass (STAT-RR9-1..3) are fixed at commit
  b9c2d4a2e298a92de24bb31f61fce23aaffff55c. D-0150 stated that every public mutator rolls back; that was an OVERCLAIM at the time it
  was written -- the rollback covered only the fragment each repair had touched, and the review
  correctly used the log's own sentence as the specification and found three violations. The
  corrected rule, now actually implemented: a mutator snapshots EVERYTHING it can touch --
  including state its children's recursion reaches that it cannot enumerate, for which the
  snapshot is the whole shared structure -- and restores all of it on any failure. Companion
  documentation rules from the same pass: a guarantee statement names its sampling assumption,
  its scope (marginal versus conditional), and its silent-failure conditions; and a comparative
  claim in an example states its estimand and carries uncertainty, with the printed conclusion
  gated on the interval rather than the point estimates.
- **Alternatives considered:** amending D-0150 in place (rejected: the log is append-only; the
  correction is this superseding entry); per-entry rollback in key_merge (rejected: child
  recursion touches keys the parent cannot enumerate, so only a whole-dict snapshot is complete);
  leaving the cascade claim qualitative (rejected: the measured interval [+1.6%, +13.0%] happens
  to SUPPORT the claim, which is exactly why gating on evidence costs nothing when the effect is
  real and prevents the assertion when it is not).
- **Evidence:** the reviewer's counterexamples ship as regressions -- combine child-failure with
  child-state assertions, key_merge later-pool failure with earlier-pool restoration, key_replace
  both-candidates-before-either -- alongside the corrected documentation and the gated example
  output; 406 tests and 444 subtests across the touched subsystems pass.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fixes directed in response to the ninth external review pass, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-07; commit b9c2d4a2e298a92de24bb31f61fce23aaffff55c
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** external statistical review (pass nine).

## D-0152 · Rollback heals through aliases, and a twin listed in the duplicate manifest is part of the repair

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The three findings of the tenth review pass (STAT-RR10-1..3) are fixed at commits
  b95040f792f3fee135068d34dd6bce774694bbcb (mutators) and ca43f130cdc390f850bb05d9675a10aacde52af6
  (claim sites and the cascade estimand). D-0151's rollback rule survives with one refinement the
  review forced: restoring a MAPPING's values or an accumulator's attributes is not restoration --
  a rollback must heal, in place and identity-preserving, every object the mutator mutated that
  external code can still reach (a caller's alias to a pooled array, the shared replacement
  children a tied site adopted, a child scaled before a later child raised). Separately, CI's
  duplicate-body gate caught what five review passes did not: every mutator repair since pass five
  had landed on the chain HMM while the tree HMM's byte-identical twins kept every defect,
  including a keyed round-trip rejection (the tree never learned STAT-RR5-2's lesson). Standing
  rules from this pass: (1) a repair to any body listed in the duplicate-body manifest lands on
  every listed twin in the same commit, preferably by deduplicating so the entry dissolves and the
  hazard cannot recur; (2) claim-scope corrections apply per CLAIM SITE, not per reference page --
  the guarantee's assumptions, marginal-versus-conditional scope, and silent-failure conditions
  belong wherever the claim is made; (3) a comparative interval's independence premise is part of
  the estimand: when a pipeline trains on its own earlier traffic, round rates are coupled, and
  the valid designs are a paired comparison of the realized systems on fresh data or resampling
  the whole adaptive procedure.
- **Alternatives considered:** re-duplicating the repaired bodies into the tree verbatim to keep
  the manifest entries true (rejected: two live copies re-arm the sibling-bug factory this pass
  demonstrated; the manifest's own guidance prefers de-duplication); pruning the manifest without
  repairing the tree (rejected: records the divergence while shipping the broken twin);
  copy-isolating the children key_replace adopts (rejected: tied sites share emission accumulators
  by design -- healing the shared objects on failure preserves those semantics); retrofitting
  every remaining latent-family accumulator's mutators now (deferred, explicitly: lookback,
  scheduled, segmental and kin have structurally divergent implementations with their own
  validation stories and no manifest listing or reviewer probe implicating them; sweeping them
  mid-cycle without measurements is the guard-overreach pattern this release documented).
- **Evidence:** seven defects measured on the tree twin before the fix (infinite replacement
  accepted, silent infinite aggregate, silent infinite scale, four partial-mutation sites, keyed
  round-trip rejected at measured unkeyed residual 5e-16) and the reviewer's three alias
  counterexamples reproduced, all fixed on both family members; the probes ship as
  WholeMutatorAliasTransactionTest and TreeSiblingGapTest with identity assertions and
  legitimate-operation controls; the duplicate manifest drops 13 groups to 11; the paired cascade
  measured +2.7% [+0.1%, +5.3%] on identical fresh traffic where the invalid unpaired interval
  would have straddled zero; 41 tests + 83 subtests on the repair file and 370 tests + 374
  subtests across the touched subsystems pass locally.
- **Owner:** release owner.

## D-0153 · The mutator contract is family-wide, and tying modes nobody could ever have used are evidence, not features

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Decision:** The transactional-mutator contract of D-0150..D-0152 now holds for every latent
  accumulator, not only the chain/tree HMM twins, at commit
  2f30dabf1668c0667057a2d7d6c37d07daf0921a. A self-initiated audit probed eleven more
  accumulators (mixture, semi-supervised mixture, joint mixture, hierarchical mixture,
  lookback/segmental/semi-supervised/scheduled HMMs, structured HMM, IOHMM, explicit-duration
  HMM) with the review passes' measured counterexample patterns before editing anything. Every
  family carried the known defect classes (partial mutation on child failure, silent infinite
  aggregates, silent or half-applied scale overflow, unvalidated keyed replacement, partial
  keyed pools) -- and the audit's most consequential finding is the overreach class: the
  segmental and lookback HMMs rejected a keyed accumulator's own round-trip and every keyed
  fit's M-step, and all four mixture-kin estimators compared pooled weight mass against the
  local observation count, so every weight-keyed mixture-family fit failed end-to-end. Those
  tying modes were shipped, documented, and unusable; no user can ever have exercised them,
  which is exactly the "guards rejecting states the library legitimately produces" pattern this
  release has documented as its dominant defect class. The repairs centralize the generic
  contracts in effective_sample (finite-total postconditions, snapshot/restore of accumulator
  statistics, in-place pool healing), alias the chain-HMM privates to them so the
  implementations cannot drift, delegate the geometry-identical segmental HMM to the
  family-shared cores outright, and give every keyed validator/estimator the mode-appropriate
  mass relation and pooled-mass carve-outs the chain HMM has carried since STAT-RR5-2.
- **Alternatives considered:** leaving the unprobed families to a future external review pass
  (rejected: pass ten proved siblings accumulate every missed repair, and the manifest ratchet
  only guards byte-identical copies -- structurally divergent siblings need measurement);
  eleven private per-family transaction implementations (rejected: that is the drift mechanism
  the shared home exists to end); tightening the keyed carve-outs into new cross-site mass
  relations for pooled statistics (rejected: unmeasured invariants are the overreach pattern;
  the carve-outs mirror exactly what the chain HMM measured in STAT-RR5-2).
- **Evidence:** per-family probe matrices measured before and after the fix (all defect cells
  clean after; keyed round-trips, keyed M-steps, and end-to-end weight-keyed optimize() fits
  accepted for every keyed family; unkeyed controls unchanged); the probes ship as
  latent_family_mutator_transaction_test with exact value-tuple fingerprint rollback
  assertions, alias-observed pool healing, and legitimate-operation controls; 340 tests + 442
  subtests across the touched subsystems pass locally, including the duplicate-body gate (no
  new groups) and the full keyed-protocol sweep.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (self-initiated audit extending the tenth review pass's sibling lesson, implementation AI-verified); 2026-08-07; commit 2f30dabf1668c0667057a2d7d6c37d07daf0921a
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** latent-family mutator audit (follow-up to external review pass ten).
