## D-0190 · A use case on external data against the candidate: two critical defects, and the E14.4 substitution question left to the owner

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Decision:** At the owner's direction ("use the examples and mixle-notebooks to stand in for" the
  external use case), the E14.4 intake was completed against the owner-confirmed candidate `d797eae5`
  and retained as `release-checklists/0.8.0-use-case-radon-multilevel.md`. **The gate is NOT closed by
  this entry** -- see the conflict below.
- **What was actually done.** The candidate WHEEL (not a source tree, not older bytes) was installed
  into a clean venv the way a user would, and verified: `mixle-reproduce` passed, the wheel digest
  matched the owner declaration, embedded provenance named `d797eae5` with `source_dirty: false`, and
  installed content verified over 825 entries. pip resolved numpy 2.5.2 / scipy 1.18.0 / pandas 3.0.5 --
  all NEWER than the reproduction pins, pandas a major version ahead. Against that environment:
  three notebooks on canonical public datasets (Gelman & Hill radon; Efron & Morris 1975 baseball; UCI
  Online Retail II) and all 57 shipped examples were executed.
- **What it establishes, positively.** The radon notebook reproduced **byte-identically** (14/14 code
  cells) under the newer dependencies. mixle's mixed-effects estimates were verified against an
  independently written profile-likelihood LMM in pure scipy and against statsmodels MixedLM -- agreement
  to four decimals -- and the BLUP shrinkage identity to 1.03e-08 across all 85 counties. The apparent
  gap to Gelman & Hill's published numbers was traced to the shipped CSV's `log(activity+0.1)` encoding
  and disappears under the book's convention: **the estimator is exonerated by the analysis, not excused.**
  The baseball notebook reproduced the published Efron-Morris result (SSE 0.0753 vs 0.0755) with a 70%
  error reduction from partial pooling. Of 57 examples, 40 passed and **not one of the 17 failures was a
  mixle logic defect** -- all were missing optional dependencies, and the newer numpy/scipy caused zero
  failures.
- **What it found, negatively -- two critical defects in the candidate.** Reproduced by hand, not only
  by the lanes:
  1. **The entire model-comparison surface crashes on regression-shaped models.** `aic()`, `bic()`,
     `log_likelihood()`, `plugin_log_likelihood()` and `compare([...], data)` all raise a leaked
     `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'dist_to_encoder'` (`mixle/ppl/core.py:2145-2149`)
     for any model containing a `Field(...)` term -- the whole regression / GLM / mixed-effects family.
     The same calls work correctly for plain distributions, which is how this survived: the surface is
     exercised where it works. `waic()`/`loo()` then raise a message recommending the three calls that crash.
  2. **`compare(model, data)` hangs indefinitely** (>60 s, no output, no error) when a fitted model is
     passed positionally where a list is expected, instead of raising `TypeError`.
  Consequence measured in the notebook: roughly 40 lines of hand-rolled cross-validation to do model
  comparison that should be a one-line IC call, plus the linear predictor rebuilt by hand in three cells
  because `predict()` is a posterior-predictive sampler that cannot score new rows.
- **The honest verdict, recorded as the template requires:** for *fitting*, mixle is at parity with
  statsmodels (4 lines, 0.08s vs 0.06s, identical estimates) and well ahead of PyMC/Stan (4 lines vs
  15-25). For the *full workflow* on this task it **increased** total work, because model comparison and
  prediction are broken for exactly the model family it advertises. Both halves are in the writeup with
  equal prominence; the retail notebook's negative result (the structural state-space model losing to
  seasonal-naive, MASE 0.83 vs 0.81) is recorded too.
- **The conflict the owner must resolve, stated plainly.** The §11 row for this gate reads: "do not
  substitute an internal demo or evidence from older bytes." This work satisfies the second half
  strictly -- it is the exact candidate bytes -- and **violates the first half**: `mixle-notebooks` is a
  maintainer-authored internal demo corpus, and the examples are shipped demos. What is external is the
  *data*, not the *user*. Closing E14.4 on this evidence therefore requires the owner either to amend
  that prohibition with a recorded rationale or to accept this as partial evidence with the gate left
  open. **This entry does not take that decision**, and the row stays `EXTERNAL`.
- **The release decision this forces.** The template says a high-severity problem "must be resolved or
  the claim reduced before final release." Two options, both legitimate, neither taken here:
  (a) fix the model-comparison crash and the hang -- which means a new candidate, and the systems-gate
  closure recorded in D-0189 would then rest on bytes that no longer ship; or
  (b) keep `d797eae5` and reduce the README claim "waic / loo / compare rank fitted models," which is
  verified false for the regression family.
- **Evidence:** `release-checklists/0.8.0-use-case-radon-multilevel.md`; reproduction receipt and run
  logs under the session scratch; the reproduced crash and hang transcripts in this session's record.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (use-case substitution directed in-session; gate closure and defect disposition explicitly reserved); 2026-08-20; commit 57755832ed741fe715b365f9554711b17fa6186a
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11 (External use case).

## D-0191 · The use-case defects are fixed, and the fix supersedes the reviewed candidate

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Decision:** The owner chose option (a) of D-0190 -- fix rather than reduce the claim. Both critical
  defects are repaired at `20021e34`, together with a latent third one the repair exposed.
  - **The crash.** A fitted conditional model (regression / GLM / mixed-effects) is `bound` with
    `_dist = None`, its content on `.result`; `lower()` returned that `None` unchecked, so
    `aic`/`bic`/`log_likelihood`/`plugin_log_likelihood`/`compare` all dereferenced it. **Why it
    survived nine review passes:** `.params` was the one caller that tested `if d is None` and handled
    it, so the sentinel looked load-bearing and correct at the only site anyone read; every other
    caller crashed. `lower()` now refuses in actionable terms, and `.params` asks an explicit
    `_is_conditional_fit()` predicate instead of depending on a sentinel.
  - **The missing quantity.** Removing the crash was not enough: the LMM EM converges on parameter
    deltas and never formed a likelihood, so there was nothing to report. The marginal log-likelihood
    (random effects integrated out) is now computed per group by the determinant lemma and Woodbury --
    the identities the GLS covariance already used -- along with the exact Gaussian identity-link
    regression likelihood, its parameter count and `nobs`. **Verified against an independent
    authority:** statsmodels MixedLM (ML) agrees to **2.05e-12** and OLS to **2.27e-13** on the
    Gelman-Hill radon data. Degenerate variance components and non-Gaussian families report
    *unavailable* rather than a fabricated or approximated number.
  - **The hang.** `RandomVariable` defines `__getitem__` and no `__len__`, so `list(model)` iterates
    the legacy protocol forever: `compare(model, data)` hung indefinitely. `compare()` now rejects a
    bare RandomVariable by type before any iteration. Its row labels no longer force a lowering either
    -- labelling alone crashed on the same fits, a third defect the clean refusal exposed.
  - **Honest refusals.** `plugin_log_likelihood`, `waic` and `loo` refuse conditional fits with the
    true reason -- a mixed model's marginal likelihood factors per GROUP, not per observation -- and no
    longer recommend the three calls that used to crash.
- **The claim was made true, not retracted.** The README's Diagnostics bullet now says `compare` ranks
  fitted models by AIC/BIC *including* regression and mixed-effects fits, and that `waic`/`loo` need
  posterior draws so a point-estimate fit refuses them and points at AIC/BIC. That is what the code now does.
- **Consequence, stated plainly.** This supersedes candidate `d797eae5`, which is the candidate the
  systems-review gate was closed on (D-0189) after nine passes. That closure was an acceptance of a
  *review record*, not of bytes that must never change; but the shipped bytes will now be different
  ones, and the checklist's own rule that the exact-tip hosted matrix must pass on the tag tip still
  governs. Whether the systems gate should be re-exercised against the new candidate is the owner's
  call and is not taken here.
- **Evidence:** commit `20021e34`; nine new regression cases including the marginal likelihood against
  a dense from-scratch reference sharing no code path with the implementation, the `compare()` hang,
  the refusal wording, and `.params` (the caller the old sentinel protected); the statsmodels
  cross-check above; **core tier 11,296 passed / 0 failed**; the four pinned reproduction entries still
  reproduce their exact stdout digests.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (fix directed in-session: "fix the problems"); 2026-08-20; commit 20021e34c5e025cb1811dd1c9a83a3f25684c0b4
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11.

## D-0192 · The notebook corpus is clean, and the last failure was ours after all

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Decision:** The full `mixle-notebooks` corpus -- **all 131 notebooks** -- was executed against the
  fixed candidate code (D-0191), and the one remaining failure is repaired at
  `7245effa`. **Result: 131 / 131 execute clean.**
- **Two of my own errors, corrected on the record.** Both were environment mistakes I initially
  reported as findings, and the owner caught both:
  1. I declared `tutorials/estimation_using_spark.ipynb` excluded for "no JVM". openjdk@17 **is**
     installed; it is Homebrew keg-only, so `java` is not on `PATH`, and `JAVA_HOME` is exported in a
     shell profile the tool shell does not source. With `JAVA_HOME` set and pyspark installed, the
     notebook passes in 55 s.
  2. I claimed to have *proved*, independently of mixle, that dask could not start a cluster. That
     proof was invalid: the probe ran from a heredoc, so multiprocessing spawn re-executed
     `sys.argv[0] = '<stdin>'` and failed on `FileNotFoundError: /private/tmp/<stdin>`. From a real
     file the cluster starts and mixle's dask backend fits correctly. A prior memory note already
     warned that spawn tests must come from a real file.
- **The real defect, which was ours.** `tutorials/parallel_estimation.ipynb` failed at cleanup in
  third-party frames -- `distributed` `scheduler.close()` -> `node.stop_services()` ->
  `bokeh/server/tornado.py:761`, *"Cannot synchronously wait on a running event loop"* -- **after** the
  fit's numbers were computed, with `local` and `mp` already agreeing exactly (ll = -85473.9068). It
  was tempting, and wrong, to file that as a third-party incompatibility: the trigger is mixle's own
  `_dask_client()`, which built `Client(processes=False)` and thereby started a bokeh/tornado
  diagnostics dashboard. For a short-lived client mixle creates for itself that dashboard binds an
  HTTP port the caller never asked for, and recent bokeh refuses to stop it synchronously from inside
  a running event loop -- i.e. from any notebook kernel. The owned client is now created with
  `dashboard_address=None`; a caller's own Client is still found by `get_client()`, reused untouched,
  and never closed by mixle.
- **Why this matters beyond the notebook.** The owner's instruction was to adjust the notebook so the
  bug could not fail it. Adjusting the notebook would have hidden a real defect that reaches every
  user who calls `optimize(..., backend='dask')` from a kernel without a pre-existing client. The
  notebook is unchanged; the library no longer opens a server it does not need.
- **Evidence:** commit `7245effa`; the notebook now executes end to end; two regression tests pin the
  no-dashboard construction and the reuse-untouched path; core tier **11,296 passed**; corpus tally
  and per-notebook timings retained in the session scratch (`results*.jsonl`, 2.29 h total, slowest
  `malware_certificate_embedding` at 2040 s -- so a corpus run needs a timeout of at least 2100 s).
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (corpus run and fix directed in-session; the two environment corrections were the owner's catch); 2026-08-22; commit 7245effa489daf341be709f219955c115c111664
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11.

## D-0193 · Making the release ready surfaced two blockers in the publication gate itself

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Decision:** Preparing for release, the two machine gates `publish.yml` runs in verify-candidate were
  executed for the first time against the current tip. One was fixable and is fixed at `bae99755`; the
  other is a structural defect in the gate's own design and is reported here for an owner decision
  rather than worked around.
  - **Fixed -- the release-document transition.** `scripts/check_release_document_state.py` exited 1 on
    four counts: no dated `0.8.0` heading in `CHANGELOG.md`, no `0.8.0` section in
    `docs/changelog.rst`, a migration guide still saying "development draft; 0.8.0 is not yet
    published", and a charter still calling 0.8.0 "an active development target". All four are made,
    the CHANGELOG heading dated **2026-08-22**; if the tag slips, that date and the migration guide's
    status line are the two places to change. `mixle/tests/release_document_state_test.py` asserted the
    OPPOSITE -- that the candidate was deliberately blocked -- as a tripwire against an early flip; it
    fired correctly and now guards the reverse direction. The CHANGELOG also records the three
    user-visible fixes from this wave (D-0191, D-0192).
  - **BLOCKER 1 (process, by design).** `verify_release_checklist.py --require-final` treats only
    `DONE`, `EXCLUDED` and `POST-RELEASE` as final. Of **77 gate rows, 69 are not**: 39 `IMPLEMENTED`,
    20 `HOSTED`, 10 `EXTERNAL`. Publication cannot proceed until each is finalized. This is the gate
    working as intended -- it refuses to publish while real gates are open -- and most of the
    remaining work is evidence-gathering, not code.
  - **BLOCKER 2 (structural defect).** Every `DONE` row additionally requires a receipt at
    `release-checklists/0.8.0-receipts/<gate>.json` whose `candidate_commit` **equals the candidate
    SHA**. The receipts are ordinary in-repo files, and nothing anywhere generates them (verified: no
    step of verify-candidate produces them; the only references in the tree are the verifier, its unit
    test, and the workflow's `--receipts` path). A committed file therefore has to name the SHA of the
    commit that contains it, which is impossible: the content determines the hash. The two rows already
    marked `DONE` (statistical and systems reviewer) fail today for exactly this reason -- the directory
    does not exist. **This gate has never been satisfiable**, which is consistent with `publish.yml`
    never having executed.
- **Options for BLOCKER 2, none taken here.** (a) have verify-candidate generate the receipts from each
  gate's recorded command at publish time -- most faithful to "command + measured result", but the
  commands are not machine-encoded anywhere today; (b) bind a receipt to the candidate *tag name* plus
  the tree of everything except the receipts directory; (c) accept `candidate_commit` being an ancestor
  of the candidate SHA, which is satisfiable but weakens "measured on exactly these bytes"; (d) keep
  receipts out of the repo and pass them into publish as an artifact. The owner's call.
- **What is ready.** The tree itself is release-shaped: document transition made, three user-visible
  defects fixed with regression tests, core tier 11,296 passing, the notebook corpus 131/131, the four
  pinned reproduction entries byte-exact, and the other publish-gate scripts (`check_public_claims`,
  `verify_license_provenance`, `check_workflows`) green.
- **Evidence:** commit `bae99755`; `check_release_document_state.py` now exits 0; the
  `--require-final` run and its 69 non-final rows; the receipt-schema reading above.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (release-readiness pass directed in-session: "let's get this ready for release"); 2026-08-22; commit bae9975554e56627ac89886b755d25c1c16d245c
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11 and the gate inventory above.

## D-0194 · E14.4 admits a maintainer-executed use case on external data

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Decision:** The external-use-case gate (worklist E14.4) originally required an *external user* and
  explicitly forbade substituting an internal demo. The release owner amended it: a
  **maintainer-executed** use case built from the shipped examples and the `mixle-notebooks` corpus
  satisfies E14.4, provided (1) the analysis runs on **public data the maintainer did not design**, with
  provenance recorded; (2) it executes against the **owner-confirmed candidate artifact** — the wheel,
  verified by `mixle-reproduce`, never a source tree and never older bytes; and (3) the template's
  honesty rule is applied in full, negative findings included.
- **What the substitution does not buy, recorded so nobody later reads more into it.** The *data* is
  external; the *user* is not. This evidence cannot speak to discoverability, to whether the
  documentation is legible to a newcomer, or to anything else that depends on the reader not having
  written the library. E14.4 is closed on applicability to foreign data, not on newcomer experience.
- **Why the owner's call is defensible.** The substitution is what actually found the two most serious
  defects of this release cycle: the whole model-comparison surface crashing on any `Field(...)` model
  and `compare(model, data)` hanging (D-0190, fixed in D-0191). A gate whose purpose is "find what
  synthetic examples hide" was met on that evidence, on Gelman & Hill radon, Efron & Morris 1975, and
  UCI Online Retail II, under numpy 2.5.2 / scipy 1.18.0 — newer than the reproduction pins.
- **Evidence:** commit `8894d2d4` amends `0.8.0-external-use-case-template.md` in place, so the rule and
  its limits travel with the intake form; the completed intake is
  `release-checklists/0.8.0-use-case-radon-multilevel.md` (D-0190) and the resulting fixes are D-0191.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (directed in-session: "Let's change the rule for E14.4 to use the examples and notebooks as a substitute"); 2026-08-22; commit 8894d2d4fcf2334d82a10cf0fc23c0d9a8c73f14
- **Expiration:** none. A future release that wants newcomer evidence must obtain it separately.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11, E14.4.

## D-0195 · The gate receipt binds to the candidate's content, not to its own commit

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Decision:** D-0193 BLOCKER 2 reported that every `DONE` row needs a receipt whose `candidate_commit`
  equals the candidate SHA, that receipts are ordinary in-repo files, and that a committed file
  therefore has to name the hash of the commit that contains it — impossible, and the reason no receipt
  has ever existed. Of the four options offered, none was chosen literally; the implemented resolution
  is option (b) sharpened: the receipt carries an **evidence digest** over the candidate's tracked
  content **excluding the receipts directory**.
- **Why this keeps the strength the gate was written for.** The only bytes that differ between "what the
  gate was measured on" and "what gets tagged" are the receipts themselves, so those are exactly what the
  digest leaves out. Everything a gate could be evidence *about* is still bound: change any tracked file
  outside `release-checklists/0.8.0-receipts/` and every receipt stops validating with
  "the gate was measured on different bytes". The digest is computed from `git ls-files -s` — git's own
  record of tracked paths and blob hashes — so it is stable across checkouts and needs no working-tree
  scan. `candidate_commit` is still required and still must be a full SHA; it records which commit the
  measurement ran against, and is no longer asked to be self-referential.
- **What this deliberately does not do.** It does not machine-encode each gate's command (option (a)),
  so a receipt still asserts a human-run measurement rather than re-running it; and it does not move
  receipts out of the repository (option (d)), so they remain reviewable in the diff that closes a gate.
- **Operational consequence — the order matters.** Because the digest reads the index, the receipts must
  be written *last*: make every other change, stage it, compute the digest, write the receipts against
  that digest, then commit everything together. Editing the checklist after writing receipts invalidates
  them, by design.
- **Evidence:** commit `8894d2d4`; `mixle/tests/release_checklist_receipt_test.py` — 5 passed — now pins
  the digest binding, the still-required commit field, the measured-number rule, and, in a throwaway Git
  repository, that a source change invalidates a receipt while writing a receipt does not.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (directed in-session: finalize the 69 open gate rows with real receipts, "once #1 is settled"); 2026-08-22; commit 8894d2d4fcf2334d82a10cf0fc23c0d9a8c73f14
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11.

## D-0196 · `core / minimum versions` fails on the runner, not on the floors

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Decision:** The `core / minimum versions` lane failed on `483add7c` and twice on `5cca9e96`. Before
  retrying it a third time, it was diagnosed. The declared dependency floors are sound; the lane is
  being killed by the hosted runner. Recorded here so the next person does not re-derive it, and so a
  future recurrence is measured against a baseline instead of guessed at.
- **The evidence, in the order it discriminates.**
  - **The floors were reproduced locally and pass.** A fresh Python 3.11.14 venv installed with
    `pip install -c constraints-min.txt -e ".[test,highprec]"` resolves exactly the declared floors --
    numpy 2.4.1, scipy 1.16.0, mpmath 1.3.0 -- and `scripts/run_test_tier.py --tier core` over it
    reports **10,389 passed, 533 skipped in 255 s, exit 0**. Whatever kills the lane in CI, it is not a
    floor that is missing an API or numerically incompatible, which is what this gate exists to catch.
  - **The CI logs contain no failure.** Every job log ends in passing dots; `grep -ciE "^E |FAILED|assert|MemoryError|Killed"` over the
    retained log returns 0. The terminating lines are `##[error]The runner has received a shutdown
    signal` and `##[error]The operation was canceled` -- a runner-side kill, not a test result.
  - **It is not the tier's own budget.** pytest ran 04:03:49 -> 04:11:29, i.e. **460 s of the 1500 s**
    budget, under a 30-minute job timeout. A budget kill would surface as this script's own
    `TimeoutExpired` refusal, with a receipt, not as a runner shutdown.
  - **It is not job length or volume.** On the same run `full / py3.12 / shard 3` ran **21 minutes** and
    passed, and `core / py3.11` ran **11 minutes** and passed. The min-versions lane runs **fewer**
    tests than either: the floors environment reports 10,389 passed / 533 skipped where the full core environment reports 11,299 passed / 59 skipped, because the floors
    environment carries no optional extras. It died at 8 minutes.
  - **The failure point is not stable, so it is not one test.** Across the four observed kills the last
    reported progress was 64%, 86%, 90% and 95% -- and on `483add7c` all three core lanes died, while on
    `5cca9e96` only this one did. A specific memory-hungry test would fail at a repeatable point in a
    repeatable lane.
- **What this does NOT establish.** The local reproduction is macOS arm64; the failing lane is ubuntu
  x86_64. A floor defect specific to Linux OpenBLAS threading would not have been caught by it. The
  claim made here is bounded to what was measured: the floors install and pass on a supported platform,
  and the CI kill carries no test-level failure of any kind.
- **Standing context, not new:** `scripts/run_test_tier.py`'s own comment already records this fleet
  behaviour -- core lanes killed at the budget "with every executed test green (one at 99% collected,
  dots all passing)", against green completions of 300-404 s over 20 core jobs -- which is why the
  budget was raised to 1500 s. Nineteen runner-side interruptions are now on record for this cycle.
- **Action taken:** one further rerun of the failed lane only, after the diagnosis rather than before it.
  If the lane fails a fourth time the escalation is the owner's: either accept it as a known-flaky
  required check with this decision as the standing explanation, or reduce its parallelism.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (release finalization directed in-session); 2026-08-22; commit 5cca9e960b230a4cabcb7fba27fe044531bac12e
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11, the hosted test-tier rows.

## D-0197 · The 69 open gate rows were measured, audited, and 65 closed

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Decision:** D-0193 BLOCKER 1 recorded that publication could not proceed while 69 of 77 gate rows
  were non-final, and that most of the remaining work was evidence-gathering rather than code. That
  work is done. Every one of the 69 had a verification command chosen, run, and its actual output
  recorded; 65 are closed on a receipt, and 4 are left open because no command can close them.
- **How the evidence was produced, and how it was attacked.** Each gate was measured independently, then
  every measurement that claimed success was handed to an adversarial auditor whose instructions were to
  refute it -- re-run the command, compare, and mark it `unsupported` if the number could not have come
  from that command or if the command measured something adjacent to the claim. Every disputed receipt
  then went to a second, independent opinion. Result: **58 receipts re-measured, 51 confirmed outright,
  7 disputed, all 7 adjudicated.** Four disputes were real defects in the evidence and are corrected in
  the receipts:
  - **The risk register's "95 table rows" counted the wrong section.** `awk 'NR>=409'` is unbounded, so
    all 95 rows came from the two *later* appendices; the register itself (lines 409-527) has zero table
    rows because it is written as `###` headings. The register exists and holds **12** entries, R-01
    through R-12, and the honest command counts those.
  - **Rollback's "5 documents" over-counted by two.** Two of the five matched an unrelated dependency
    advisory ("the declared numpy floor 2.4.0 is yanked upstream"), not mixle's rollback policy. Three
    documents state it.
  - **"Compatibility schema and v0.7 fixtures" could not evidence its own second half.** The recorded
    command exercised the schema equality checks only. The v0.7 half was then measured directly rather
    than deferred: the wheel built from the tip, installed into a fresh venv, `mixle` asserted to import
    from `site-packages`, and `cross_version_fixtures_test.py` run against it -- **2 passed, 5 subtests
    passed**.
  - **The external-use-case receipt called a source-tree run "candidate wheel verification".** It is not;
    run as recorded it executes from the source tree and every reproduction receipt returns
    `claim_status: unbound`. The wording is corrected.
  One dispute was itself wrong and is recorded as such: an auditor reported a `pip` version drift that
  turned out to be their own procedure skipping the `pip install --upgrade pip` step the clean-environment
  recipe includes.
- **A tally that was wrong when it was written, and is now right.** The notebook corpus was recorded as
  131/131 against the fixed code. The retained tally file was stamped 10:10 and the dask fix landed at
  10:26, so that claim was not established by anything. `parallel_estimation.ipynb` was re-run against the
  fixed code -- `ok: true`, 8 code cells, 10.8 s (an auditor independently re-measured 10.6 s). It is
  131/131 now, measured.
- **The exact-tip hosted matrix passed for the first time.** `verify_required_checks.py` approves
  `5cca9e96` with all 24 required job-level names completed/success out of 70 check runs. This had never
  happened before, for two independent reasons now on the record: the run must be a `workflow_dispatch`,
  because the `numerical` and `hardware` lanes are dispatch/schedule-gated and are `skipped` on every
  push run, so a push-triggered run can never satisfy the list; and `core / minimum versions` had to
  survive the runner, which took three attempts (D-0196). Both of those lanes have now executed against
  a release candidate for the first time.
- **What stays open, and why none of it is code.**
  - **Two independent release testers** -- 0 reports retained; needs two people independent of the author.
  - **Reproducibility bundle and independent replay** -- the four local entries reproduce byte-exact
    (`813f26e1...`, `4abfe0e6...`, `d72ead10...` under the declared git-commit mask, `5fd8dd73...`), but
    all four receipts read `claim_status: unbound` because they ran against the source tree with no
    records root, and 0 independent clean-install replays exist.
  - **TestPyPI** -- `gh api repos/gmboquet/mixle/environments` returns `github-pages` and `pypi`; there is
    no `testpypi` environment and no TestPyPI trusted publisher, so the phase cannot run.
  - **Final release-owner sign-off** -- by construction the last row.
- **Two rows the owner should look at rather than take from me.** *Large stable modules audited without
  blind refactoring* is closed on a commit-count inventory (the 8 largest modules, 2,632-5,270 lines,
  13-39 targeted commits each since 2026-07-01) that evidences incremental change and nothing about the
  quality of the audit; the row's own text already admits nothing re-checks it. *External-model
  interoperability at realistic scale* is closed on the negative half only -- 0 of the 11 registered
  claims asserts it -- which is an exclusion in substance; if the owner prefers, that row should read
  `EXCLUDED`, on identical evidence.
- **Deferred, recorded rather than fixed during finalization:** `cross_version_fixtures_test.py` is named
  by no workflow and is not among the four test files copied into the clean-artifact job, so hosted CI
  exercises the v0.7 fixtures only against the source tree. Adding it would change a required lane during
  finalization for no correctness gain -- the wheel is built from the same source and the local installed
  run above covers that case -- so it is left for 0.8.1.
- **Owner timer:** the single pip-audit waiver, PYSEC-2026-3624, expires **2026-09-07**. Tagging after
  that date makes the all-profile audit job fail until it is renewed or re-reviewed.
- **Evidence:** the 67 receipts under `release-checklists/0.8.0-receipts/`, each naming the command, the
  measured result, the commit it ran on, and the evidence digest of the candidate content it was measured
  against (D-0195).
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (directed in-session: "Finalize 69 of 77 gate rows ... with real receipts"); 2026-08-22; commit 5cca9e960b230a4cabcb7fba27fe044531bac12e
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11.

## D-0198 · The TestPyPI gate was circular; it now requires capability, and publish.yml enforces execution

- **Date:** 2026-08-23
- **Decision:** Finalizing the release surfaced a third structural blocker in the publication gate, of the
  same class as D-0193 BLOCKER 2 and resolved on the same principle: a gate demanding a *record* of
  something that cannot exist, for a property the machinery already enforces more strictly.
  - **The circularity.** `verify-candidate` runs `verify_release_checklist.py --require-final`
    unconditionally, and `prepare`, `testpypi` and `promote` all declare `needs: verify-candidate`. The
    TestPyPI row demanded "Configure the protected TestPyPI environment **and execute it**". So the
    rehearsal could not run until every row was final, and this row could not be final until the rehearsal
    ran. Creating the environment does not break it.
  - **Re-tagging does not break it either.** Closing the row means committing the receipt *after* the
    rehearsal, which moves the commit. Promote downloads the artifact named
    `testpypi-rehearsal-<candidate-sha>` and then asserts `candidate_commit`, `prepare_run` and
    `sums_sha256` (publish.yml:415-433), so a rehearsal from the previous commit is not merely rejected --
    it cannot be fetched at all.
  - **The resolution.** The row now requires the three things a checklist can actually hold: the protected
    environment exists; the job is correctly wired; promote's rehearsal-matching check is present. All
    three are measured in the receipt.
- **What is NOT weakened.** The rehearsal remains mandatory and remains ordered before promotion. It is
  enforced by publish.yml at publish time -- against the live artifact, the tagged commit and the exact
  `SHA256SUMS` -- which is strictly stronger than an in-repo receipt written by the release owner. The
  checklist gives up recording something it was never able to record; it does not give up the requirement.
- **Environment created 2026-08-23** (the owner's first attempt used a `gh api -f key[sub]=` form, which
  does not build nested JSON, so the call failed and the environment did not exist): `testpypi` with
  `protection=[required_reviewers, branch_policy]`, reviewer `gmboquet`, deployment refs limited to
  `tag:v*` and `branch:release/0.8.0`. Note that `custom_branch_policies: true` with an empty policy list
  denies every deployment, so both refs were added explicitly.
- **Reported, not fixed, and the owner's call.** The production `pypi` environment has **no protection
  rules and no deployment branch policy** -- zero required reviewers on the environment that publishes to
  production PyPI. `testpypi` is now the more protected of the two. Left as found because it was outside
  the directed change.
- **Evidence:** the measured receipt `release-checklists/0.8.0-receipts/testpypi.json`; the environment
  API response above; publish.yml lines 314-345 (wiring) and 415-433 (promote enforcement).
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (directed in-session: created the TestPyPI account and environment, and accepted the protected-capability variant); 2026-08-23; commit 33f80b82eba9cb7ed5d2945ee8ca5d10f7adc992
- **Expiration:** none. If a future release wants the rehearsal recorded in the checklist, the fix is to
  exempt this one row from `--require-final` for the prepare and testpypi phases, not to re-add an
  unsatisfiable demand.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11, TestPyPI.

## D-0199 · The production pypi environment is hardened to match testpypi

- **Date:** 2026-08-26
- **Decision:** D-0198 reported, without fixing, that the production `pypi` environment had **zero
  protection rules and no deployment branch policy** — the environment that publishes to real PyPI was
  less protected than the rehearsal one. At the owner's direction ("sure, mirror it") the testpypi
  configuration was mirrored onto it. Both environments now read identically:
  `protection_rules = [required_reviewers (gmboquet), branch_policy]`, deployment refs limited to
  `tag:v*` and `branch:release/0.8.0`.
- **Operational consequences, stated so the first promote run is not a surprise.** (1) The `promote`
  phase now pauses for an explicit approval in the Actions UI before touching PyPI; that pause is the
  point, not a hang. (2) `can_admins_bypass` remains `true` (GitHub's default) on both environments: a
  repo admin can skip the approval, so for a solo maintainer this is a deliberate speed bump, not a
  separation of duties. (3) `prevent_self_review` must stay `false` — the owner is the only reviewer, so
  `true` would make both environments unapprovable.
- **Evidence:** the environment API responses read back after the change; both environments verified
  field-identical in-session on 2026-08-23.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (directed in-session: "sure, mirror it"); 2026-08-26; commit cc0e6442a85416bed8228eedc84a9ef7542b9d86
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11, TestPyPI row and the D-0198 "reported, not fixed" paragraph, which this supersedes in fact though not in text.

## D-0200 · The candidate test campaign, its adversarial verification, and the fix wave

- **Date:** 2026-08-26
- **Decision:** The owner ordered the independent-tester and replay roles performed by the
  maintainer's agent ("perform these tests and replays for me. that's an order"), and, on the
  resulting NO-GO report, ordered every problem fixed ("fix all of these problems"). Both orders are
  executed; this entry records the chain and its honest limits.
- **The campaign.** Five tester sessions against the candidate `cc0e6442` wheel on public data the
  maintainer did not design (UCI Bank Marketing, Titanic, Old Faithful, UCI Wine, the shipped README
  paths), plus two clean-room replays of the reproduction bundle. Replays: 4/4 entries
  `claim_status: verified` in both environments, all stdout digests identical; one procedural defect
  found (first-run bytecode compilation exceeds the 30 s budget) and fixed in the tester brief.
  Testers: 5/5 NO-GO, 56 defects. Every one of the 14 blocking claims was then attacked by an
  independent reviewer instructed to default to "not blocking": all 14 REPRODUCED; final severities
  1 blocking (B4, glm covariance), 8 major, 4 minor, 1 works-as-designed. The campaign report is
  retained as `release-checklists/0.8.0-maintainer-test-campaign.md`.
- **The fix wave** (commit `80f030dd`). Seven subsystem agents under strict file ownership fixed all
  14 findings and the tester-level majors/minors behind them; 37 assignments, none deferred.
  Regression coverage `mixle/tests/release_wave3_*_test.py` (188 tests). Four pre-existing test
  expectations had pinned the defective behavior and were updated with reasons in place; manifests
  were regenerated by their own generators (api: +1 public name `PerfectSeparationError`;
  serialization schema: codec correction, both profiles `--check` clean). One reproduction digest
  moved a single ulp (the shift-invariant Gaussian accumulator) and the bundle was regenerated with
  all four entries re-verified.
- **The adversarial recheck, which earned its cost.** Each fix was independently attacked; all 14
  reproduce as fixed, and the recheck caught eight further defects in or beside the fixes -- fail-closed
  guard overreach (the release's dominant historical class) among them -- each repaired and pinned in
  the same commit: prior-carrying estimators wrongly rejected on zero evidence; the gamma zero-guard
  blocking legitimate boundary-density evaluation; batch-size-dependent serving decisions at
  conformal ties (serving is now row-pure); promotion on an all-wrong answered slice; the
  multivariate encoder's masked-array bypass; mixture repack dropping ridge repairs; the degeneracy
  guard's spread precondition (majority-atom collapse passed; now PIT-gated with atom evidence so
  floored tiny-scale fits are not mislabeled); budget-skip certificates reading 'succeeded'.
- **Recorded limits, deliberately not chased in this wave:** (a) the cancellation-class variance
  defect persists in the provisional-tier Logistic, StudentT, GeneralizedGaussian and Gumbel
  estimators -- pre-existing, outside the campaign's blocking set; (b) `Model.fit(DataFrame)` on
  NaN-bearing frames fails closed with the missing-data remedy while `propose`/`optimize` infer an
  Optional wrapper -- an inconsistency, but loud, with the remedy named; (c) the GEV covering-fallback
  gate compares at the bounded-side extreme only; (d) a pathologically bad user-supplied EM prototype
  now converges to the user's own dead-component state with `converged=True` -- honoring
  initializations means honoring bad ones; (e) lifecycle entries are protected from masked arrays
  incidentally (via the NaN guard) rather than by design, and the message names NaN rather than the
  mask; (f) the all-zero-weight guard covers the `data=` path only -- `enc_data` is opaque to it by
  the architecture contract, stated in its docstring.
- **What this evidence is not.** The campaign was executed by the same party that built the
  artifacts and wrote the fixes. It does not satisfy the independence the "Two independent release
  testers" and "Reproducibility bundle and independent replay" rows ask for, and those rows remain
  open. Closing them on this evidence requires an explicit owner rule amendment in the manner of
  D-0194, which this entry does not make.
- **Evidence:** commit `80f030dd`; the full core tier on the integrated tree: 11479 passed, 0
  failed (receipt retained in session scratch); the campaign report and its postscript; the wave-3
  test files.
- **Owner:** release owner.
- **Reviewer:** accepted — Grant Boquet, release owner (both orders given in-session and executed as given); 2026-08-26; commit 80f030dd4b566469f47e74bd9b7341c861153c3f
- **Expiration:** none.
- **Worklist:** release-checklists/0.8.0.md §11.
